One intended behavior delta while off: the optimistic read flip is no longer replayed onto refetched lists, so pressing **Refresh** after hearing a voicemail restores the unread marker (ADR-024 §2 by design; resolves when both flags are on). **RotaryPhone is no longer owner-HELD**: their `MarkRead` / thread-read routes + `ReadStateChanged` broadcast are shipped and dark by config only (`GVBridge:EnableMarkRead=false`). **Wire GV mark-read / durable read-state.** Fast-follow that flips read-state from UI-local to **durable via GV write-through** (Google is single source of truth; NO local store): repoint the GV-2 `MarkVoicemailReadAsync` seam at `POST /api/gvbridge/voicemail/{id}/read`, add the SMS-thread sibling (`POST /api/gvbridge/sms/threads/{threadId}/read`), subscribe to the unified `ReadStateChanged` SignalR event on the existing `/hub`, reconcile **idempotently keyed by `(id-or-threadId + isRead)`** (RotaryPhone echoes unconditionally, incl. to the originator), drop UI-local seeding (list endpoints' `isRead`/`hasUnread` are source-of-truth on reload), behind `RotaryPhone:Gv:MarkReadEnabled` (default off). No unread toggle (hidden). No new auth.

### GV-8 — Distinguish a failed conversation load from an empty one (the silently-empty texts pane).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#461](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/461) |
| Plan | [`plans/.../gv-texts-load-error-state.md`](superpowers/plans/2026-07-31-gv-texts-load-error-state.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [UAT F-1/F-2](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/REPORT.md) · [root-cause § Defect C](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/F-1-DIAGNOSIS.md) · [handoff error-state copy](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-phone-dark-theme-and-scrollbars.md) · [**GV-8 UAT — 10/10, 0 HIGH/MEDIUM**](uat/2026-07-31-gv8-error-state/REPORT.md) |
| Depends on | **GV-3** (texts surface: `PhoneTextsPanel`, `PhoneMessagesPanel`, `PhonePage` thread-open path). _**SHIPPED 2026-07-31.** Verified against a **genuine upstream HTTP 502** during a natural auth blackout (`psidtsAgeSeconds` 707), not a synthesised failure: the pane rendered `cloud_off` + "Couldn't load messages." + a working `Retry`. C7 confirmed the opposite failure mode is **not** introduced — a genuinely empty group thread still reads as **empty**. **One user-visible behavioural delta, by design (Task 6): a failed thread open no longer marks the thread read** — the unread dot is restored rather than silently cleared for a conversation the user was never shown, and the durable write-through is skipped (which also avoids 2-3 more upstream Google calls inside a dead auth window). A **successful `Retry` then performs the read-marking the failed open skipped**, so the dot does not linger once the conversation actually renders. A *successful* load followed by a *failed* mark-read still keeps the optimistic flip and reconciles later — ADR-024 §2, deliberately unchanged. Pre-merge review found 0 HIGH / 3 MEDIUM and a Polisher pass 1 HIGH / 1 MEDIUM / 1 LOW; all seven fixed. Two LOW deferred with justification (skeleton shimmer amplitude — a design-token question spanning every skeleton in the app; and a deploy-script runtime default), triaged 2026-07-31 into **UX-1** (shimmer amplitude — new row) and **OPS-1 (c)** (the deploy runtime default — folded, not standalone); a third item Builder flagged forward, the missing thread-list `== null` guard, is folded into **GV-9**. **⚠ ONE THING IN THIS PR IS SHIPPED BUT NOT LIVE-VERIFIED — `M-1`. Do not record it as proven.** Polisher's M-1 fix is the `Error && Messages == null` ordering (`PhoneTextsPanel.razor:61`) — *arriving content outranks a stale error flag*, so an inbound SMS landing on an open-but-failed thread renders the message rather than staying on `cloud_off`. It is **unit-covered** (`PhoneTextsPanelTests:141` — `Conversation_ShowsMessages_WhenErrorSetButMessagesArrived`) but was **never observed live**: it needs a **real inbound SMS to land while a failed thread is open**, no inbound traffic arrived during the UAT window, and the Tester **correctly declined to inject a synthetic message** rather than manufacture a pass. What *is* established live is the branch ordering around it (skeleton → error → empty → list, all four observed). **It is cheap to close opportunistically and needs no cycle:** text the GV number during a blackout (`psidtsAgeSeconds` 660–1200) with the failed thread open, and watch the pane. Also tracked in § Dependency / ordering notes. **Explicitly NOT blocked on RotaryPhone.** Their two defects (§ Cross-repo handoffs #5/#6) make this failure **rare**; this row makes it **honest**. Neither subsumes the other — shipping only theirs would leave a surface whose only defence is that the backend seldom fails. **Same bug class as GV-6** (every non-2xx → `null`) and deliberately kept separate; the reasoning is in § Dependency / ordering notes._ |
| Branch | `fix/gv-texts-load-error-state` |

**Distinguish a failed conversation load from an empty one (the silently-empty texts pane).** UAT **F-1** (HIGH): opening a text thread renders **"Start the conversation below."** with **no error, no spinner, no retry** — a 502 and a genuinely empty thread are indistinguishable on screen, and the failure is invisible to browser instrumentation (0 console errors, 0 failed requests — the page is Blazor Server, so the fetch happens server-side over SignalR).

**Root-caused, confirmed by source:** `GvBridgeApiService.GetSmsThreadMessagesAsync` (`src/Radio.Web/Services/ApiClients/GvBridgeApiService.cs:204-218`) wraps `GetFromJsonAsync` in `catch (Exception) { log; return null; }`, and `GetFromJsonAsync` calls `EnsureSuccessStatusCode()`, so **every non-2xx, timeout and deserialization error collapses to `null`** with no status distinction; then `PhonePage.razor:632` does `_openThreadMessages = messages?.Messages.ToList() ?? new()`, after which the two states are **byte-identical**. No `_openThreadError` field exists.

**Scope:** (a) make the API client express *outcomes* (result type / status) instead of a bare `null`; (b) add `_openThreadError` / `_openThreadLoading` page state and pass `Loading` + `Error` through `PhoneMessagesPanel.razor:184-191`, which today passes **neither** although **both parameters already exist** on `PhoneTextsPanel` (`PhoneTextsPanel.razor:172-173`) — this is also why the UAT saw **no spinner**: the skeleton branch is unreachable dead code in this hosting; (c) add the missing **error branch** to `PhoneTextsPanel.razor:36-68` (today exactly three branches — skeleton / empty / list, **no error branch at all**), reusing the **F-6** pattern verbatim from `PhoneMessagesPanel.razor:110-117` — `cloud_off` + **"Couldn't load messages."** + `Retry` — a string already specified at [`HANDOFF-phone-dark-theme-and-scrollbars.md:310`](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-phone-dark-theme-and-scrollbars.md); (d) fold in UAT **F-2** (MEDIUM) — the "Start the conversation below." copy must appear **only** for a genuine empty. Today it fires on failure *and* invites a reply into a hard-disabled composer (`SendEnabled=false`), which on a short-code thread is doubly wrong.

**The fix template already exists one level up:** the thread list does this correctly — `PhonePage.razor:595-616` sets `_threadsError` (and toasts "Showing the last update" when a cached list exists) and `PhoneMessagesPanel.razor:110-117` renders `cloud_off` + `Retry` off that flag.

**This is mostly copying an already-designed pattern down one level.** **Test gap is explicit:** `tests/Radio.Web.Tests/Services/GvBridgeApiServiceVoicemailSmsTests.cs:81` asserts **only** the happy path — add a non-2xx case there **plus** a bUnit case asserting the pane renders the **error** state, not the empty state.

**Do NOT attempt a client-side escaping workaround for the group-thread `%2F` bug** (that is RotaryPhone's — § Cross-repo handoffs #5): both double-escaping (`%252F`) and a raw `/` were tested and both fail, the latter falling through to their SPA fallback and returning `index.html` with HTTP 200.

### OPS-1 — Build stamp on `radio-web` + deploy verification for both services.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#485](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/485) |
| Plan | _plan TBD (small; scope is fully specified in this row)_ |
| Spec / handoff | [ADR-024 §3.3 amendment](../design/decisions/2026-06-20-gv-mark-read-durable-readstate.md) (context: the incident that surfaced it) · [GV-8 UAT `L-2`](uat/2026-07-31-gv8-error-state/REPORT.md) |
| Depends on | — |
| Branch | `feat/ops-web-build-stamp` |

**Build stamp on `radio-web` + deploy verification for both services.** **Most of this already exists — scope is the gap, not a greenfield build.** `Directory.Build.props` already stamps the git SHA into `AssemblyInformationalVersion` (`_StampGitCommitHash`), `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1` already passes `-p:SourceRevisionId` to **both** publishes and already **fails loudly (`exit 1`) on an API SHA mismatch**, and `Radio.API` already serves `GET /api/health/version` → `VersionInfoDto { GitSha, GitShaShort, InformationalVersion, AssemblyVersion, BuildTimestampUtc, AssemblyName }`.

**Two gaps remain, and together they are exactly the 2026-07-30 incident:** (1) **`Radio.Web` exposes no version endpoint at all** — its assembly already carries the correct SHA, it is simply unreadable at runtime; (2) **deploy verifies `radio-api` only** — for `radio-web` it checks `systemctl is-active` and nothing more, so a stale web binary passes verification silently. Add a minimal-API `GET /api/health/version` to `Radio.Web` (matching the existing `app.MapGet("/api/albumart/…")` convention — Radio.Web has no Controllers dir), returning the same shape; extend the deploy verify block to poll `:5002` and fail identically on mismatch. Also document that `BuildTimestampUtc` is file-mtime, i.e. *landed-on-disk* time, not compile time — the SHA is the authoritative signal.

**Interim stopgap, proven in the GV-8 UAT — reference it in the plan and keep it documented until the endpoint ships:** grep the deployed web binary for a symbol that exists only on the branch under test — `grep -ac RetryOpenThreadAsync /opt/radio-console/web/Radio.Web` (non-zero → the new binary is live). The Tester used `OpenThreadLoading`, `RetryOpenThreadAsync` and `GvCallOutcome` to confirm freshness by hand, precisely because `systemctl is-active` had reported a healthy service while proving nothing about *which* binary was running. Now also recorded in `CLAUDE.md` § Deployment.

**(c) FOLDED IN 2026-07-31 — the script's runtime default does not match the box (GV-8 UAT `L-2`, LOW, but sharp).** `deploy/Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:51` declares `[ValidateSet("linux-arm64","linux-x64")] [string]$Runtime = "linux-arm64"`, while `uname -m` on `192.168.86.50` is **`x86_64`** — so the **literal documented invocation** `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1 -TargetHost 192.168.86.50` publishes and ships **ARM64 binaries to an x64 host**. This nearly cost the GV-8 UAT its validity; the correct call is `-Runtime linux-x64` and the report records it as the rollback recipe.

**Fix direction, not prescribed:** probe `uname -m` over the SSH session the script already opens and **fail before publishing** on mismatch (cheapest, and it cannot go stale), and/or derive the default from the target host instead of hardcoding one.

**Note the sibling default has the identical shape** — `$TargetHost` defaults to **`piradio`**, which does not resolve from WSL (§ Dependency / ordering notes) — so the real defect is *"the script's defaults describe a box that no longer exists,"* and a `uname -m` guard fixes the half that silently produces a wrong-architecture deploy rather than an obvious connection failure.

**Why this is folded here rather than standing alone:** it is the **same file** and the **same failure class** as gap (2) above — *the deploy reports success without proving what it did* — so one Builder cycle should own deploy integrity end to end; a separate row would put two PRs on one script for a ~5-line change. _Splittable back out if OPS-1 slips: it has no dependency on the version endpoint and could ship on its own in an afternoon._

### KIOSK-1 — One kiosk launch line, and it works under Wayland.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ |
| Plan | [`plans/.../kiosk-desktop-launcher.md` § Part A](superpowers/plans/2026-08-18-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [handoff](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md) |
| Depends on | [#479](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/479) |
| Branch | `fix/kiosk-launch-contract` |

**One kiosk launch line, and it works under Wayland.** **This row exists because every deploy currently leaves the screen dead, and the script itself documents why.** `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:376` relaunches the kiosk with `DISPLAY=:0 nohup google-chrome …` on a box that runs **Wayland**, which its own comment at `:369-374` labels a *"KNOWN DEFECT, deliberately not fixed here."* The owner hit it live on 2026-08-18.

**The replacement is verified by hand, not proposed:** `systemd-run --user --collect google-chrome --kiosk … --ozone-platform=wayland …`, which starts the browser from the graphical session's *own* service manager and therefore inherits that session's `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` / `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS` instead of an SSH shell's.

**Confirmed by 2 established connections to `:5002` afterwards — and that is the meaningful check, not process existence: during the 2026-08-02 outage Chrome was running and `radio-web` returned 200 for ~33 hours while the count was 0.** Scope: a new `deploy/debian-x64/kiosk/bin/radio-kiosk-launch` that becomes the **single definition** of the kiosk command line (three callers converge on it — autostart, deploy, and the desktop icon — which today carry three different flag sets); a dedicated `--user-data-dir=~/.config/radio-kiosk-chrome`, which **pays for itself three times**: it lets `KIOSK-2`'s Exit spare the GV bridge, it **restores CDP on `:9223`** (Chrome ≥136 silently ignores `--remote-debugging-port` on the default profile), and it makes `--password-store=basic` safe **by construction** on a profile that was `basic` from first run — `CLAUDE.md` measured that flag destroying 45 `v11` → 16 `v10` cookies on an existing profile; **`setup-kiosk.sh` fixed to `install -m 755`, NOT `chmod +x`** (with the default umask `+x` yields **775**, and GNOME silently refuses to launch a group-writable `.desktop` — that mode bit is the entire reason the box's `GV-Bridge.desktop` was unlaunchable); the **missing repo→`~/Desktop` installer**, which is the root cause of the whole drift problem (the in-tree `radio-console.desktop` has carried `--password-store=basic` since Aug 11 and the live copy still does not, because nothing has ever installed it — **three separate instances of this drift turned up in one day**); and dropping **`onboard`** from `deploy/provision/packages.sh:88` + the autostart entry, which `docs/uat/2026-08-03-osk-wayland-viability/REPORT.md` already recommended (Chrome 151 on Wayland issues **zero** `zwp_text_input_v3.enable()` calls, so the OS keyboard cannot type into the kiosk at all).

**⚠ Two `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1` details that will silently misfire if skipped:** the stop block's cache and `Singleton*` paths point at `~/.cache/google-chrome` and `~/.config/google-chrome`, which belong to the **default** profile — a `--user-data-dir` profile keeps both **inside itself**, so leaving them alone clears the wrong cache and leaves the kiosk's own lock in place; and its comment at `:146-154` describes exactly those paths, so it becomes a **false comment** unless rewritten with the diff (this repo's `## Pre-Merge Review` rule).

**⚠ Merge-order: `KIOSK-1` BEFORE `KIOSK-2`, and it is not a preference** — every piece of `KIOSK-2` (scoped exit, single-instance guard, launcher) needs the dedicated profile to already exist; landing `KIOSK-2` first would mean matching the kiosk on the *absence* of a flag, which the design explicitly rejects as fragile. Plan Tasks **A0–A7**; A0 is a **box-probe task that pins four unknowns** (zenity's GTK major, `--timeout`/`--icon-name`/`--default-cancel` support, whether `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` is in `systemctl --user show-environment`, the `rotary-phone` unit name) and **must run first** — several later choices branch on its output.

**⚠ PROMOTED above `TEST-1` on 2026-08-18 with the owner's explicit approval, and the reason is the blast radius rather than the size of the fix: this defect fires on **every single deploy** and costs a dead kiosk screen each time, so it taxes every other row in this queue — including `TEST-1`'s own verification, which needs a live panel to confirm anything on the box. `TEST-1` remains the highest-priority row of the 2026-08-10 tranche; this is one row jumping it, not a re-ordering of that tranche.**

### TEST-1 — Stop the suite producing false CI signals — four flaky tests, and the ambient-`localhost:5000` defect underneath them.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#483](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/483) |
| Plan | _(a) done — and widened from 1 test to 3: `Fall` and `Ring` had the identical click-then-assert shape and raced the same await; only `Phase` had lost. (b) measured and **re-filed as `TEST-3`** — not this defect. (c) done: transport seam on all four hub services + an `IHttpMessageHandlerBuilderFilter` covering every `AddHttpClient<T>()`. **Verified with a listener bound to the port during full runs: 74 connections → 0 on :5000, 0 on :5004**, 877/877 Web and 325/325 API. UAT on the live app: all four hub services connected in production config with events flowing. Pre-merge review caught a comment of mine that overclaimed safety — `GetCoverArtUrlAsync` builds its own `HttpClient`, so the stub covered one of two transports; fixed with a second seam plus a regression test proven able to fail._ |
| Spec / handoff | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · provenance: the 2026-08-10 "no audio from the soundbar" debugging session (PRs #467 / #468 / #469) |
| Depends on | — _(no code dependency; claimable now, and **should be claimed before the other seven** — every one of them is verified by this suite)_ |
| Branch | `fix/test-rig-hermetic-and-flaky-waits` |

**Stop the suite producing false CI signals — four flaky tests, and the ambient-`localhost:5000` defect underneath them.**

**Highest priority of the 2026-08-10 tranche.** Four distinct tests failed under timing/load pressure on 2026-08-10 and passed on retry, during a debugging session that was chasing a real audio bug — **the flake was read as a regression and produced a wrong diagnosis before it was recognised as noise.** That is the cost this row exists to remove: a suite that lies intermittently makes every other row's verification untrustworthy.

**Three parts, and (c) is the actual fix.** **(a) A bUnit assertion raced across a real `await`.** `ModePicker_ClickingPhase_ActivatesPhaseSegment` (`tests/Radio.Web.Tests/Components/Shared/VisualizerPanelTests.cs:184-193`) clicks the Phase segment and asserts `is-active` / `aria-selected` **immediately**, with no wait for the re-render. `phase.Click()` dispatches `VisualizerPanel.SelectMode` (`src/Radio.Web/Components/Shared/VisualizerPanel.razor:207-229`), which assigns `_currentMode` **only after** `await UnsubscribeFromCurrentMode()` — and that await **completes synchronously when the SignalR hub is disconnected but makes a real network round-trip when it is connected**, which is exactly why this passes locally and flakes on the runner. Fix: wrap the assertions in `cut.WaitForAssertion(...)`.

**⚠ This is NOT loosening the assertion — it is the same condition, waited for rather than raced.** Do not re-file it as "the test was made permissive."

**Five files in this project already use the idiom — copy one rather than inventing a wait:** `Components/Pages/PhonePageThreadLoadErrorTests.cs`, `Components/Pages/PlayHistoryPageTests.cs`, `Components/Shared/CastDeviceDropdownTests.cs`, `Components/Shared/RadioControlPanelTests.cs`, `Components/Shared/PhoneStatusHeroTests.cs`.

**(b) ⚠ MEASURED AND RE-FILED 2026-09-01 — this part was misdiagnosed and is NOT part of this row.** The record described three `Radio.API.Tests` timing out at 30–46 s. Measured: **16–18 tests inflate and none times out** (325 passed / 0 failed both under load and alone), worst case **14.2 s**. Every inflated test is the **first-executed test of a class holding `IClassFixture<CustomWebApplicationFactory<Program>>`** — 17 such classes, 17 entries — so what is measured is host boot, which xUnit bills to the collection's first test.

**`Radio.API.Tests` cannot reach the network at all**: every `HttpClient` comes from `_factory.CreateClient()` over an in-memory `TestServer`. Control run of the project alone against 40 CPU-burner processes with `:5000` closed reproduced it exactly (1.05 s → 3.43 s per first-of-class). Cause is **CPU oversubscription** — 11 assemblies as concurrent processes, each defaulting xUnit to all 32 cores. The *"22/22 in ~5 s"* also fails checking: **no class here has 22 tests**, and ~5 s is the whole project's isolation wall time.

**Split out as `TEST-3` (P2, bound test parallelism).** Nothing to do in this row.

**(c) The underlying defect, and the real fix: unit tests can reach an ambient `localhost:5000`.** A unit test whose result depends on whether `radio-api` happens to be running on the self-hosted runner is the root problem — **(a) and (b) are both symptoms of a test rig that is not hermetic**, and (a)'s whole flake mechanism is "the hub was actually connected." Stub the hub/config clients in the test rig so no unit test can open a socket to the box.

**Ordering within the row: do (c) first, or at least alongside.** Shipping only (a) and (b) fixes two instances and leaves the class open — the next timing-sensitive test added will rediscover it.

### AUD-6 — Output device identity is an enumeration ordinal, so a persisted output preference silently changes which physical device it means.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#499](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/499) |
| Plan | _plan TBD (medium — the identifier change is small, the **migration** and the two read paths are where the work and the risk are)_ |
| Spec / handoff | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · provenance: the 2026-08-11 production deploy; enumeration captured from the `radio-api` journal on both days · the pre-existing name-keyed precedent is `AudioOutput:DeviceDisplay:DeviceFriendlyNames` ([`AudioOutputOptions.cs:70`](../src/Radio.Core/Configuration/AudioOutputOptions.cs)) |
| Depends on | — _(no row dependency; claimable now. **⚠ CLAIM THIS BEFORE `AUD-7` — the ordering is load-bearing, not a preference:** `AUD-7` makes startup **act** on this preference, and acting on an unstable key is worse than ignoring it. See § Dependency / ordering notes. **Same files as `AUD-7`** (`SoundFlowDeviceManager.cs`, `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs`, `SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs`, `DevicesController.cs`) — expect anchors to move if both are in flight. **Same root class as `AUD-2`/`AUD-4`** (an unstable identifier used as a persistent key) but **no file overlap with either**, so it can run alongside them.)_ |
| Branch | `fix/stable-output-device-identity` |

**Output device identity is an enumeration ordinal, so a persisted output preference silently changes which physical device it means.**

**⚠ The diagnosis is complete — do NOT re-brainstorm it. And read the falsification note before anyone "reopens" the 2026-08-10 question; this row is a different claim.**

**The mechanism, verified end-to-end against `main` @ `0b2ebcb`.** `SoundFlowDeviceManager` mints output ids straight off the MiniAudio enumeration index — `Id = $"playback-{i}"` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/SoundFlow/SoundFlowDeviceManager.cs:517`, and the sibling at `:695`), where `i` is the raw loop counter over `playbackDevices` (`:498`).

**That string is what reaches storage.** It is persisted to `AudioPreferences:CurrentOutput` from two places — `SoundFlowDeviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync` (`:234-238`) and `SoundFlowAudioEngine.PersistActiveOutputAsync` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/SoundFlow/SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:425`) — and read back at startup by `AudioEngineInitializationService` (`src/Radio.API/Services/AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:210`).

**And resolution back to hardware is pure string parsing, not a lookup:** `SoundFlowAudioEngine.GetDeviceIndexById` (`:1030-1043`) checks the `playback-` prefix and returns `int.TryParse(deviceId.AsSpan("playback-".Length))` — **there is no name comparison anywhere on that path.** So the persistent key *is* the ordinal, and any change in enumeration order, or a device appearing or disappearing, silently repoints the preference at different hardware.

**The measurement, on the box across today's deploy restart.** Aug 10 16:17 — `Playback device [0]: "AB13X USB Audio Analog Stereo"`, `[1]: "Built-in Audio Analog Stereo" (IsDefault=True)`. Aug 11 15:04 — `[0]: "Built-in Audio Analog Stereo" (IsDefault=True)`, `[1]: "AB13X USB Audio Analog Stereo"`. The store held **`playback-1`** across both.

**Aug 10 that meant the Soundbar; Aug 11 it means USB Audio Out. Nothing in the store changed — the meaning did.**

**⚠ It is intermittent by nature and will NOT reproduce on demand.** It surfaced only because today's restart happened to reorder enumeration; a plan that budgets for a deterministic repro will not get one. Reordering can be *forced* for a test (unplug/replug the USB DAC, or restart PipeWire) but that is a hardware exercise, not a unit test.

**⚠ THE CODEBASE ALREADY KNOWS ABOUT THIS, AND THE GUARD IT WROTE FOR IT CANNOT FIRE — read this before writing a fix, because the obvious reaction is "there is already a check."** `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:294-296` carries the comment *"After PipeWire restarts, MiniAudio device indices may shift, causing the wrong device to be selected even though the ID matches,"* followed by a verification block at `:297-330`.

**It is a tautology.** Its "expected" value, `preferredDeviceName`, is derived from the ordinal itself (`:258-262`, `outputDevices.FirstOrDefault(d => d.Id == preferredOutputId)`), and its "actual" value is `currentDevices.FirstOrDefault(d => d.Id == selectedId)` (`:300-303`) where `selectedId` is the same id just written at `:286`.

**Both sides are the same lookup, by the same key, against the same `SoundFlowDeviceManager` cache** — `outputDevices` is `GetOutputDevicesAsync()` from `:142`, and `currentDevices` is the same call again at `:299` — **so `activeDevice.Name` and `preferredDeviceName` are the same string, and the mismatch branch at `:304` does not fire.** If the ordinal has gone stale, the check confirms the *wrong* device against itself and passes. _Stated precisely, because this row must not overclaim the way the comments it is criticising do: the branch is not unreachable in the strict sense — the hot-plug refresh can rewrite `_cachedOutputDevices` between those two reads, and a **reorder inside that window** would make the two sides differ. That is a narrow, unrelated race, not the restart-scale reordering this row is about, and **it is the only path on which the guard can fire at all.**_ Its repair path is dead for a second, independent reason: it calls `_deviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync(correctDevice.Id)` (`:317`), which performs no native switch (see `AUD-7`).

**THE STABLE KEY ALREADY EXISTS, IS ALREADY COMPUTED, AND IS ALREADY TRUSTED — this row is largely about promoting it, not inventing one.** `AudioOutput:DeviceDisplay:DeviceFriendlyNames` (`src/Radio.Core/Configuration/AudioOutputOptions.cs:70`, written by `DevicesController.SetDeviceFriendlyName:1016-1021`) is a `Dictionary<string,string>` keyed by **device name**, consumed by `SoundFlowDeviceManager.ApplyFriendlyName` (`:432-451`) on `rawName`.

**That mapping survived the reordering correctly** — `"Built-in Audio Analog Stereo": "Soundbar"` and `"AB13X USB Audio Analog Stereo": "USB Audio Out"` resolved to the same physical devices on both days, which is precisely why the friendly names in the live payload are right while the id underneath them is wrong. The raw name is already carried through the domain model as `AudioDeviceInfo.RawName` (`src/Radio.Core/Interfaces/Audio/IAudioDeviceManager.cs:144`, populated at `SoundFlowDeviceManager.cs:519`).

**Fix direction — persist a stable identifier (the device name, or something more hardware-derived if the implementer can find one that survives a PipeWire restart), and keep `playback-N` as a transient view-model concern that never reaches storage.** The exact identifier is the implementer's call and should be argued in the plan; name-keying is the cheapest option that is already proven on this box, but it is not obviously the best one — device names are not guaranteed unique, and two identical USB DACs would collide where ordinals would not.

**Say which way and why.** **⚠ THREE CONSTRAINTS ON THE FIX, all cheap to miss.**

**(1) The key is polymorphic.** `AudioPreferences:CurrentOutput` holds either a device id **or** one of the virtual-output sentinels `google-cast` / `http-stream` (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.SetActiveOutputAsync:204-206`; the startup branch at `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:226`). A format change must leave those two values untouched and still distinguishable.

**(2) There are TWO read paths for this preference, not one, and they do not read the same store.** The startup service reads the **config store** (`:210`) and falls back to `IOptionsMonitor` (`:223`), while `SoundFlowDeviceManager`'s constructor restores from `IOptionsMonitor` **only** (`:115-128`, i.e. `appsettings.json:170`, which ships empty). The asymmetry is already commented at `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:201-203`.

**Both paths must handle a legacy value or the migration is half-applied.** **(3) The API DTO does not carry the raw name** — `DevicesController.MapToDeviceDto` (`:1486-1501`) projects `Id` and the friendly `Name` and drops `RawName` — so surfacing a stable id to the UI is a DTO change plus a `Radio.Web` change, not an infrastructure-only edit.

**⚠ MIGRATION NOTE — REQUIRED, and it is the part with real risk.** Every existing store holds an ordinal.

**A fix must be able to read a legacy `playback-N` without repointing anyone's output on upgrade** — and note the cruel detail: **on the very deploy that ships the fix, the legacy ordinal may already mean the wrong device**, so "resolve the old ordinal and rewrite it as a name" silently canonises whatever the enumeration happens to be at that moment. Decide deliberately between resolving-and-rewriting, and treating an unmigratable legacy value as *no preference* and falling back to the system default; **the second is more honest and the more defensible default, but it will move one user's output once, so say so in the plan rather than discovering it in UAT.**

**⚠ RECORDED SO IT IS NOT REOPENED: this exact fragility was raised as a hypothesis on 2026-08-10 and CORRECTLY FALSIFIED as the cause of the soundbar silence.** The app had opened the right device; the cause was the Cast startup mute, shipped in **#468**.

**That falsification stands — do not let this row be read as overturning it.** This row makes a strictly different claim: not *"this caused that outage"* but *"this fragility has now been observed firing,"* which the 2026-08-10 evidence neither established nor excluded.

**Test gap, stated because the one test that exists gives false comfort:** `tests/Radio.Infrastructure.Tests/Audio/SoundFlowDeviceManagerTests.cs:188-207` (`SetOutputDeviceAsync_SetsSelectedDevice`) asserts only that `GetSelectedOutputDeviceId()` returns the string that was just passed in — **a bookkeeping round-trip that would pass unchanged even if no audio device were ever opened** — and it silently `return`s (i.e. passes) when the runner has no audio hardware (`:194-198`), which is the same non-hermetic pattern `TEST-1` owns.

### AUD-7 — The reported active output diverges from where audio physically goes — the output selector cannot be trusted.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#500](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/500) |
| Plan | _plan TBD (medium — half (b) is small and shippable alone; half (a) is small in diff and sharp in blast radius, and is gated on `AUD-6`)_ |
| Spec / handoff | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · provenance: the 2026-08-11 production deploy — `/api/devices/output` compared against `pactl` sink state, plus the `radio-api` startup journal |
| Depends on | — _(no **row** dependency in the blocking sense, but **`AUD-6` SHOULD BE CLAIMED FIRST** and half (a) must not ship before it; see the ⚠ above and § Dependency / ordering notes. **Same files as `AUD-6`** — rebase rather than re-deriving anchors. **Also touches `DevicesController.cs`, which `AUD-5` does not**, and `SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs`, where `AUD-5`'s work is confined to `GoogleCastOutput.cs` — so no conflict with `AUD-5`.)_ |
| Branch | `fix/reconcile-reported-and-actual-output-device` |

**The reported active output diverges from where audio physically goes — the output selector cannot be trusted.**

**⚠ Diagnosis complete; do NOT re-brainstorm. This is the half that makes the UI lie, and any fix for `AUD-6` that does not also close this leaves a selector that is still wrong.**

**The observation, live on the box at `0b2ebcb`.** `/api/devices/output` returned `{"id":"playback-0","name":"Soundbar","isActive":false}` and `{"id":"playback-1","name":"USB Audio Out","isDefault":true,"isActive":true}` — while PipeWire had the application's stream on **sink 57, `alsa_output.pci-0000_00_1f.3.analog-stereo`** (the built-in analog, i.e. the Soundbar) and the USB sink (**17449**) `SUSPENDED`.

**Audio was physically reaching the Soundbar while the UI claimed USB Audio Out.**

**⚠ THE MECHANISM IS SHARPER THAN "the two concepts are not reconciled at startup" — read this, because it changes the fix.** It is not that two notions drift apart; it is that **the native device switch is reachable only from the interactive HTTP path.** `SoundFlowAudioEngine.SwitchPlaybackDevice` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/SoundFlow/SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:838`) is the **only** code anywhere that stops, disposes and re-initializes `_playbackDevice` (`:873-883`), and it has **exactly one caller in the solution**: `DevicesController.SetOutputDevice`, fire-and-forget on the thread pool (`src/Radio.API/Controllers/DevicesController.cs:250-254`; grep-verified — the only other reference to `GetDeviceIndexById` is its own declaration).

**The startup path never calls it.** What startup calls instead does nothing native. `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:286` calls `_deviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync(outputToUse)`, and `SoundFlowDeviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync` (`:213-244`) **validates the id against the device cache, assigns the `_selectedOutputDeviceId` string, and persists the preference — and touches no audio device.** Then `:337` calls `_audioEngine.SetActiveOutputAsync(outputToUse)`, which is explicit that the swap is somebody else's job: *"The local-device switch (native MiniAudio swap) is the caller's responsibility via `IAudioDeviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync` — this gate only owns mute state, virtual-output lifecycle, and persistence"* (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:222-224`).

**That comment assigns a responsibility that the named method does not discharge.** It is the same failure class as the three worked examples in `CLAUDE.md` § Pre-Merge Review, and fixing the wording belongs in whichever PR fixes the behaviour.

**So the device that is actually open is whatever `InitializeAsync` picked** — the system default, selected at `SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:504-517` and logged as *"Selected system default playback device at index {Index}"*, which is exactly the line the startup journal shows immediately before `SetActiveOutputAsync: "<none>" -> "playback-1"` and `Local output "unmuted"`.

**⚠ CONSEQUENCE THAT SEPARATES THIS ROW FROM `AUD-6`: this defect is DETERMINISTIC, not intermittent.** It fires on **every** restart in which the persisted preference is not the system default — **no reordering required**. `AUD-6` needs the enumeration to shuffle; this one does not. Do not let the two be collapsed into "the same intermittent thing."

**Why the UI reports what it reports.** `isActive` is a string comparison and nothing more: `DevicesController.cs:74-81` computes `isActive = d.Id == _deviceManager.GetSelectedOutputDeviceId()`, i.e. against the same `_selectedOutputDeviceId` field the bookkeeping just wrote. (Note `isDefault` on that payload is *also* derived from the user selection when one exists, `:79-80` — which is why the live capture shows `isDefault:true` on `playback-1` while MiniAudio reports the **built-in** device as the system default. Two different meanings of "default" on one payload; worth tidying here, but it is not the defect.)

**And the truth is not merely unreconciled — it is UNREACHABLE.** `_currentDeviceIndex` (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:44`, assigned at `:543`, `:809`, `:883`) is the only field that reflects the device actually open, and it is **private with no accessor of any kind**, so the API layer could not report the real answer today even if it wanted to. Exposing it (or something derived from `_playbackDevice`) is likely the smallest honest fix for the reporting half.

**FOUR SEPARATE NOTIONS OF "THE OUTPUT DEVICE" EXIST, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS REAL** — enumerate them in the plan rather than rediscovering them: **(1)** `SoundFlowAudioEngine._currentDeviceIndex` / `_playbackDevice` — where audio actually goes; set by init and by `SwitchPlaybackDevice`; **private, unexposed**.

**(2)** `SoundFlowDeviceManager._selectedOutputDeviceId` (`:52`) — what `/api/devices/output` reports and what gets persisted; set by startup and by the controller.

**(3)** `SoundFlowAudioEngine._activeOutputId` — the gate's local/Cast/HTTP notion, persisted to the **same** config key as (2).

**(4)** `LocalAudioOutput`'s device id (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Outputs/LocalAudioOutput.cs:166`, `:192`) — updated **only** from `DevicesController.cs:229`/`:242`, i.e. the interactive path, so it too is stale after every restart.

**A THIRD SITE SILENTLY REPOINTS WITHOUT TELLING ANYONE, and it is not in the owner's report:** the engine's recovery path re-initializes `playbackDevices[0]` unconditionally and sets `_currentDeviceIndex = 0` (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:805-809`) — so after a device-loss recovery, audio moves to whatever is at index 0 while (2), (3) and (4) all keep reporting the old preference.

**Fold this into the fix or state explicitly that it is out of scope; do not leave it undiscovered.**

**Scope, deliberately not prescribed beyond the two halves:** **(a)** make the startup path actually route to the persisted preference — the obvious shape is to call the same native switch the controller calls, but note it is currently fire-and-forget on a thread pool thread from an HTTP handler and startup wants something ordered and awaited; **(b)** make the reported active output derive from what is actually open rather than from a bookkeeping string, so the two cannot silently disagree again.

**⚠ (a) WITHOUT `AUD-6` IS A REGRESSION, NOT A PARTIAL FIX — this is the single most important sentence on this row.** Making startup honour the persisted ordinal converts a **mis-report** into a **mis-route**: today's store holds `playback-1`, which now resolves to USB Audio Out, so the owner's saved intent of "Soundbar" would be honestly and confidently routed to the wrong hardware.

**Claim `AUD-6` first, or ship (b) alone and hold (a) until the key is stable.**

**⚠ A guard already exists for the neighbouring problem and CANNOT FIRE — do not mistake it for coverage.** `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:294-330` compares "expected" against "actual" where both are the same lookup by the same key against the same cache; its repair path then calls `SetOutputDeviceAsync`, which does no native switch either. Full reasoning on `AUD-6`; stated here because whoever claims this row will find it first and may conclude the startup path is already verified.

**It is not.** **Test gap, and it is the fifth instance of this repo's comment-accuracy failure class:** `tests/Radio.API.Tests/Services/AudioEngineInitializationServiceStartupTests.cs:79-100` (`StartAsync_PersistedLocalDevice_RoutesThroughGate`) verifies **only** `SetActiveOutputAsync("playback-2")` and asserts nothing about which device ends up open — and its own comment (`:81-83`) says the persisted device must go through the gate *"not just the device-manager swap,"* **naming a swap that does not exist.** A test whose comment describes behaviour the production code never had is worse than no test: it makes the gap look covered. Any fix here must add an assertion about the **native** target, and correct that comment.

**⚠ OWNER / UAT NOTE — the evidence above is a snapshot as of `0b2ebcb`, and the owner is about to change it on purpose.** Hands-on UAT will re-select "Soundbar" by hand, which writes `playback-0` and makes the UI truthful again for the current enumeration.

**Do NOT read a later truthful `/api/devices/output` as evidence this row is fixed or was never real.** The only condition under which it fires is a **restart** with a persisted preference that is not the system default — so verification means: set a non-default output, restart `radio-api`, and confirm with `pactl list sink-inputs` that the app's stream is on the sink the UI names.

**Do not deploy or restart anything on the box to write this row's plan.**

### AUD-3 — Close the Cast connect/teardown data race — not just its observable outcome.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#468](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/468) |
| Plan | _no plan was written — and deliberately so: the fix landed inside #468 at the owner's direction rather than as its own cycle_ |
| Spec / handoff | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · merged evidence: `47da897`, `1fded0c`, `tests/Radio.Infrastructure.Tests/Audio/Outputs/GoogleCastOutputConcurrencyTests.cs`, `tests/Radio.Infrastructure.Tests/Audio/SoundFlowAudioEngineActiveOutputTests.cs` |
| Depends on | **PR #468 — ✅ merged `8b1ce0a`.** _The 🔒-on-a-PR deviation this row carried is now closed; it never had to resolve either way, because the blocker and the fix turned out to be the same PR._ |
| Branch | `fix/cast-connect-teardown-race` _(never used — shipped on `fix/cast-startup-mute-rollback-and-source-teardown`)_ |

**Close the Cast connect/teardown data race — not just its observable outcome.**

**✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-11 in PR #468, merge commit `8b1ce0a` (commits `47da897` "close the Cast connect/teardown data race" + `1fded0c`).** _This row was written as 🔒-on-#468 while #468 was open and scoped to the observable outcome only. The owner then directed the race be closed **before** #468 merged, and it was — so #468's final merged diff is materially larger than the one this row was written against.

**The diagnosis below is preserved verbatim as the record of what was wrong; the ✅ VERDICT block after it records what actually landed and what did not.**_

**The race.** The Cast connect path calls `_castOutput.InitializeAsync` / `ConnectAsync` / `SetStreamUrl` / `StartAsync` **without holding `SoundFlowAudioEngine._activeOutputLock`** (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/SoundFlow/SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:58`), while the teardown path `TearDownCastOutputAsync` (`:303-330`) manipulates the **same** `GoogleCastOutput` instance. `GoogleCastOutput` has **no synchronisation over its connection state**: `_client` and `_connectedReceiver` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Outputs/GoogleCastOutput.cs:29-30`) are reassigned from at least three places (`:142-150`, `:437-444`, `:514-520`), and the type's only lock is `_debounceLock` (`:48`), which guards something else entirely. Connect and teardown can therefore interleave on an unsynchronised client handle.

**⚠ Citation caveat — the method name in the session record only exists on #468's branch.** `TryConnectCastAsync` is `src/Radio.API/Services/AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:499` on `fix/cast-startup-mute-rollback-and-source-teardown`; on `main` @ `c129e0d` the same path is **still inline** at `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:405-452`. This row is written against the post-#468 shape because that is where the fix lands — **re-site the anchors when you claim it** rather than trusting either set. _**Resolved 2026-08-11:** #468 merged, so the branch-only shape **is** `main` now — `TryConnectCastAsync` is at `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:499` on `8b1ce0a`, confirmed. The caveat is retained because it is the reason the citation was correct in the first place._

**What #468 does and does not do, stated so its merge is not misread as closing this:** it adds a post-`StartAsync` teardown guard, which closes the **observable outcome** (the engine no longer leaves local output muted behind a Cast connect that did not survive) — **the underlying race is untouched.**

**Why this is its own cycle rather than a fix-up on #468:** doing it properly means the connect path runs under the same lock the teardown path uses (or `GoogleCastOutput` grows its own state lock), which **changes the engine's output-coordination API** and ripples into `AttachOutputCoordination` (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:171`) and every caller.

**And it wants hardware UAT, not a desk fix** — a real Chromecast, deliberate connect/disconnect churn during startup, and the `/stream/audio/mp3` path exercised while teardown runs. Budget for time on the box.

**— END OF THE 2026-08-10 DIAGNOSIS. What follows is the 2026-08-11 verdict, read off the merged diff at `8b1ce0a` rather than off the merge report. —** **✅ VERDICT: fully satisfied on the code. Recorded here so nobody re-derives it.** The fix took the **second** of the two options this row itself named — *"or `GoogleCastOutput` grows its own state lock"* — precisely because the first (running the connect under `_activeOutputLock`) would convert the race into a multi-second hang on the output picker: a Cast connect can park for tens of seconds inside SharpCaster calls that do not observe cancellation.

**No lock is held across any network call.** **(1) `GoogleCastOutput` gained `_lifecycleLock` (`:39`) + `_connectionGeneration` (`:45`).** All three reassignment sites this row cited are now inside it — `InitializeAsync` (`:179-190`), the new `TryPublishConnectionAsync` (`:599-623`), and `DisconnectAsync` (`:643-655`) — plus `DisposeAsync` (`:1651-1663`) and a snapshot read in `StopAsync` (`:930-937`).

**Six critical sections, all await-free**, all plain field/auto-property swaps (`Name` and `ConnectedDevice` are auto-properties with no side effects — checked, because a setter that re-entered the engine would break the stated lock order).

**(2) The NRE mechanism this row named is structurally eliminated, not merely guarded.** The old code stored the resolved receiver in `_connectedReceiver` and dereferenced it after a teardown had nulled it; `ConnectAsync` now resolves into a **local** `ChromecastReceiver? receiver` and snapshots the client, so the long network work operates on values a concurrent teardown cannot touch. Teardown **preempts rather than queues**: `DisconnectAsync` bumps the generation and takes the state out from under an in-flight connect *first*, then does its network work on the snapshot. A connect that lost re-validates at publish, disconnects what it built, and hands the state machine back.

**(3) The engine API change this row predicted landed** — `BeginCastConnectAsync` (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:361`) / `TryCommitCastConnectAsync` (`:387`) over `_castConnectEpoch` (`:65`), bumped inside `SetActiveOutputAsync` under the existing `_activeOutputLock` (`:58`, unmoved). The commit **re-checks the epoch and tears Cast down under a single acquisition**, replacing the check-then-act (a `_localFallbackApplied` flag read, then an action) that had a window between the two steps. It did **not** ripple into `AttachOutputCoordination` (`:173`) as this row guessed it would — the epoch handshake sits beside that API rather than through it.

**(4) One real defect was found in the race fix itself and fixed before merge (`1fded0c`), and it is worth knowing about because it was worse than the race:** `ConnectAsync` sets `State = Connecting` on entry and the new discard-on-supersede branch returned without resetting it — and `Connecting` is a dead end for this class (`ConnectAsync` requires Ready/Stopped, `ValidateCanInitialize` accepts only Created/Error), so the **first** teardown-during-connect would have made Cast permanently unusable until process restart. Losing that race is the *normal* outcome of the startup auto-connect rolling back, not an edge case. The branch now sets `State = Ready`.

**⚠ THREE THINGS THIS ROW ASKED FOR DID NOT SHIP. Do not record them as done.** **(a) The hardware UAT this row explicitly demanded never happened.** #468 added **zero** files under `docs/` — no UAT report, no ADR — and its verification is desk-only: a deterministic `internal` hook plus a real loopback listener, and a substituted transport. No real Chromecast, no connect/disconnect churn on the box, no `/stream/audio/mp3` exercised during teardown.

**This is the GV-8 `M-1` situation again** (see that row's ⚠ and § Dependency / ordering notes) — shipped, unit-pinned, **live-unverified**. It is cheap to close opportunistically and needs no cycle: switch the output picker away from Cast while a startup auto-connect is mid-flight and confirm Cast neither wedges at `Connecting` nor streams alongside the soundbar.

**(b) The service-level commit call is untested.** `AudioEngineInitializationServiceStartupTests` builds the service with `new Mock<IAudioEngine>()` (`:381`), while **both** epoch call sites gate on the concrete type — `_audioEngine is SoundFlowAudioEngine` at `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:522` (capture) and `:569` (commit). So `castEpoch` is `null` in **every** test in that file and the commit branch is unreachable there. The engine methods have four direct tests in `SoundFlowAudioEngineActiveOutputTests`; it is the **wiring between them that is unpinned**, and a refactor that dropped either `is` check would pass the whole suite.

**(c) Closing the test gap cost two `internal` seams** — `ConnectRaceHookForTests` (`GoogleCastOutput.cs:54`) and `ConnectTransportOverrideForTests` (`:64`), the second added only because the first could not reach the succeeded-then-lost path offline. Genuine design debt, and the **second** time this codebase has bought coverage with a seam — folded into `TEST-2` as a pattern rather than left as an incident.

**Two smaller observations, recorded because they are the kind of thing that decays into a bug:** the class comment at `GoogleCastOutput.cs:32` claims `_lifecycleLock` *"guards every read/write of `_client` / `_connectedReceiver` / `ConnectedDevice`"* — **it does not**; roughly twenty reads remain outside it (`:698`, `:710`, `:776`, `:781`, `:800`, `:1044`, `:1173`, `:1230`, `:1479`, `:1566`, `:1591`, `:1622`, …). The design is still sound *only because* `_client` is deliberately never nulled after first assignment — `DisposeAsync` says so in as many words at `:1656-1659` — so the `_client!` dereferences cannot NRE.

**But the comment asserts an invariant the code does not hold, and the next person to null `_client` anywhere will believe the lock protects them.** That is the same trap as `AUD-4`'s *"Removed audio source … from mixer"* log. Second: `ConnectAsync` threads the client **snapshot** into `SubscribeToReceiverStatus(client)` — a parameter added by this very fix, documented at `:1436-1441` — and then immediately calls `SyncInitialVolumeAsync()` with no argument, which reads the `_client` **field** at `:1479`. One of three sibling call sites was left on the field. Low severity (post-publish the two agree unless something superseded again) and a one-line change; **noted, not queued.** _**⚠ BOTH of those two closing observations have since resolved — updated 2026-08-11 (second pass), and this is the only edit made to this ✅ row.**_ _**(1) The over-claiming class comment is FIXED.** PR #473 (`0870410`) rewrote it: it now states that the lock serializes the connection-state **swaps** and explicitly says an earlier revision claiming it guarded every read *"never did, and believing it makes the unlocked dereferences look safer than they actually are."* It goes further than this row asked, writing the **precondition** that licenses the unlocked reads (`_client` is never set back to null) directly into the file.

**Worth knowing how it was caught, because the lesson is not the one it looks like:** #473's own **first draft of the corrected comment over-claimed in turn** — it said "never a null one," when the field is in fact null before the first `InitializeAsync` — and only a reviewer briefed to actively **falsify** the comment caught that. Reviewing a comment for plausibility is not the same as checking it against the code.

**That is now a standing checklist item in `CLAUDE.md` § Pre-Merge Review**, where this row's `AUD-4` cross-reference is cited as one of its three worked examples._ _**(2) The `SyncInitialVolumeAsync()` field-read observation has been superseded by a real defect one step downstream — now `AUD-5`, and the honest note is that this row under-read it.** This row called it "low severity, a one-line change, noted not queued," and **for the field read itself that verdict still holds** — #473's precondition block establishes why the unlocked read is safe.

**But the assessment stopped at the read.** What #473's review then found is that the same method's **success path fires `CastVolumeChanged`**, whose sole subscriber sets *and persists* `AudioManager.MasterVolume`, with **no generation re-check between the status response and the event fire** — so a teardown landing in that window persists the volume of a superseded connection.

**That is a user-visible, persistent-state defect, not a cosmetic inconsistency, and it PREDATES #468** (`b420edc`, 2026-02-11) — so it is neither this row's residue nor a regression from this row's fix.

**See `AUD-5`.** ⚠ **Note the anchor:** the `:1479` cited above is pre-#473; that read is now at `:1545`, along with every other anchor in this row's `GoogleCastOutput.cs` citations, which shifted **+66** uniformly. Those citations have been left as written because they are the historical record of what #468 fixed — **re-site them against `main` before reusing any of them.**_

### KIOSK-2 — Three desktop icons, one repair-and-open action.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ |
| Plan | [`plans/.../kiosk-desktop-launcher.md` § Part B](superpowers/plans/2026-08-18-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [handoff](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md) |
| Depends on | [#480](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/480) |
| Branch | `feat/kiosk-desktop-launcher` |

**Three desktop icons, one repair-and-open action.** Four hand-drifted icons become three, and **Radio Console** becomes the single *make-everything-right* tap: probe `radio-api` / `radio-web` / `rotary-phone` / the GV bridge in parallel (≤2s), start whatever is down in dependency order, then open the kiosk.

**⚠ TWO RULES INVERT THE WHOLE DESIGN IF GOT WRONG, and both are the reason this can ever be silent.** (1) **The kiosk browser is the SUBJECT of the action, not a reported component.** The desktop is only visible *when the kiosk is closed*, so the kiosk is **always** down when the icon is tapped — if starting it counted as a repair, the dialog would fire on **every single tap** and the silent path would never happen. (2) **Google Voice auth is dead ~9 minutes in every 20 by design, and that is deliberately NEVER reported.** `available` / `degraded` / `cookiesValid` on `/api/gvbridge/status` are **liars** (captured reading healthy while both SMS endpoints returned hard 502s); `psidtsAgeSeconds` is the only honest field and is a live blackout clock — `<660` healthy · `660–1200` **normal trough, silent** · `>1200` or absent = the refresh cycle itself stopped. A naive "is Google OK?" check answers *no* on ~45% of taps, and **a status surface that cries wolf is worse than no status surface**. Owner approved the **stateless `>1200` probe** (§11 Q3, no Architect escalation) and confirmed the field live: `psidtsAgeSeconds: 310`. When something *did* need fixing, one zenity dialog reports **all four** components using the corpus's existing `[label][value][pill]` grammar and `--signal-*` hex values, in three tiers mapped onto `--info` / `--warning` / `--error` — **no new severity vocabulary is invented** (two new pill *labels* are: `Starting` and `Needs sign-in`, both justified in handoff §9). Started-for-you auto-closes in 10s; degraded waits for a tap; hard failure **holds the kiosk back** and offers `Try again` / `Open anyway`.

**Two ordering rules that are easy to get backwards:** on the success path the kiosk launches **before** the dialog (Wayland stacking is decided by launch order, so a dialog raised first is swallowed by the fullscreen kiosk), and on the error path the kiosk is **held back** (the error dialog carries the only explanation plus the retry). Also: `Exit Browser` → **`Exit to Desktop`** (§11 Q1 approved), scoped to the kiosk profile so the GV bridge survives — today's `pkill -f chrome` kills it and nothing restarts it on demand; **`Shutdown System` gains a confirm that never auto-proceeds** (§11 Q2 approved, overriding the earlier "leave as-is") — GNOME's own confirm carries a **60-second countdown and then powers off by itself**, so an accidental tap plus walking away powers the box off anyway; and the **Anderson Console mark finally ships** (`branding/favicon.svg`, unwired until now) to `~/.local/share/icons/radio-console/` referenced by **absolute path** — a theme name is precisely what failed here (`Icon=audio-radio` exists in no installed theme and silently resolved to a generic document glyph).

**⚠ The in-app Blazor favicon (`App.razor:9`) is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE** — the owner deferred it to its own pass because `--kiosk` hides the tab strip entirely, so it is never visible on the appliance. Do not "finish the job" by changing it here.

**⚠ Cross-repo boundary: `gv-bridge-ensure.sh`, its watchdog and its nightly timer are ROTARYPHONE-OWNED and are being brought under version control in that repo RIGHT NOW.** This row **invokes and probes only** — it must never own, reimplement, edit, install or `pkill` bridge startup; the script is resolved from a candidate list at runtime because the canonical path may move.

**Three prerequisites, not polish:** 56px GTK buttons (GTK's ~34px default is under the 48px touch floor — **if the override cannot be made to work, say so rather than shipping 34px**), the `chmod 755` fix (`KIOSK-1`), and re-running `setup-kiosk.sh` **on the box** — merging alone changes nothing.

**Testability is designed in because the box cannot be screenshotted** (Shell screenshot API + `GetWindows` are `AccessDenied` on GNOME 46; `gnome-screenshot`/`grim`/`scrot` absent): `radio-console-open` ships `--print-status`, `--dry-run` and `--dialog-demo` so every tier decision is verifiable over SSH, leaving only two checks that genuinely need the owner's eyes. Plan Tasks **B1–B8**.

### ENC-4 — `EncoderHud` — every knob visible within 100 ms, on every route.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ |
| Plan | [`design/plans/ENC-4-encoder-hud.md`](../design/plans/ENC-4-encoder-hud.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [punch list §3.0 `ENC-4`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [encoder handoff Rev 3 §6](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) |
| Depends on | **✅ met** — `ENC-1` [#498](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/498) and `ENC-3` [#511](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/511) both merged |
| Branch | `fix/enc-4-review-findings` |

**`EncoderHud` — every knob visible within 100 ms, on every route.** 🔴 **P0, and the defect the whole encoder design handoff exists to fix.** Two of the four knobs currently produce **no visible evidence that anything happened**, and one changes the machine's entire behaviour from an invisible internal counter — a knob that acts silently is worse than a knob that does nothing, because the user's response to silence is to turn it further. Scope: one component, **two hosts** (`EncoderHud.razor` in `MainLayout` for normal routes, again inside `Sleep.razor` with `Variant="Sleep"`, because `/sleep` is on `EmptyLayout` and `MainLayout` is not in that tree).

**Geometry is the whole trick** — the HUD divides 1920 px into quarters (centres 240 / 720 / 1200 / 1680) and renders the active knob's card in its own quarter, so something lights up above the knob that was turned. Built from three pieces already in the tree: the **unused** `.snackbar-enter` / `.snackbar-exit` primitives (`design-system.css:1218-1219`), the `GainPopoverService` layout-root hosting pattern, and `SourceBubble`.

**No new design tokens** (handoff §6.9 — no `--hud-*` anything). Plus **host-side long-press synthesis** at 600 ms with a ring from 300→600 ms (the protocol has no long-press gesture; this is synthesised from press/release edges), which also moves the short press **from press to release**; and **`ENC-4b`** — turning the volume knob while muted unmutes and applies the delta in the same frame, which the Designer calls "the most important small rule in this document".

**`ENC-4a` (the persistent topbar `MUTED` chip) already shipped** as [#493](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/493) and is the pattern to follow for cross-route persistence, **not work to redo**.

⛔ **Does NOT remap the router's index→handler table** (still `0=Volume 1=Tuning 2=Source 3=Visualization` against a cabinet reading VOLUME / SOURCE / PRESETS / TUNING) — index 0 is VOLUME under both, so the dangerous knob is already right, and the remap belongs with `ENC-5`/`ENC-7` which introduce the handlers it would point at.

⛔ Does not build the selector overlays, the three-state wake model, blanking, or the `MEMORY`→`PRESETS` rename. 17 tasks, 6 phases, ~49 new unit/bUnit tests, and a browser Test Plan Tester can drive.

### ENC-4c — The `EncoderHud` geometry rotation — `ENC-4` shipped it on the wrong axis.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ |
| Plan | [handoff §6 Rev 5](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) · [`design/plans/ENC-4-encoder-hud.md`](../design/plans/ENC-4-encoder-hud.md) (geometry sections superseded) |
| Spec / handoff | [encoder handoff Rev 5 §6.1, §6.2, §6.7, §6.9, §6.10](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) · [`design/hardware/front-panel-layout_4.svg`](../design/hardware/front-panel-layout_4.svg) |
| Depends on | **✅ met** — `ENC-4` shipped |
| Branch | `fix/hud-vertical-geometry` |

**The `EncoderHud` geometry rotation — `ENC-4` shipped it on the wrong axis.** ⚠ **Not a redesign and not a re-plan: every design question was closed before this row was claimed** (handoff Rev 4 §6.2 corrected the geometry against the owner's as-built drawing; Rev 5 closed the collisions, the centring, the keyframe exception and the phase contract). `ENC-4` divided the **1920 px width** into quarters at `left: 240 + index * 480`, which is right for four knobs in a **row beneath** the display. The drawing puts them in a **vertical column to the LEFT of the LCD**, uniform 29.63 mm pitch, 15 mm diameter, on a 406.4 × 152.4 mm panel — so the axis was wrong and all four constants with it. Cards now anchor to `left: 24px` and band down the **720 px** axis at **90 / 270 / 450 / 630**, the measured projections (93.05 / 271.02 / 448.98 / 626.95) deliberately rounded: 3.05 px of worst-case deviation is 0.508 mm on the panel against a nearest wrong band 178 px away, and *“93.05 reads as a measurement and invites the next reader to re-measure or round it; 90 reads as a layout decision.”* **The bands, the engraved names and the index→knob mapping have ONE definition** — `Radio.Core.Configuration.FrontPanelGeometry`, citing `design/hardware/front-panel-layout_4.svg` — because §6.2 names four surfaces that need them and this repo has already paid once for a value defined in three places. Three mechanics changed with the axis: `margin-left: -180px` has **no vertical twin** (card height varies — measured on the box, 178.5 px volume vs 92.5 px frequency and 113.5 px track), so the card is vertically centred on its band and **clamped ≥ 8 px inside the viewport** — load-bearing, since the measured 178.5 px card centred on band 90 would span y 0.75–179.25, i.e.

**off the top of the viewport**, and on band 630 would come within 0.75 px of the bottom; the clamp engages on exactly those two bands and nowhere else, moving the card 7.25 px — expressed on the independent `translate` property so the entrance animation cannot drop it (verified mid-animation on the appliance); `snackbarSlideIn`'s `translateY(100%)` points the wrong way for a left-anchored card, so §6.1's **declared, narrow** mirrored keyframe pair is implemented as declared (⚠ **a Polisher must read `.encoder-hud-enter` as declared, not as drift**); and §6.10's phase contract is **four explicit arms, not three** — an unrecognised phase is *not holding* (it renders nothing, and a true `IsHolding` suspends the dismissal timer and strands a card forever), while **`"Value"` keeps preserving `IsHolding`** or a turn mid-hold collapses the progress ring.

⛔ **Occlusion was decided, not redesigned:** transient occlusion accepted on every band, no position / width / z-order change — per-route suppression and z-order yielding were rejected because they violate the handoff's own principle 2.

⛔ Does not remap the router's index→handler table; that is still `ENC-5` / `ENC-7`.

✅ **SHIPPED** — unit suite green, UAT on the box at 1920×720, pre-merge review clean.

### ENC-5 — The SOURCE overlay, the shared selector component, and the router remap.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ |
| Plan | [`ENC-5-source-overlay.md`](../design/plans/ENC-5-source-overlay.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [rotary-encoder-mapping §4.4 · §6.6 · §6.2](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) |
| Depends on | **`ENC-4`** (hard — mounts into its seam and edits its files) |
| Branch | — |

**The SOURCE overlay, the shared selector component, and the router remap.** 🔴 **P0.** The SOURCE knob today moves an invisible counter and logs at Debug; this row gives it a screen. Centred overlay, ~440 px, **preview-then-commit** — ⛔ do **not** "simplify" to live-commit-per-detent, which would tear down and stand up an audio source at every detent, straight into the capture-lifecycle and `autoSwitchOnConnect` bugs.

**Press is one rule, not two:** press commits the highlight, so with the overlay closed it commits what is already playing — which changes nothing and opens the overlay showing you where you are. That is what makes a mis-grab free. All five states ship: A preview · B dimmed **with a reason** (reusing `SourceBubble`'s `" · offline"` idiom) · C a press on a dimmed row flashes amber 1.5 s and **leaves the overlay open**, never a silent no-op · **D in-flight spinner · E failure card** — D and E are not optional polish, because an overlay that dismisses into silence is how a person concludes the knob is broken and starts pressing repeatedly, which is the input pattern that provokes the capture-lifecycle bug.

**D7 folded the radio bands in and that is where 2–3 d became 5–6 d:** committing a band while radio is *already active* is a **BAND CHANGE, not a source switch** (`SetBandAsync`, no teardown, no spinner, no fade, restore that band's last-tuned frequency); committing one from another source does both and *is* a real switch; the current-marker tracks the active **band**, so on AM row 2 is marked; and the knob and `RadioControlPanel`'s band pills must be one state.

⭐ **This row also carries the router remap** — see the ordering note below; it ends at `0 = VOLUME · 1 = SOURCE · 2 = Visualization (seat-warmer) · 3 = TUNING`.

**Five deviations are declared in the plan's §0.4** and every one is a case where the handoff assumed something the tree does not have — most importantly **no tuner reports which bands it supports**, so the plan adds `IRadioControl.SupportedBands` with a default interface implementation.

**Two findings correct the row's own text:** `RadioControlPanel` **already** subscribes to `RadioStateChanged`, so the one-state rule is met today and the task is a regression guard rather than a rebuild; and **`ENC-9a` did not remove `VisualizerPanel`'s local `_currentMode`** — it added the missing subscription — so the template is "one authoritative owner + typed broadcast + a component that re-syncs without echoing", not "no component may hold a copy".

**Builds the shared component `ENC-7` consumes** — row model, preview machine, overlay, CSS, wire format, and the seven-row window SOURCE does not need.

✅ **SHIPPED** — [#536](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/536). End state `0 = Volume · 1 = SOURCE · 2 = Visualization · 3 = Tuning`; index 2 is `ENC-7`'s.

⚠ **`ENC-7` inherits three things:** the shared `EncoderSelectorOverlay` is consumed unchanged (its only parameter is the payload — adding a `ListKind` would undo "one component, two lists"); the plan's **44 px preset-row modifier was deliberately not built**, because nothing could apply it without that forbidden branch, so add it beside the caller that needs it; and the Settings page's cabinet-mismatch warning is now computed per knob, so `ENC-7` should not need to touch it.

⚠ **Pre-merge review found two HIGH defects** (the State D spinner dismissed itself at 1500 ms mid-switch; the no-tuner band list was cached permanently), both fixed with regression tests.

⚠ **UAT H2 passed — zero new xruns across 1,664 tuner calls — but measured ~52 ms per tuner step**, so an 8-step detent is ~416 ms and a hard flick tunes for ~6 s after the hand stops; logged in `design/FUTURE-WORK.md`, not changed here.

### ENC-7 — The PRESETS knob: recall and save on the existing bank, and the last remap.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#541](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/541) |
| Plan | [`ENC-7-presets-knob.md`](../design/plans/ENC-7-presets-knob.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [rotary-encoder-mapping §4.3 · §4.4 · §6.6](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) |
| Depends on | **`ENC-5`** (hard — consumes its component and remaps the index it parks) |
| Branch | [#541](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/541) |

**The PRESETS knob: recall and save on the existing bank, and the last remap.** 🔴 **P0.** Turn moves a highlight through the saved-station bank and **nothing plays**; press = **Recall** (switch source *and band* if needed, tune, play — **not** scoped to the active source, which is what keeps the knob alive from Bluetooth or Phono); long-press 600 ms = **Save to the next free slot, never overwrites**. Empty state is instructional — `NO STATIONS SAVED` / `hold this knob to save what's playing` — so the knob teaches its own use.

⚠ **The bank is not the shape the punch list describes, and this is the biggest finding: there are no 7 slots.** `RadioPresetService.MaxPresets` is **50** globally; `RadioPreset` has **no** `SlotNumber` and neither does the SQLite table — the slot is a per-band ordinal re-derived per request in `RadioController.GetPresets:585-589`; there is **no next-free-slot logic** because gaps cannot exist (deleting slot 2 of 3 renumbers the survivor). The design survives intact and is simpler — "never overwrites" is free because no overwrite path exists, and `PRESETS FULL` is kept verbatim while being honestly noted as a message that will essentially never fire.

⭐ **But one case the handoff does not cover fires constantly:** holding on a station already saved throws a duplicate error, and silence there is the same defect this arc exists to fix — the plan adds **`ALREADY SAVED · slot NN`** and flags it as an addition to the spec, not an interpretation.

⚠ **Recall cannot use `POST /api/radio/presets/{id}/load`** — it returns 400 `"Radio is not the active source"`, which is exactly the case the knob exists to serve; recall is implemented server-side instead.

⚠ **`IRadioPresetService` is scoped and the router is a singleton** — reach it through `IServiceScopeFactory`, never a constructor injection.

⭐ **The on-screen bank is renamed `MEMORY · n saved` → `PRESETS · n saved`** because the cabinet is engraved PRESETS.

**This is a DELIBERATE, DECLARED deviation** from [`HANDOFF-saved-station-display.md`](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-saved-station-display.md), recorded in Rev 3's header, in Designer §4.4 and in punch-list §6 precisely so a later consistency pass does not revert it — **Builder must put it in the PR body and Polisher must not flag it as drift.** One functional line (`RadioControlPanel.razor:249`), one test (`RadioControlPanelTests.cs:835`) and ten stale comments; scope the verification grep with `--glob '!**/bin/**' --glob '!**/obj/**' --glob '!.claude/worktrees/**'` or it reports ~90 false positives from generated XML docs and stale agent worktrees. **Ends the remap at `0 = VOLUME · 1 = SOURCE · 2 = PRESETS · 3 = TUNING`** — matching the escutcheon and what `ENC-11` has pushed to the device since #509. **Consumes `ENC-5`'s component unchanged; the Web side of this row is one string.**

### ENC-8 — The encoder Settings surface, and the stale docs behind it.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#527](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/527) |
| Plan | [`design/plans/ENC-8-encoder-settings-surface.md`](../design/plans/ENC-8-encoder-settings-surface.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [punch list §3.5 `ENC-8`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [encoder handoff Rev 3 §7.7–§7.8](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) |
| Depends on | **✅ met** — `ENC-1`, `ENC-2` and `ENC-11` all merged (O10). Not blocked by `ENC-4`, but shares three files with it (`RotaryEncoderActionRouter.cs`, `AudioServiceExtensions.cs`, the `ApiModels.cs` tail) — expect to rebase. |
| Branch | [#527](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/527) |

**The encoder Settings surface, and the stale docs behind it.** 🔴 **P0. `ENC-11`'s tiered fault model works and is verified on hardware — this is the half that lets anyone diagnose it.** Four cards under System Config → Integrations → Rotary Encoders: **Status** (connection, config tier, last verified, last saved-to-device with a staleness comparison); **Device configuration** (read-only, every field × 4 encoders, labelled by cabinet name `VOLUME · SOURCE · PRESETS · TUNING` and **never by index**, each row showing whether the device agrees); **Direction** (four `Reverse` toggles — the one editable thing; toggling pushes immediately and marks the flashed copy stale); **Actions** (`Save to device`, `Re-apply settings`, `Reset counters`).

⛔ **Factory reset is deliberately absent and a test pins it absent** — it would put the device on defaults where one volume detent spans the full range.

⚠ **The `Save to device` copy is load-bearing and a reviewer should treat a mismatch as a defect:** flash holds exactly the operating configuration, i.e. exactly what the screen shows — enforced structurally (screen, push and flash read one object from one call) and pinned by a test, not left to care. Underneath it: an `IRotaryEncoderProvisioning` facet on the same `HidRotaryEncoderService` instance, retained sent/read-back state and timestamps, **the first code in this repo that ever sends `SaveConfig` (0x01)**, staleness decided by comparing a stored SHA-256 of the flashed bytes rather than inferred from a timestamp, and a maintenance channel that writes under a lock while the read loop stays the only reader. The mapping table is served **from the router** so it cannot drift again — one array that both dispatch and display read, which makes `ENC-5`/`ENC-7`'s remap a one-line edit. Plus the doc fixes: `INTEGRATIONS.md:22-26` (wrong 8-byte report format, superseded by `ENC-1`), `:125` (swap-the-A/B-pins advice, superseded by `reverse`), `:80`/`:95` (`TuningStepKHz` — deleted from code by #490, still documented), `:88` (`Enabled` documented as defaulting `false`; it is `true` since `ENC-0`), `:5` (“all integrations are disabled by default”), and `CLAUDE.md`'s MudBlazor claim. `VolumeStepPercent`'s duplicate **editable** numeric is deleted; the value itself is kept (the router reads it every detent) and relocated read-only into card 2.

**20 tasks, 5 phases, a hardware gate at Task 4, and a 16-step browser Test Plan at 1920×720.**

### ENC-12 — Tiered config-fault surfacing — giving `ENC-11`'s safety response a voice.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#535](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/535) |
| Plan | [`design/plans/ENC-12-config-fault-surfacing.md`](../design/plans/ENC-12-config-fault-surfacing.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [punch list §3.5 `ENC-12`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [encoder handoff Rev 3 §7.4/§7.6](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) · [bell-failure handoff §3.7](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-bell-failure-surfacing.md) |
| Depends on | **✅ met** — `ENC-8` [#527](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/527) and `ENC-4` / `ENC-4c` all merged. |
| Branch | — |

**Tiered config-fault surfacing — giving `ENC-11`'s safety response a voice.** 🔴 **P0, and the row that closes the encoder arc's worst silence.** When a safety field does not read back, the host **already** drops the volume knob's per-event clamp from 6 units to 2 and holds it there until a push verifies. That response is real, correct, verified on hardware — and completely invisible. The owner experiences it as a volume knob that has quietly gone sluggish inside sealed furniture. Two surfaces: **(1)** a cross-route fault badge on the **Settings** nav pill, reusing the bell-failure §3.7 pattern verbatim (same corner, same 12 px glyph) — amber `warning` for Degraded, `--signal-red` `error` for a hard fault, amber `link_off` for absent.

**Three glyphs, not one glyph in two colours:** Designer separates the tiers by colour alone, and colour alone fails WCAG 1.4.1 and this project's own §8.3 rule.

**(2) One** Radzen notification, **once per session, on escalation only** — *“Knob settings couldn't be applied. The knobs still work, but they may feel wrong.”* / *“Knob safety settings couldn't be applied. Volume is limited until this is fixed.”* The latch is monotonic and **never resets**, so `Degraded → Configured → Degraded` speaks **once**; ten literal tests prove every row of the table.

**A healthy boot stays completely silent** — no toast, no splash, no banner. Also adds the `ConfigStatusChanged` event and its SignalR broadcast (**the tier only** — the field detail stays on `ENC-8`'s page, which is the same `UI-2` load argument answering a different question), **resets the tier to `Unknown` on disconnect** (today an unplugged device still reports `Configured`, and the same value drives the volume clamp), and finally consumes `ENC-0`'s `WasEverConnected` flag, which nothing has used since it shipped.

⛔ **Adds no markup to the Settings page and no endpoint to `IntegrationsController`** — the punch list attributes the `Sent` vs `Read back` table and its buttons to this row; they are `ENC-8`'s, decided once in the joint planning pass.

**11 tasks, 5 phases, 15-step browser Test Plan.**
