### PHN-1a — ADR-029 PR 1 — the event-playback contracts.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ |
| Plan | [`design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md`](../design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) D1/D2/D4 · [PHN arc PR breakdown](../design/plans/PHN-arc-pr-breakdown.md) PR 1 · [punch list §3.5 `PHN-1`, §2 `O6`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [phone/audio handoff](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-phone-console-audio-and-canned-replies.md) |
| Depends on | [#528](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/528) · **None — was the head of the arc.** `O6` makes it the predecessor of `PHN-2`; PRs 2-7 of the breakdown all sit behind it and are **not yet planned**. |
| Branch | `feat/phn-1a-event-playback` |

**ADR-029 PR 1 — the event-playback contracts.** 🔴 **P0, `O6`, and the head of the seven-PR phone arc.** A new **`IEventPlaybackService` in `Radio.Core`, sitting BESIDE `IAnnouncementService` rather than inside it** — the announcement service is fire-and-forget *by signature* (bare `Task`, no identity, one global `StopAsync`) and has two callers that depend on exactly that; the split it encodes is **attended vs unattended**, which is what PR 4 and PR 5 branch on. Plus the **closed discriminated `EventPlaybackRequest` with asymmetric arms** — speech carries literal text, voicemail carries a `(kind, id, duration)` **reference** — and the `EventPlaybackSnapshot` anchor (position + broadcast time + state; **no position tick**, ADR §8.2). Plus `IEventAudioSource` gaining `Position` / `IsSeekable` / `SeekAsync` / `PauseAsync` / `ResumeAsync`, copied verbatim from `IPrimaryAudioSource` and implemented on `AudioFileEventSource` and `TTSEventSource`.

⚠ **NEVER a caller-supplied URL** — that is an SSRF primitive, and "it's a LAN kiosk" is not a defence. Pinned two ways: `Validate` rejects a URL-bearing `MediaId` with a named reason, and a reflection test asserts the request type declares **no** URL-shaped property at all.

⚠ **PLANNING FOUND THE ADR WRONG ON ITS OWN LOW-RISK CLAIM, and it adds a task.** ADR §8.3 says D4 is "a lift" because `FilePlayerAudioSource` "already implements seeking through `SoundFlowPlaybackService`".

**`SoundFlowPlaybackService` has no seek method at all**, and `FilePlayerAudioSource.SeekCoreAsync` (`:910-922`) assigns `_position = position` and returns — it moves a field, not any audio, under an `IsSeekable => true` at `:119`. That is a **fourth** instance of the `CLAUDE.md` § Pre-Merge Review comment-accuracy class; a **fifth** is `SoundFlowPlaybackService.GetPosition` returning `TimeSpan.Zero` behind the comment *"Position tracking not available in current SoundFlow API"* — false: `ISoundPlayer.Time` exists in the restored SoundFlow 1.4.1, along with `bool Seek(TimeSpan, SeekOrigin)`.

**So PR 1 builds the primitive (Task 4) instead of lifting one; `GetPosition` has zero callers, so the fix carries no blast radius.** `FilePlayerAudioSource` is **NOT** fixed here — live primary path, own UAT, logged to `FUTURE-WORK` instead.

⚠ **Two completion traps the ADR half-missed:** a seek OR a pause makes `AudioFileEventSource`'s wall-clock `Task.Delay(_duration)` (`:205`) fire at the wrong time (ADR §14 Q4 caught only the seek half), and pausing a `TTSEventSource` would make its monitor loop read `!IsPlaying(Id)` as **natural completion** (`:169-175`) — no document mentions this one. Both fixed by re-arming on transport events; **no poll and no timer is added** (ADR §1.3).

⛔ **Do NOT use `POST /api/sources/events/{tts,file}` as a template** — re-verified live at the cited lines: `_duckingService` taken at `:44` and never read, `mixer.AddSource` at `:651` never removed or disposed, `PlayFileEvent` double-plays via `:719` then `:732`. 🎁 **Ships a gift to PR 4:** `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests` pins that ducking is **binary and transition-only today**, so PR 4's change to it lands as an edited test in PR 4's own diff rather than as a silent shift inside a shared audio service.

### OPS-5 — Stale static assets on the kiosk — `Cache-Control` was absent entirely.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ |
| Plan | _no plan — small operational fix, scoped in this row_ |
| Spec / handoff | `CLAUDE.md` § Deployment ("Verifying a deploy actually landed") |
| Depends on | — |
| Branch | `fix/static-asset-cache-control` |

**Stale static assets on the kiosk — `Cache-Control` was absent entirely.** **This is the hole `OPS-1` left in deploy verification, and it silently invalidated any UAT of a styling change.** `Radio.Web`'s `UseStaticFiles()` sent `ETag` and `Last-Modified` but **no `Cache-Control` at all**, so browsers applied heuristic freshness (RFC 9111 §4.2.2) and reused `css/`, `js/`, fonts and the Radzen theme **without revalidating** — the server offered a cheap way to check and never told the client to use it. Measured 2026-09-02 while UAT'ing `ENC-4c`: the kiosk painted a `design-system.css` of 775 rules missing the whole `ENC-4` block, i.e. a copy predating a deploy earlier the same day, and those measurements only became valid after a forced reload.

**A CSS-only change could land, pass the SHA check on both services, and still not be on the panel** — `OPS-1` verifies the git SHA stamped into each *assembly*, a true statement about which binary runs and a silent one about which bytes a browser paints. The kiosk relaunch does not help either: Chrome's HTTP cache lives in the profile directory and outlives the process.

**Fix:** `Cache-Control: no-cache` on everything `UseStaticFiles` serves, via a testable `StaticAssetCaching.CreateOptions()` (7 unit tests). That is *revalidate before reuse*, **not** *don't store* — the existing `ETag` turns it into a `304` with **0 bytes** (measured; vs 193,069 on a full GET).

**Applied uniformly on purpose:** nothing this app links is content-hashed — it is Blazor *Server*, so `_framework/` carries `blazor.web.js` at a fixed path rather than a fingerprinted set, and `_content/Radzen.Blazor/*`, `Radio.Web.styles.css`, `css/*`, `js/*`, `fonts/*` are all stable names (`js/virtual-keyboard.js?v=2` is a *hand-maintained* buster — evidence of someone patching this by hand once). A long `max-age` on any of them is this same bug with a longer fuse; caching hard needs fingerprinting first (`MapStaticAssets` + `@Assets[...]`), a larger change deliberately not taken. `Radio.API` serves no static files and needed nothing. `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1` already deleted the kiosk profile's cache for this reason (since 2026-08-18) — one profile on one box, nothing for a LAN client, nothing under `-NoRestart`; left in place, but no longer what correctness rests on.

**Verified on the appliance:** header present on all 6 asset classes; `If-None-Match` → `304` **carrying the header** (so `OnPrepareResponse` runs on the revalidation path — confirmed, not assumed); and the bug itself closed — a CSS probe reached the kiosk's *computed style* over CDP after a plain `location.reload()` **with no cache clear, no restart and no relaunch**, isolating the header as the sole mechanism.

### PHN-1b — ADR-029 PR 2 — `GvMediaClient`, the bounded cache, and API-side auth.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#534](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/534) |
| Plan | [`design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md`](../design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) D3/D8 · [PHN arc PR breakdown](../design/plans/PHN-arc-pr-breakdown.md) PR 2 · [punch list §3.5 `PHN-1`, §2 `O6`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [`PHN-1a` plan](../design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md) §0.4/§5 — **authority over the ADR wherever they disagree** |
| Depends on | **`PHN-1a`** ✅ [#528](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/528) — merged, so this row is claimable now. Blocks PR 3 (`EventPlaybackService` + `POST /api/audio/events`), which consumes `GvMediaClient` and `GvMediaOptions`. `O6` still governs the arc. |
| Branch | ✅ [#534](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/534) — merged |

**ADR-029 PR 2 — `GvMediaClient`, the bounded cache, and API-side auth.** 🔴 **P0, `O6`, second of the seven-PR phone arc.** A typed `GvMediaClient` in `Radio.Infrastructure/External/` that fetches a voicemail recording **server-side** into a size-bounded LRU disk cache at `./data/gvmedia/`, a `GvMediaAuthHandler` that can carry `X-RotaryPhone-Auth` where a browser `<audio>` element never could, and Radio.API's own `GvMedia` config block.

**No endpoint, no `EventPlaybackService`, nothing that plays a sound** — PR 3 is the first PR a user can reach.

⚠ **The cache is the load-bearing part and must not be reviewed as an optimisation.** GV auth is dead ~9 minutes in every 20 (`XR-3`) and their `/api/gvbridge/status` reports healthy *during* the blackout, so a replay 30 s later has ~45% odds of 502ing without it. Its correctness properties are: a hit never touches the network, eviction **actually deletes**, and `CacheMaxMegabytes = 0` is a **no-cache** path rather than an infinitely-evicting one (ADR ⟨A1·2⟩).

⚠ **PLANNING FOUND THE ADR WRONG ON THE CLAIM THAT SET THIS ROW'S RISK.** ADR §5.1/§10.1 say Radio.API has *"no `AddHttpClient`, no `IHttpClientFactory`, and no `DelegatingHandler` infrastructure at all"* and the breakdown concludes the handler infrastructure is *"genuinely net-new"*. The grep is right about the **directory** and wrong about the **container**: `Program.cs:102` → `AddSoundFlowAudio` → `AudioServiceExtensions.cs:257` `AddPhoneIntegration` (unconditional) → `:469` `AddHttpClient<PhoneContactLookupService>()`, and `Program.cs:107` → `AddRadioWeather` → two more named clients. `IHttpClientFactory` resolves in Radio.API **today**, and `Microsoft.Extensions.Http` is already a package reference. Genuinely net-new is only the handler **type**, which has a 31-line template plus tests in `Radio.Web`.

⚠ **ADR §5's registration instruction walks into the DI hazard this row exists to close** — *"beside its sibling in `AudioServiceExtensions.cs`"*, but that sibling lives inside `AddPhoneIntegration`, reachable only through `AddSoundFlowAudio`, so registering there buries the feature in the audio graph and any test resolving it initialises real audio hardware. That is exactly why `ActiveSourceAccessorRegistrationTests` can only inspect descriptors, and therefore exactly why **nothing in this repo would catch a missing registration today** — `ValidateOnBuild`/`ValidateScopes` appear **nowhere** in `src/` or `tests/`.

**Scoping correction:** a standalone `AddGvMedia` following the `AddRadioWeather` precedent, which is what makes this row's **first real build-and-resolve guard** possible.

⚠ **ADR §5.1 tells this row to copy `PhoneContactLookupService`'s masking, and that file leaks on four lines against one** — raw number at `:62` (Information, **with the contact's full name**), `:78`, `:96-97`, and `:102` (Warning, so it still reaches the journal after `LOG-11`); the one masked line still logs `contact.Name` in full.

**A fifth leak nobody had named: exception messages**, which every caller's catch logs. Rule here is *no raw id in a log message, a log argument, or an exception message*, masked as a **hash prefix** (`gvm:1a2b3c4d`) rather than `***last4` — nobody recognises a voicemail id, so a suffix leaks characters for zero operator benefit — pinned by a test that captures every line at every level across success/404/502/timeout/cache-hit. ⚠ **Four decisions PR 1 handed forward are settled here, so they cannot drift again:** `MaxSpeechChars` is **rejection, not truncation** (ADR §4.2 overridden — its own rule is that utterance composition belongs to `Radio.Web`, and a server that speaks less than it was asked while returning 200 is the untruth PR 1 refused when it made `SeekAsync` throw); `IEventAudioSource.SeekAsync` **stays `Task`** (widening breaks D4's verbatim-lift and drags in `FilePlayerAudioSource`, a live primary path — a refused seek is still visible as an anchor that does not move); `Label` gets `MaxLabelChars = 128` in `Validate`, **implemented by PR 3** because it would be unreachable here; and the masking rule above. ⛔ **Do NOT use `POST /api/sources/events/{tts,file}` as a template** — re-verified live at `c830fb8`: `_duckingService` taken at `:44`, never read; `mixer.AddSource` at `:651` never removed or disposed; `PlayFileEvent` double-plays via `:719` then `:732`. ⛔ **Do not touch `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests`** — PR 4's tripwire, all four green. ⚠ **Deploy trap the ADR's D8 table depends on and does not mention:** `src/Radio.API/appsettings.Production.json` **does not exist**, the per-machine file ships from `deploy/<target>/`, and `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:217` excludes it from rsync while `:222` seeds it **only when absent** — so a repo edit to it will **not** reach a box that already has one. Defaults therefore go in `src/Radio.API/appsettings.json` (which *is* overwritten), and flipping `AuthKey` on a live box is a documented hand edit plus a restart.

### ENC-6 — Sleep, wake, and the three-state model — the non-blanking half.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ |
| Plan | [`ENC-6-sleep-wake-state-model.md`](../design/plans/ENC-6-sleep-wake-state-model.md) — 10 tasks |
| Spec / handoff | [punch list §3.5 `ENC-6`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [encoder handoff **Rev 5** §8](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) · [`ENC-15` gate report](uat/2026-09-02-enc15-touch-wake-gate/REPORT.md) — **withdraws the blanking half** |
| Depends on | **✅ met** — `ENC-1` [#498](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/498) and `ENC-4` [#519](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/519) / `ENC-4c` [#526](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/526) all merged. **`ENC-15` was the hard predecessor of the blanking half only, and that half is out of scope.** Not blocked by `ENC-5`/`ENC-7` in either order, but **shares `RotaryEncoderActionRouter.cs` with both** — expect to rebase. |
| Branch | `feat/enc-6-sleep-wake` |

**Sleep, wake, and the three-state model — the non-blanking half.** 🔴 **P0, the last unplanned one, and the row that closes the overnight failure mode.**

⚠ **THE BLANKING HALF IS WITHDRAWN AND MUST NOT BE REINSTATED.** `ENC-15` was its hard predecessor and **its gate FAILED** on 2026-09-02 ([report](uat/2026-09-02-enc15-touch-wake-gate/REPORT.md)): the touchscreen is powered by the panel and **leaves the USB bus when it blanks**, so touch cannot wake it — there is no device left to generate the event — and `cafe:4005` has **zero evdev nodes**, so a knob cannot wake the compositor either. That is one *application-mediated* wake path where two were required, and the investigation **reproduced the brick** (a ~13 s on/off oscillation the documented recovery line could not break).

**So this row builds NO DPMS, no `Ambient-Dark`, no `Standby-Dark`, no re-blank timers and no encoder-presence coupling rules.** Designer Rev 5 §8.5 and §15's first and last *Sleep, wake, blanking* checkboxes are out of scope. If a later session reads “ENC-6” and reaches for blanking, `design/INTEGRATIONS.md` §1 and `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` §7 (Sleep Mode) both carry the reason it stays off.



**What ships — and the five-state model does still cohere as three.** Rule 1 (“if the panel is dark the first input lights it”) governed *only* the two dark states, so it drops out whole; Rule 2 governs all three survivors and §8.3 already specifies both remaining columns completely.

**(1) The headline defect, and it is current behaviour:** idle-at-30-min navigates to `/sleep` **without calling `SleepService`**, so `IsSleeping` stays `false` and the API never learns the clock is up. Fixed by **`Sleep.razor` reporting itself** through a new `POST /api/system/sleep-screen` — which also collapses the finding a Builder confirmed today while shipping `ENC-4`, that **`/sleep` reached by idle and `/sleep` reached by the Sleep pill are different states**.

⚠ **Do NOT “fix” this by making `idle-dimmer.js` call `SetSleepAsync(true)`** — Ambient is *defined* by audio still playing, so that converts it into Standby.

**(2) The wake latch.** `TryWakeFromSleep` calls `WakeAsync` fire-and-forget and returns `true`, so a 10 ms poll discards every following event; a synchronous `TryClaimWake()` makes the state read `Awake` from the claim instant. Under the new model the window is **worse** than the handoff assumed — leaving Ambient needs a *browser round trip*, not just an await. *A fast spin must lose one detent, not twelve.*

**(3) D22, settled scope, not an open question:** from Standby a **turn** lights nothing new and **does not resume audio**; a **press** (any button) or a **screen tap** does. Owner approved Designer's narrowing verbatim — *“that D8 narrowing is fine, keep it.”* **(4) Rule 2:** VOLUME **acts in place** on the lit clock and does not change screens; everything else wakes to the full UI and is consumed.

**(5) The refinement that turns a loss into an answer:** a consumed input still renders that knob's **current value** — *the first detent tells you where you are, the second one moves it* — which is also what stops three knobs looking broken for a whole standby, since D22 makes every turn there consumed. Plus the Standby hint, closing the `TODO(ENC-6)` `Sleep.razor:113-116` has carried since `ENC-4`.



⚠ **Three things a reviewer should check rather than assume.** **(a)** This row **does not depend on `ENC-5`/`ENC-7` and is not blocked by them, in either order** — the gate runs *before* the dispatch tables and the only index it compares is `0`, which is VOLUME under both mappings (already pinned by `EncoderIndexZero_IsVolume_UnderBothTheOldAndTheNewPhysicalOrder`); the consumed-value readout is a **fourth parallel array beside the two `ENC-5` Task 7 already reorders**, index threaded in so no literal is left behind. If `ENC-5` lands first, re-read the router — its handlers gain a leading `int index` and **the code wins**.

**(b)** Two shipped assertions **invert and must be updated, not deleted**: `WakeConsumesThePressEdge_…`'s `Assert.Empty(h.Hud.Published)` (a consumed input now publishes, by design) and `SleepService`'s class doc, which claims DPMS control the code has never performed — a **fourth** comment/code mismatch of the class `CLAUDE.md`'s pre-merge rule exists for.

**(c)** `dotnet test` **cannot** cover the new HTTP round trip: `AddHermeticTestRig` fails every outbound call by design, so a null result is the *expected* state — exactly how `ENC-8` shipped a page that could not deserialize its own response with a green suite.

**UAT scenario B is the only gate on it, and the plan's Test Plan exercises both `/sleep` entry paths separately** because a run through the pill produces a false pass for the idle path.



📋 **PLANNED 2026-09-02** — [`design/plans/ENC-6-sleep-wake-state-model.md`](../design/plans/ENC-6-sleep-wake-state-model.md), **10 tasks** across 4 phases, **2 declared deviations** (§0.4) and **3 mechanism decisions** the handoff does not make (§0.3).



✅ **SHIPPED 2026-09-02** — [#539](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/539). Three states derived from `IsSleeping` + a new `IsSleepScreenVisible` the `/sleep` page reports about itself; the sleep gate, the `TryClaimWake()` latch, D22, the consumed-value readout and the Standby hint all landed.

**Blanking did not ship and must not be reinstated.** ⚠ **Three things the plan got wrong, all verified against the tree:** (1) its `_currentValuePublishers` literal was the **pre-`ENC-5`** order and would have put a TUNING readout on the SOURCE band — *the exact defect its own four-array test exists to catch* — and (2) **that test would not have caught it**, because it asserted only `card.EncoderIndex`, which every publisher threads through from its own parameter; it now pins the label per index. (3) `SystemControllerTests` is an HTTP integration class, so the plan's controller-construction helpers do not exist; Task 3's facts were rewritten over real HTTP and assert the **raw wire text** `"wakeState":"Ambient"`, which catches the enum-as-number regression a typed read would accept.

**Also: "0 warnings" was never true here** — `main` carries 53 pre-existing `IDE0011`; the set is byte-identical before and after.

⚠ **Open follow-up, recorded in `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` §7 rather than fixed:** `IsSleepScreenVisible` is in-memory, so an API restart while the kiosk is parked on `/sleep` reinstates this row's headline defect until something re-renders — and every deploy restarts `radio-api`. `SetSleepScreenVisible` was made **edge-triggered** here so the re-report heartbeat that closes it cannot wipe an in-flight wake claim.

### TEST-4 — The BT capture watchdog's flaky test — a wall-clock test window racing an independent wall-clock poll loop.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ |
| Plan | — _(Builder-discovered; no Planner spec — the diagnosis is in this row and in the test file's class-level `<remarks>`)_ |
| Spec / handoff | `CLAUDE.md` → **Test Timing** · `tests/Radio.Infrastructure.Tests/Audio/Services/BluetoothCaptureWatchdogTests.cs` `<remarks>` |
| Depends on | — _(no dependency; test-only, touches no production file)_ |
| Branch | `fix/flaky-bt-capture-watchdog-test` |

**The BT capture watchdog's flaky test — a wall-clock test window racing an independent wall-clock poll loop.** `BluetoothCaptureWatchdogTests.IntermittentStall_ResetsCounter` failed `Build and Test` on `main` at `2a81f56` and passed on `c3b62d8` with no change to the file: timing-sensitive, not a regression.

⚠ **"It passed on the retry" was not the resolution** — per `TEST-1`/`TEST-3` and criterion (d), a randomly-red suite trains everyone to re-run rather than read, and five Builders were merging into `main` the day it surfaced.

**The root cause is entirely in the test; the production watchdog is correct** and was not changed — which matters, because `AUD-8`'s watchdog guards a real long-uptime capture failure and this repo has a documented long-running capture-lifecycle bug. The test sequenced three phases with `await Task.Delay(60)` while `BluetoothCaptureWatchdog` polled the snapshot source on its own independent `Task.Delay(20)` tick, **with no rendezvous between the two clocks**. Nothing guaranteed the watchdog ever observed the middle, below-threshold phase — the one whose entire job is to reset `_consecutiveStalledChecks`.

**Reproduced at 13/200 under 64 concurrent CPU burners on a 32-core host**, and the poll log names the mechanism exactly: the whole phase-2 window (`t=60ms → t=125ms`) elapsed **without a single poll** — ticks stretched to 42–91 ms, the reset value was set and replaced unobserved, and phase 1's two stalled polls concatenated with phase 3's three into the five consecutive checks that then **correctly** fired. Given the polls it actually received, raising was the right behaviour.

**Fixed test-only.** The fake `ICaptureStreamSnapshotSource` is now a **rendezvous gate**: the watchdog parks on entry to every `GetCaptureStreamSnapshot()` until the test grants that poll, so a test states how many polls observe each value and every assertion runs while the watchdog is frozen before its next read. Polls are counted, not timed; `WatchdogTickIntervalMs` now affects only how fast the tests run, never whether they pass.

**Verified 200/200 at the same 64-burner load that produced the 13/200 failure rate** — the apples-to-apples comparison — plus 200/200 and 360/360 (all six tests) at 40 burners: **760 executions under load, zero failures**, against load severe enough to slow each iteration ~12x.

⚠ **No box UAT is owed and none was run** — this is test infrastructure with no user-visible surface; `build.yml` is the gate.

**Sibling tests:** three shared the shape but only in the *safe* direction (starvation could only remove polls, never flip an assertion red); they are now exact rather than "at least" assertions.

**Two further defects found and fixed while there.** (1) `NullSnapshotSource_ExitsImmediatelyWithoutRaising` documented that it *"verifies the watchdog idles (never enters its main loop)"* while asserting only `ConsecutiveStalledChecksForTest == 0` — which **reads 0 on both paths**, because a null snapshot resets the counter every tick. The doc claimed a verification the assertion did not perform: a sixth instance of the `CLAUDE.md` comment-overclaim class. It now asserts `ExecuteTask` reaches `RanToCompletion`, which is what actually distinguishes the early return from the loop. (2) `AboveThreshold_RaisesAfterConsecutiveChecks` asserted `RaiseCount >= 1`; it now pins the exact count, the one-short boundary, and the post-fire reset.

**The pattern is recorded in `CLAUDE.md` → "Test Timing — never race a wall clock against a wall clock"**, because it is not a one-off: the same shape is live in `Radio.Web.Tests` (see the `NowPlayingPanel` fast-follow below).

### PHN-5 — WIDENED BY OWNER DECISION 2026-09-05, AND PLANNED — read this before the original text below, which describes the four-site version.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#580](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/580) |
| Plan | [`PHN-5-phone-pii-out-of-the-logs.md`](../design/plans/PHN-5-phone-pii-out-of-the-logs.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) §5.1 (the citation that propagates it) · [`PHN-1b` plan](../design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md) §0.3 ⓸ (the count, and the masking rule that replaced it) · `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` |
| Depends on | **None.** Independent of the ADR-029 arc — `PHN-1b` only *found* it. Best done before `PhoneIntegration:Enabled` is ever flipped. |
| Branch | `fix/phn-5-phone-pii-out-of-the-logs` |

⭐ **WIDENED BY OWNER DECISION 2026-09-05, AND PLANNED — read this before the original text below, which describes the four-site version.** The owner ruled the row covers **every site that logs a phone number or a real contact name**, not just `PhoneContactLookupService`. That is **eleven sites across six files and three assemblies** — the owner's list of ten, plus `src/Radio.API/Services/PhoneCallIntegrationService.cs:127`, which logs `$"Incoming call from {callerName}"` at Information where `callerName` **falls back to the raw number**, and whose *identical string* is masked nine lines later by the callee (`AnnouncementService.cs:97-98`).

⚠⚠ **TWO OF THE ROW'S STATED PREMISES DID NOT SURVIVE VERIFICATION, and both make the row WORSE than filed.**

**(1) `LOG-11`'s “the journal carries Warning and above” is true of `Radio.API` ONLY.** The API adds its console sink in code with `restrictedToMinimumLevel: Warning` (`Radio.API/Program.cs:48-53`); **`Radio.Web`'s console sink has no restriction at all** and its `MinimumLevel.Default` is `Information` — so under systemd every `Radio.Web` Information line reaches `journalctl -u radio-web`.

**(2) The row's “latent until `PhoneIntegration:Enabled` flips” is FALSE for the five `Radio.Web` sites:** that service **never binds `PhoneIntegration` at all**, and `PhoneHubService.StartAsync()` is called unconditionally at `Radio.Web/Program.cs:652-653`.

**So `PhoneHubService.cs:82` — a raw phone number at Information — is written to journald on every incoming call, on a stock box, today**, and `GvTrunkApiService.cs:94` (Error) with it. The row is live, not latent.

⭐ **The fix reuses `LogSafeText` rather than minting a second idiom** — one new method, `ForPhone` → `phn:{8 hex}` over the **normalised** number (hashing the raw string would give one caller three tokens and destroy the correlation the mask exists to buy), and **contact names are DELETED rather than tokenised**, per `LogSafeText`'s own rule that where enumeration is the threat you log nothing.

⚠ **This REVERSES `TTS-11` §6.1's stated expectation** that these sites would keep the local `***{last4}` idiom; plan §1.2 argues it and §7.3 names what would falsify it — it is the one decision in the row worth the owner's eye. ⭐ **The durable deliverable is the lint rule:** `LogSafetyLintTests.cs:62-67` deliberately does **not** enforce `phoneNumber` because *“a rule over it would be all exemption and no coverage”* — this row removes the exemptions and turns the rule on. ⚠ **Two defects found in that same test file and fixed here:** its `SafeCall` regex does not match `ForPhone`, so the rule would flag every FIXED site; and **`LogSafetyLintTests` FAILS in any checkout whose path contains a `worktrees` segment** — **measured**, not reasoned, and unrelated to PII (plan `C-100`, §6.5 offers it as a separate row if preferred). ⛔ **Deliberately NOT fixed, and filed instead:** the `PhoneIntegration:Enabled` asymmetry (verified — **exactly one reader in all of `src/`**, `PhoneCallIntegrationService.cs:44`) and `IntegrationsController.cs:215-223` reporting `Enabled = true` unconditionally. Both are behavioural changes and belong to the owner (plan §6.1). **Est. 1 d**, as scoped. ⬇ **THE ORIGINAL ROW TEXT FOLLOWS, unedited:** **`PhoneContactLookupService` logs raw phone numbers on four lines and the contact's real name on the one line it calls masked.** ⚠ **This is a PII-in-logs defect, not a style issue** — filed by `PHN-1b` rather than fixed there, because it is a different subsystem with its own callers and deserves its own reviewable change. Verified against the tree, `src/Radio.Infrastructure/External/PhoneContactLookupService.cs`: **`:62`** `LogInformation("PBAP contact found: {Number} → {Name}", phoneNumber, contact.DisplayName)` — raw number **and the contact's real name**, at **Information**, which since `LOG-11` persists to the file sink at `/opt/radio-console/logs/radio-*.txt`; **`:78`** `LogDebug("Looking up contact for {PhoneNumber}", phoneNumber)` — raw; **`:96-97`** `LogDebug("Contact lookup returned {StatusCode} for {PhoneNumber}", …, phoneNumber)` — raw; **`:102`** `LogWarning(ex, "Contact lookup failed for {PhoneNumber}", phoneNumber)` — raw, at **Warning**, so it is one of the few that still reaches `journalctl` after `LOG-11`, **and it logs `ex`**, so any exception whose `Message` carries the number leaks a second way. The single line the file treats as masked, **`:90`**, masks the number to `***1234` but still logs **`contact.Name` in full** — and ADR-029 §5.1 asks for *"voicemail ids **and caller identity**"* to be masked. So the count is **four raw-number sites against one masked, and the masked one leaks the name anyway.**

⚠ **ADR-029 §5.1 cites this exact file as the masking *example* to copy**, which is how the pattern propagates; `PHN-1b` refused the citation and masked as a hash prefix on every line instead, pinned by a test.

**Exposure, stated honestly rather than inflated:** the only caller of `FindCallerNameAsync` is `Radio.API/Services/PhoneCallIntegrationService.cs:121`, whose `ExecuteAsync` returns at `:44` when `PhoneIntegration:Enabled` is false — and it is false and has never been true. So this is **latent today and becomes live the moment that flag flips**, which is a one-line config change with no code review between it and the leak.

⚠ **A second, smaller instance for the same Planner pass to scope in:** `src/Radio.Web/Services/ContactResolutionService.cs:173` logs a raw number at **Debug** and is **not gated at all** — it is injected into `PhoneMessagesPanel.razor`, the live `/phone` texts surface. Debug is normally below the configured minimum level, so it likely does not emit today; that is a level away from leaking, not a design that prevents it.

**Fix shape (~15 lines):** compute the masked form once at method entry and use only it; mask `contact.Name`/`DisplayName` the same way; do not pass `ex` where its message can carry the number.

### PHN-1c — ADR-029 PR 3 — `EventPlaybackService` and the `/api/audio/events` route family.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#556](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/556) |
| Plan | [`design/plans/PHN-1c-event-playback-service-and-route.md`](../design/plans/PHN-1c-event-playback-service-and-route.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) D1/D2/D3 + §14 Q10 · [PHN arc PR breakdown](../design/plans/PHN-arc-pr-breakdown.md) PR 3 · [punch list §3.5 `PHN-1`, §2 `O6`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [`PHN-1a` plan](../design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md) §0.4/§5 and [`PHN-1b` plan](../design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md) §0.3/§0.4/§5 — **authority over the ADR wherever they disagree; this plan's §0.4 C-21…C-33 is authority over both wherever it contradicts them, and it does in three places** |
| Depends on | **`PHN-1a`** ✅ [#528](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/528) and **`PHN-1b`** ✅ [#534](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/534) — both merged, so this row is claimable now. Blocks PR 4 (priority becomes load-bearing), PR 5 (server-owned state + the three stop conditions) and PR 6 (`PHN-2`, retire the `<audio>` element). `O6` still governs the arc. |
| Branch | `feat/phn-1c-event-playback-service` |

**ADR-029 PR 3 — `EventPlaybackService` and the `/api/audio/events` route family.**

🔴 **P0, `O6`, third of the seven-PR phone arc, and the first PR a user can reach.** Implements `IEventPlaybackService`: one in-flight attended playback, a server-minted `playbackId`, a lifecycle that ends exactly once, and ADR §3.3's six routes. Both arms land here — `RemoteMedia` fetches through `GvMediaClient` into the bounded cache and plays the local file as an ordinary file event; `Speech` synthesises through `ITTSFactory` with the engine resolved explicitly.

**No `Radio.Web` change, no `/hubs/audio` broadcast, no ducking change, no config key.**

⚠ **THE SHAPE IS `202` + BACKGROUND ACQUISITION, AND FOUR DECISIONS FOLLOW FROM IT.** PR 1's shipped `StartAsync` doc says it returns at `Preparing` and ADR §3.3 says `202`, so acquisition outlives the response: (1) the acquisition token **must not** be `HttpContext.RequestAborted`; (2) a fetch failure is a `FailureReason` on a `Failed` snapshot, not a status code; (3) `Preparing` must be bounded; (4) no exception may escape the background task.

⚠ **`GvMediaCache.WriteAsync`'s remark says PR 3 passes `HttpContext.RequestAborted`. It must not, and the remark is corrected in this PR (Task 11).** `RequestAborted` is request-scoped on a pooled, reset context — binding acquisition to it would cancel **every** fetch the instant it was accepted, so the RemoteMedia arm would fail 100% of the time looking like a network fault. PR 2's staging fix is still right and still load-bearing; only its stated *trigger* was wrong. The cancellation that actually exists is `StopAsync`, a replacing `StartAsync`, and disposal — all three land mid-`WriteAsync`.

⚠ **PLANNING SETTLED PR 2'S DEFERRED GVBRIDGE QUESTION BY READING THEIR SOURCE, AND FOUND A BUG THAT INVERTS A HANDOFF INSTRUCTION.** `D:\prj\RotaryPhone\...\GvVoicemailController.cs`: the audio route is `PhysicalFileResult("audio/mpeg")` with `Content-Length` present on the whole-body GET this client issues, and answers `200`/`404`/`502`, plus `401` once `GvBridgeAuthMiddleware`'s default-off gate is configured — so PR 2's size bound and auth handler are both correct.

**But `GetAudio` resolves through `FindNodeAsync`, which never checks `ListVoicemailsAsync`'s `Succeeded` flag** — unlike the sibling `GetList`, which guards it and returns 502 with a comment saying why. A failed authenticated list returns an **empty** item set, so `FirstOrDefault` yields null and **the route answers `404 "has no recording"` during a GV auth blackout** — ~9 minutes in every 20. Both handoffs say map `NotFound` → 404 *"retrying will not help"*, and `IsPermanent` says the same: **wrong ~45% of the time**, telling a user a voicemail is permanently gone when it will play in a minute. That is the `GV-6`/`GV-8` class arriving through an unwatched door.

**Fix: `IsPermanent` becomes `Disabled`-only** (Task 10), the name `MediaNotFound` survives so nothing is collapsed, and the cross-repo ask is written out for the owner rather than filed here.

⚠ **`TTSOptions.GenerationTimeoutSeconds` has never had a reader** — declaration, a Web DTO twin and a `RadzenNumeric` the owner can set, and nothing in `src/Radio.Infrastructure`. So synthesis is unbounded and a hang would park the seam in `Preparing` forever; PR 3 gives the knob its first reader by bounding its own acquisition, without touching `TTSFactory`.

⚠ **The `TTSParameters` trap was four fields wide when this row was written; `TTS-9` closed two of them, and it is now TWO.** `Engine` and `Voice` are nullable as of [#548](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/548) and fall back to configuration correctly; `Speed` and `Pitch` are still non-nullable with `1.0f` initializers, so any non-null `TTSParameters` silently pins them to the type's default rather than to `TTS:DefaultSpeed` / `DefaultPitch`. All four are filled explicitly anyway — passing `null` would be correct only until a `VoiceId` is set, which is the trap re-armed.

⚠ **The pin only has teeth because its fixture configures NON-default speed and pitch:** the shipped `1.0f` defaults are identical to the type's own initializers, so the plan's original fixture would have passed against the exact bug it exists to catch.

⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-04 by the `PHN-1c` Builder cycle — the security item this row carried is DISCHARGED, and the instruction it gave is withdrawn.** This row previously said `TTSFactory.GenerateESpeakAsync` interpolates the caller's voice into a process argument line, that `espeak-ng -w <path>` is an arbitrary file write, and that PR 3 should **file the live one in `design/FUTURE-WORK.md`**.

**All of that predates `TTS-9`** ([#548](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/548)), which removed eSpeak entirely: `GenerateESpeakAsync` no longer exists, and punch-list `SEC-4` is ✅ closed.

**Nothing was filed and nothing should be** — filing a hole that does not exist is how a future session spends a cycle looking for it.

⚠ **The `VoiceId` allow-list still shipped, on different and still-valid grounds:** it bounds this seam's own new caller-supplied string to `[A-Za-z0-9._~+-]`, 64 chars, which is a strict subset of any known-voice set, so it pre-empts nothing. The surviving **class** question — allow-listing against the engines' known voices — is punch-list **`SEC-5`** and belongs to Planner.

⚠ **The `TTSParameters` trap is TWO fields wide, not four:** `TTS-9` made `Engine` and `Voice` nullable, so only `Speed` and `Pitch` still pin to the type's `1.0f` initializers. Recorded in `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` § *TTS seam* item 1.

⚠ **A stop after natural completion raises `PlaybackCompleted` TWICE** — both sources raise from their monitor *and* from `StopCoreAsync`, and `AudioSourceBase.StopAsync` short-circuits only on `Created`/`Disposed`. `AnnouncementService` is immune by accident (`TrySetResult`); a state machine is not, so terminal transition is once-only via `Interlocked`.

⚠ **`AudioFileEventSourceFactory.CreateFromFileAsync` cannot be used for a cached recording** — it re-roots relative paths against `FilePlayer:RootDirectory` (`/mnt/nas/music` on the box) and estimates the duration ADR §4.1 says to pass through. One new overload; that is also what keeps `SoundFlowPlaybackService` out of the service's constructor and makes the DI guard buildable.

⚠ **`ADR §14 Q10` answered: the speech pre-flight is DROPPED**, synthesis is the only gate — on the deployed config an unsubstituted `${secret:` tag advertises Google as *available* anyway, so the pre-flight would miss the actual misconfiguration while blocking a fixed engine until restart.

⚠ **A `Speech` snapshot's position is always zero** (`TTSEventSource` never overrides `Position`) — pinned as-is, not faked, handed to PR 5.

⛔ **Do NOT use `POST /api/sources/events/{tts,file}` as a template** — all three defects still live at `d1226da`.

**`AnnouncementService` is the template, and the one line not to copy is that it awaits playback inside the call.**

⛔ **No `mixer.AddSource`** — it does not route audio, and never removing it is where `SourcesController` leaks.

⛔ **Do not touch `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests`** (PR 4's tripwire) or `INTEGRATIONS.md:566` (still true today; PR 4 makes it false).

**Verification that can actually fail:** a `CustomWebApplicationFactory` test that turns `GvMedia:Enabled` on, points `BaseUrl` at `127.0.0.1:1`, and asserts the playback reaches `Failed`/`MediaTransport` through `GET /current` — route → validate → 202 → background fetch → auth handler → taxonomy → snapshot, end to end, with no network and no GV clock. It also closes PR 2's carried *"does `radio-api` still start"*.

**`./data/gvmedia` writability is RE-CARRIED to PR 6**, not claimed: `GvMedia:Enabled` ships `false` and PR 3 does not flip it, so the first real fetch is still PR 6's.

### ENC-20 — Three knob-feel defects the owner found with his own hand on the panel.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ |
| Plan | _(no plan — owner-reported defects, specified in the dispatch)_ |
| Spec / handoff | [encoder handoff **Rev 8** §5.2 · §5.4 · §7.6 · §15](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) · [punch list `ENC-3`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) |
| Depends on | **✅ met** — `ENC-11` [#509](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/509), `ENC-16`, `ENC-4` [#519](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/519) and `ENC-17` [#553](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/553) all merged |
| Branch | `fix/enc-20-knob-feel` |

**Three knob-feel defects the owner found with his own hand on the panel.** ⭐ **The first findings from real knob use, and the reason they exist is that `ENC-17` proves *mechanism* while *feel* is only reachable by turning the thing.** Shipped as one PR because they are one user-facing complaint ("the knobs don't feel right yet").

**(1) One detent moved volume 4 points; it now moves 1.** VOLUME ran `step_size 2` while the host applied `VolumeStepPercent 2`, so the device's *units* were multiplied into *points* twice over. Now `step_size 1` / `VolumeStepPercent 1`, tiers re-cut `(150ms x2), (80ms x4)`, `VolumeClamp` 6 -> 4. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;⚠ **And the safety argument's arithmetic was wrong, in the file whose entire job is to justify a safety decision.** `RotaryEncoderConfigDefaults` claimed *"a maximum slew of 6 points per 80 ms"* and *"silence to full takes at least 1.33 seconds"*. The real figures were **12 points** and **0.67 s** — and 0.33 s at the fastest rate tier 2 actually fires at, because **a tier threshold is a maximum interval, not the rate the user turns at**. The claim was not a typo: handoff §5.4's table computes points as `multiplier x 2`, correct only at `step_size 1`, and **`ENC-11` ([#509](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/509)) shipped `step: 2` while inheriting that justification**. Corrected in the code, the handoff (Rev 8), the punch list, `INTEGRATIONS.md` and the harness. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;**(2) Knobs did not wake the dimmed screen.** `idle-dimmer.js` undims on `pointerdown`/`keydown`/`wheel`/`pointermove`; encoder input is a **SignalR server push and never becomes a DOM event**, so a knob turn neither undimmed nor reset the idle timers. `MainLayout` now wakes on `EncoderHudService.StateChanged`.

⚠ **Not an `ENC-6` regression** — that row shipped the `/sleep` three-state model at the 30-minute boundary; this is the separate 5-minute dim. The input is **not consumed** (unlike ENC-6 Rule 1), and the two stay separate **by construction**: `Sleep.razor` is on `EmptyLayout`, so `MainLayout` is not mounted on `/sleep`. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;**(3) The change tile vanished too fast.** `HudHoldMs` 1500 -> 2500 — the tail after the hand stops, not the lifetime, since the timer re-arms on every detent. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;⭐ **`ENC-17`'s first real job, and it paid for itself:** the harness measured **4 points/detent and a 12-point clamp before, 1 and 4 after** — the gap this whole arc was trying to close, measured rather than reasoned about. It also caught that the harness's own `turn()` docstring claimed *"the designed config uses step_size 1"* while VOLUME ran 2, which is **why the harness had under-reported the real knob all along**. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;📋 **QUEUED 2026-09-03** — owner-reported; specified in the Builder dispatch rather than by a Planner plan. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;✅ **SHIPPED 2026-09-03** — [#555](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/555). Measured 4 points/detent → 1, clamp 12 points → 4, HUD dwell 2500 ms, and all four knobs wake the dimmed panel.

### PHN-1d — ADR-029 PR 4 — priority becomes load-bearing.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#558](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/558) |
| Plan | [`design/plans/PHN-1d-ducking-priority-load-bearing.md`](../design/plans/PHN-1d-ducking-priority-load-bearing.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) **D5** §6.1/§6.2/§6.3 · [PHN arc PR breakdown](../design/plans/PHN-arc-pr-breakdown.md) PR 4 · [punch list §3.5 `PHN-1`, §2 `O6`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [`PHN-1a`](../design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md) §0.4/§5, [`PHN-1b`](../design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md) §0.3/§0.4/§5 and [`PHN-1c`](../design/plans/PHN-1c-event-playback-service-and-route.md) §0.4/§5 — **authority over the ADR wherever they disagree; this plan's §0.4 C-34…C-46 is authority over all of them, and it is in five places — plus **C-46, the owner decision of 2026-09-04 — ✅ now FILED as [`D28`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) in punch list §7 (*Closed 2026-09-04 by the owner*), so this row no longer carries an unfiled action. The plan proposed `D27`; that number was already taken by the `prefers-reduced-motion` decision of 2026-09-02 and the collision was caught before it was written** |
| Depends on | **✅ MET — `PHN-1c` merged 2026-09-04** as [#556](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/556), merge commit `b77ffe12`. **This row is CLAIMABLE NOW, not pending.** The distinction is worth spelling out rather than leaving a reader to infer it from a ✅ further up the table: the plan was written while PR 3 was still in flight, so its own text speaks of the dependency in the present tense — that text is historical, and the dependency is closed. **Builder's first action is still §0.3's re-check list**, which is now possible to run for real: re-grep its twelve assertions against merged `main` and answer them in the PR description. Blocks PR 5 (server-owned state + the three stop conditions) and PR 6 (`PHN-2`, retire the `<audio>` element). `O6` still governs the arc. |
| Branch | `feat/phn-1d-priority-load-bearing` |

**ADR-029 PR 4 — priority becomes load-bearing.** 🔴 **P0, `O6`, fourth of the seven-PR phone arc, and the one to review hardest.** `DuckingService.StartDuckingAsync` starts raising `DuckingStateChanged` for **every source that joins the ducking set**, not only the one that causes the fade; `EventPlaybackService` subscribes and **stops attended playback when a source starts at or above `GvMedia:PreemptAtPriority` (8)** — the key's first reader in its life.

**This is the first load-bearing use of priority in this system**: `GetPriority` has been read in exactly one place, inside `GetActiveEventsByPriority`, which has had zero non-test callers since it was written. This PR gives it its first.

**No `Radio.Web` change, no route change, no broadcast, no duration cap, no config key** — `PreemptAtPriority` already ships in `GvMediaOptions.cs` and in `src/Radio.API/appsettings.json`.

⚠ **FIVE THINGS THAT MAKE THIS SHARP, ALL AUDIBLE RATHER THAN TEST-VISIBLE.** (1) **`GetPriority` answers `DefaultEventPriority` (8) for any event source it has not been told about, and `StopDuckingAsync` deletes what it was told** — so a priority resolved one moment too late reads 8, and every *ending* announcement would stop the voicemail. The handler ignores `IsDucking:false` and reads the priority **synchronously on the raising thread**. (2) **The raise is synchronous and the handler runs inside `AnnouncementService`'s call**, mid-announcement, from `POST /api/notifications/announce` — and `StopAsync` takes a gate `EventPlaybackService` already holds whenever the raise came out of `TearDownAsync`. The stop is **dispatched**, exactly as `OnSourceCompleted` is, for exactly the same reason. (3) ⚠ **The worst outcome available in this PR: a teardown landing between `StartDuckingAsync` and `PlayAsync` produces audio with no `playbackId`** — no route, no chip and no later preemption can address it, so it plays to the end over the announcement that preempted it. The acquisition tail now runs **under `_gate`**, which `TearDownAsync` also holds, making the two mutually exclusive; and a cancelled acquisition **disposes what it acquired**, which PR 3 leaked. (4) **Self-preemption:** `EventPlaybackRequest.Priority` accepts 1-10 and the attended source's own start now raises — so an identity check by reference is what stops a playback posted at `Priority` 8 from stopping itself the instant it starts. (5) ⭐ **OWNER DECISION 2026-09-04 — the mirror direction is deferred, and deliberately.** D5 rule 2 is symmetric: pressing play while a ≥ 8 source is *already* sounding should not add a second voice either. Three options went to the owner; he rejected mixing, **rejected refusing** (*"press play, get an error, nothing happens"* is the punch list's tier (b) shape) and chose **queueing** — wait for the blocker, then play.

**That lands in PR 5, not here**, on his own reasoning turned on the queue: a waiting state nobody can see is the same complaint for longer, and visibility is PR 5's broadcast plus PR 6's chip. Nothing reaches a user in between — `GvMedia:Enabled` is `false` until PR 6 — and what it falls back to is the mixing this system has always done.

**PR 4 pins that behaviour as a characterization test** so PR 5's queue arrives as an edited assertion, exactly as `PHN-1a` did for this row. `ThrowIfAlreadyPreempted`, `PreemptedByPriority` and `EventPlaybackState.Waiting` are **not built here**.

⚠ **PR 5's row must be re-sized**: §5 carries the queue's five open questions, including the awkward one — the wake-up trigger is the `IsDucking:false` raise this PR ignores, and `StopDuckingAsync` raises it only when the ducking set *empties*, so a ≥ 8 source ending while a sub-8 one continues produces no wake.

⚠ **TWO tripwires, not one.** `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests` is the one all three predecessor handoffs name; `DuckingServiceTests.StartDuckingAsync_MultipleEvents_DoesNotDuckAgain` asserts the same property in a file nobody was told to look at.

**Both are updated, neither deleted.** ⚠ **CITE `design/INTEGRATIONS.md`'s ducking correction BY CONTENT, NEVER BY LINE NUMBER — the number has moved twice in two days, and the most recent *correction* was already wrong on the day it was written.** The claim this PR owns is the struck bullet under **§ How Audio Ducking Works**, beginning *"~~Higher priority announcements can interrupt lower priority ones~~ — **this is not true today.**"* Grep that sentence; do not trust a number.

**The history, recorded so it stops being rediscovered:** three merged plans — [`PHN-1a`](../design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md), [`PHN-1b`](../design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md) and [`PHN-1c`](../design/plans/PHN-1c-event-playback-service-and-route.md) — all instruct the reader *"do not touch `INTEGRATIONS.md:566`"*.

**`:566` has never held this claim and does not hold it today: it is a code fence inside the RotaryPhone hub runbook.** The claim was at `:932` before [#556](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/556); that PR's own banner in this file recorded its new position as `:980`, which is **also wrong** — `:980` is inside a Python request example. It is in fact at **`:997`** at `b77ffe12`, and it will move again the next time anything is inserted above it.

**Three successive documents got this number wrong; that is a citation-format defect, not three typos**, which is why the number is dropped here rather than corrected a fourth time.

**And this PR does not make the struck-through claim true as written.** Announcement-versus-announcement still **mixes** (ADR §6.2 rule 3 declines to fix it); what becomes true is exactly *"an event at ≥ 8 stops attended GV playback"*. Restoring the original sentence would replace a true statement with a false one.

⚠ **`PreemptAtPriority` is safe to LOWER and a trap to RAISE**, and only the lowering case is argued in the ADR: because `GetPriority` answers 8 for every source that never claimed a rank, a threshold of 9 or 10 silently exempts almost everything while leaving the feature apparently intact. A test pins the two shipped defaults against each other; the doc line covers the per-machine override a test cannot see.

⚠ **The live consequence, an acceptance rather than a discovery:** with `PhoneIntegration:Enabled` false, **a doorbell posted to `/api/notifications/announce` at its default priority 8 will stop a voicemail mid-play.** Intended (ADR §6.1).

**Est. 2-3 d, unchanged** — C-46 removed work rather than adding it. **Determinism:** this PR adds no timer, so `TimeProvider` is the wrong seam; what is asynchronous is a `Task.Run`, and the plan adds an internal `PreemptionTail` so the **negative** assertion — *"a priority-5 source changed nothing"* — is a rendezvous rather than a sleep.

⚠ **Plan §0.3 is a re-check list, not decoration.** Nine of its twelve assertions were **verified against real `PHN-1c` code** at `56fef797` rather than against plan text — which is how the plan caught that PR 3 ships `PublishNonTerminal`, not the `Publish` its own plan showed, and that substituting one for the other would reintroduce the synchronous-`PlayAsync`-failure bug its remark describes. Three are still open because Builder was mid-file.

**PR 3 was not merged when this was written** — it has since merged at `b77ffe12`, so its pre-merge review may already have moved some of it: Builder's first action is to re-grep the list against merged `main` and answer it in the PR description.

### PHN-1e — ADR-029 PR 5 — server-owned state and the stop conditions that need no client.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#561](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/561) |
| Plan | [`design/plans/PHN-1e-server-owned-state-and-the-queue.md`](../design/plans/PHN-1e-server-owned-state-and-the-queue.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) **D6, D7**, §8.1, §8.2, §14 Q8 |
| Depends on | ✅ **MET — `PHN-1d` merged 2026-09-04** as [#558](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/558), merge commit `a8656c71`. **This row is CLAIMABLE NOW, not pending** — said in those words rather than left to be inferred from a ✅ one row up, which is the convention `PHN-1d`'s own cell established. |
| Branch | `feat/phn-1e-server-owned-state` |

**ADR-029 PR 5 — server-owned state and the stop conditions that need no client.**

🔴 **P0, `O6`, fifth of the phone arc.** `EventPlaybackService.PlaybackChanged` has been raised on every transition since PR 3 and subscribed by **nothing**; this row connects it to `/hubs/audio` as `EventPlaybackChanged`, caches it in `AudioStateStore`, and seeds that cache once from `GET /api/audio/events/current` so a browser arriving mid-playback is not told the room is silent (ADR §8.1 ⟨A1·4⟩). Plus **the max-duration cap** (D7 §7.1), **entering `/sleep` stops attended playback** (D7 §7.5, closing ADR §14 **Q8**), and **the last-circuit-closed `CircuitHandler`** — the first this application has ever had (D7 §7.3).

**No user-visible change:** `GvMedia:Enabled` is `false` until PR 6, and the chip is PR 6's.

**No config key** — `MaxPlaybackSeconds` already ships. ⚠ **THE QUEUE IS NOT IN THIS ROW — see `PHN-1f`, and read the plan's §0.2 before disputing it.**

⚠ **THREE THINGS THAT MAKE THIS SHARP.** (1) ⭐ **The cap is NOT `CancelAfter` on `Playback`'s token, and two merged plans say it is** (`PHN-1c` §5, `PHN-1d` §5). Nothing observes that token once acquisition returns — the last read is `source.PlayAsync(token)`, which starts playback and returns — so a cap built that way would expire silently at 300 s and change nothing: the worst available shape for something the ADR calls *"THE guarantee"*. It is a one-shot `TimeProvider` timer whose callback **dispatches** a stop (plan C-49). (2) ⭐ **SignalR does not use MVC's `JsonStringEnumConverter`.** `AddControllers().AddJsonOptions` configures the output formatter and nothing else, so handing the snapshot straight to `SendAsync` would put `"state": 1` on the hub while `GET /current` says `"state": "Playing"` — and ADR §8.1 feeds **both** into the same client field. The enums go on the wire as **strings**, which is also what lets a `Radio.Web` build that predates `PHN-1f`'s `Waiting` ignore it instead of failing to parse (plan C-47). (3) **The circuit backstop fires on the TRANSITION to zero, never on an observed zero**, and is registered **singleton** so one instance counts every circuit — a scoped registration gives each circuit its own counter reaching zero on its own close, which is precisely the owner-circuit rule ADR ⟨A1·4⟩ deleted (plan C-52).

⚠ **`AudioStateStore` "had never been constructed in its life" is STALE** — `ENC-12` itself added `MainLayout.razor:20`'s injection, so nothing needs bringing to life (plan C-48).

⚠ **Three things no unit test in this repo can prove** and which the plan's §2.2 settles on the box: that the payload survives the real `JsonHubProtocol`, that the backstop's latency is what ADR §7.3 assumes, and that a **browser refresh does not stop playback**.

⛔ **IT DOES — measured on the box 2026-09-04, twice: reloading the only browser goes 1 → 0 → 1 and stops the playback immediately.** U3 resolved AGAINST the design; see § *Blocker — `PHN-1e`*. The "four minutes late" in the previous wording was a third retention figure, neither the framework's 3 nor this app's configured 10, and the prediction was inverted.

⭐ **RESOLVED — `D30` says the stop is WANTED, so the handler shipped as built and the correction landed on §7.3's reasoning; and the review found a SECOND, independent gap — §7.5's rule never fired on the idle timer, fixed with the `SetSleepScreenVisibleAsync(true)` edge (ADR-029 §16.5).** See § *Resolved — `PHN-1e`'s blocker*. Est.

**3 d**.

### PHN-1f — ADR-029 PR 5b — the mirror-case queue (`D28`), and the ducking-args change that makes its wake correct.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#564](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/564) |
| Plan | [`design/plans/PHN-1f-the-wait-then-play-queue.md`](../design/plans/PHN-1f-the-wait-then-play-queue.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) **D5 §6.2**, `D28` (punch list §7), [`PHN-1d`](../design/plans/PHN-1d-ducking-priority-load-bearing.md) §0.4 C-46 |
| Depends on | ✅ **MET — `PHN-1e` merged 2026-09-04** as [#561](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/561), merge commit `4ec0fb85`. |
| Branch | `feat/phn-1f-mirror-case-queue` |

**ADR-029 PR 5b — the mirror-case queue (`D28`), and the ducking-args change that makes its wake correct.**

🔴 **P0, `O6`, new row: the arc becomes eight PRs.** ✅ **THE 🔒 CLEARED BECAUSE THE PLAN LANDED — that was the row's only lock, and its own flip condition.** This row read *"NOT CLAIMABLE WHEN `PHN-1e` MERGES — THIS ROW HAS NO PLAN"* and *"Flip 🔒 → 📋 only when a plan file exists and this cell links it."* [#563](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/563) wrote that plan on 2026-09-04, the Plan cell now links it, so the condition is met and the row went straight to 🚧. `PHN-1e`'s plan §5 was the **design input**, never the plan — and the planning pass re-derived all five of its settled questions against merged code, where **one did not survive** (C-57, below). Owner decision **`D28`**: pressing play while a source at or above `GvMedia:PreemptAtPriority` is already talking must **wait and then play** — not mix (today), not refuse (rejected as *"press play, get an error, nothing happens"*).

⭐ **The design is `PHN-1e`'s plan §5, complete, with all five of `PHN-1d` §5's open questions settled** — three leans confirmed, one overturned, one dissolved.

**The shape:** a waiting playback **IS** the current playback in a new state; there is no pending slot, so replace semantics, `StopAsync`, `Current`, the re-attach path and the controller's 404/409 rule are all inherited rather than built. Audio is acquired **first** and the wait happens after, so the room's silence is not followed by a fetch — and an acquisition failure surfaces at once rather than after thirty seconds of Waiting.

⚠ **THE OVERTURNED LEAN, AND IT IS THE BULK OF THE ROW.** `PHN-1d` §5 leaned to accepting a known starvation case: `StopDuckingAsync` raises **only when the ducking set empties**, so a ≥ 8 source ending while a sub-8 source continues produces **no wake**, and the playback expires as `Failed`/`WaitExpired` — which is `D28`'s rejected option delivered thirty seconds late.

**Rejected. This row adds `DuckingSourceTransition` and `TriggeringSourcePriority` to `DuckingStateChangedEventArgs`** (a `Radio.Core` change), makes `DuckingService` raise for **every** source that leaves with the true aggregate in `IsDucking`, and updates `AudioManager` to branch on the transition.

⭐ **That closes BOTH residuals `PHN-1d` handed forward as a by-product**: the fade-window race where a synchronous `GetPriority` still answers 8, and the `ActiveEventCount` guard's acknowledged hole — the guard is **deleted**, not supplemented.

⚠ **HIGH RISK, `PHN-1d`'s review posture.** Two hazards make the naive versions wrong: raising `IsDucking:false` while others remain makes `AudioManager` restore the radio to **full volume mid-announcement**, and raising `IsDucking:true` for a stop hands the handler a source whose priority entry is already deleted, turning a **stop into a preemption**. Both are why the transition field exists.

**Third rewrite of the same two ducking tripwire files in three PRs** (`DuckingServiceTests`, `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests`).

⛔ **`EventPlaybackState.Waiting` goes at the END of the enum**, and `APlaybackStartedUnderAHigherPrioritySourceStillMixes_TODAY` is **updated, never deleted**. One new key, `GvMedia:MaxQueuedWaitSeconds` (30) — ⚠ read `PHN-1b` **C-14** first.

⚠ **TWO THINGS THE PLANNING PASS FOUND THAT `PHN-1e` §5 DID NOT, both in plan §0.4.**

**C-56:** `SleepService`'s stop is an **allow-list** (`SleepService.cs:356-358`) while `EventPlaybackSnapshotDto.IsLive` is a **deny-list** (`ApiModels.cs:1522`), so adding `Waiting` silently removes it from ADR §7.5's `/sleep` rule while adding it to the circuit backstop for free — a queued playback would then start audio up to 30 s after the panel goes dark, on `EmptyLayout`, with no stop control anywhere on screen.

**C-57:** the wait adds two throwing exits between acquisition and `TryAdopt`, and neither `TearDownAsync` nor `FailAsync` can release an *unadopted* source — so without a `DisposeOrphanAsync` guard the `RemoteMedia` arm leaks an open `FileStream` over the cached recording.

⭐ **And one thing that got SMALLER:** `Waiting` needs **no `Radio.Web` change and no broadcast change at all** (C-64) — `.ToString()` on the hub plus `IsLive`'s deny-list carry it, which is C-47's payoff arriving. Est.

**2.5-3 d**.

| Depends on | ✅ **MET — `PHN-1f` merged 2026-09-04** as [#564](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/564). Claimed by the Builder cycle of 2026-09-05. |
| Branch | `feat/phn-2-retire-audio-element` |

**ADR-029 PR 6 — retire the `<audio>` element (Feature A).** 🔴 **P0, `O6`, sixth of the eight-PR phone arc, the LAST OPEN P0 on the GA punch list, and the first PR of this arc a user can hear.** `VoicemailPlayer.razor:8` is an HTML5 `<audio>` pointed straight at the GV bridge, so the **browser** fetches and decodes voicemail — bypassing mute, master volume, balance, ducking and Cast routing. Owner decision **`D17`** establishes this is **live behaviour today, not a latent risk**: press play while the radio is on and two sounds run in the room at full level each. This row spends the seam PRs 1–5b built and shipped dark: the element, `wwwroot/js/voicemail-player.js`'s player half and `GvBridgeApiService.GetVoicemailAudioUrl` go **together**; the transport becomes a view of the server snapshot driven through `POST /api/audio/events`; a **console-playback chip** lands in `.topbar-primary` (handoff §Cross-3) as the global stop control that earns *"playback survives navigation"*; **`GvMedia:Enabled` flips `true`** in `src/Radio.API/appsettings.json`, which the deploy overwrites and therefore reaches every box. Also renders `PHN-1f`'s `Waiting` state — **`D28`'s queue is live on the appliance today and nothing shows it** — and adds the `TTSEventSource.Position` override `PHN-1e` §5.2 assigned here.

⭐ **THE OWNER UAT SCRIPT IS [`design/plans/PHN-2-retire-the-audio-element.md` §3](../design/plans/PHN-2-retire-the-audio-element.md#3-owner-uat--feature-a) — a fifteen-minute script written to be read on its own, at the cabinet, by someone who has not read the plan.** Part 0 = two failure modes mistakable for defects (the GV blackout probe; **Cast making the cabinet go quiet is a PASS**); Part 1 = six pre-flight steps incl. the `InterServiceAuthKey` check; Part 2 = U1–U4, the four checks that settle Feature A (ducking, mute, master volume, Cast); Part 3 = four optional; Part 4 = what to capture on a fail.

⚠ **PRE-FLIGHT BEFORE FLIPPING `GvMedia:Enabled` ON THE BOX, and it is a numbered step, not a footnote:** check whether RotaryPhone's `InterServiceAuthKey` is set **on the appliance** — the repo cannot tell you, `GVBridgeConfig.cs:81-82` instructs storing it outside source. Its gate ships default-off, but if it has been set, **every** fetch returns 401 → `MediaUnauthorized` until `GvMedia:AuthKey` matches, across **two** `appsettings.Production.json` files the deploy does not re-seed. Runbook: `design/INTEGRATIONS.md:961-973`.

⚠ **THIS ROW CARRIES THE PHONE ARC'S ENTIRE VERIFICATION DEBT — fourteen deferred items, inventoried in plan §2.2** and labelled `SOUND` (a human confirmed the room changed) or `PATH` (the code path ran), because `AudioFileEventSource.PlayCoreAsync:175-184` selects a **silent simulation** (`PlaybackLoopAsync:261-271`) when no playback service is present and reports a clean completion having produced no audio, at **Debug**, which since `LOG-11` never reaches the journal.

**A green "it completed" is the least trustworthy evidence available here.** ⚠ **A `MediaNotFound` during UAT is as likely to be the GV auth blackout as a bad id** — six distinct transient conditions reach RotaryPhone's 404 because `FindNodeAsync` never checks its list's `Succeeded` flag. Record the wall-clock time of every failure and retry after five minutes before concluding anything.

⚠ **`UI-6` does not block this row and the reason is NOT the punch list's.** Plan §0.6 introduces a `ConsolePlaybackState` singleton so `AudioStateStore.EventPlaybackChanged` keeps **exactly one** subscriber for the life of the process; the obvious two-component design would have taken it to two per circuit — four with two browsers open.

**If a future row adds a second subscriber to that event, that argument expires with it.**

**Est. 3–4 d.**

### OPS-7 — The deploy's Production-config seed can silently destroy `RotaryPhone:Gv:AuthKey`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ |
| Plan | [`OPS-7-production-config-seed-guard.md`](../design/plans/OPS-7-production-config-seed-guard.md) |
| Spec / handoff | [`PHN-2` plan §0.4 C-81](../design/plans/PHN-2-retire-the-audio-element.md) |
| Depends on | [#570](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/570), merge commit `47056cda` |
| Branch | `fix/deploy-production-config-seed-guard` |

**The deploy's Production-config seed can silently destroy `RotaryPhone:Gv:AuthKey`.**

🟡 **P2 — dormant on `radio`, live on the next box provisioned.** `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:222` guards the seed on **`api/`**'s file only (`test -f $TargetPath/api/appsettings.Production.json`), while `:226` copies the seed into **both** `api/` and `web/`. A box whose api overlay is absent while its web overlay is present therefore has the web file overwritten with the api seed — and the web file is where `RotaryPhone:Gv:AuthKey` lives, which is the key `PHN-2` made load-bearing.

**Fix:** guard each destination independently, or seed only the directory whose file is missing.

⚠ **Found by `PHN-2`'s planning pass and deliberately NOT folded into it** — different mechanism (PowerShell deploy sequencing, not a Blazor surface), different verification (a second box or a deliberately-removed file, not a listening test), and a change that can silently destroy a secret does not belong behind a UAT about whether a voicemail ducks the radio. Filed by `PHN-2`'s Builder cycle.

**Est. 0.5 d.** ⭐ **A SECOND DEFECT IN THE SAME FOURTEEN LINES, found by this planning pass and folded in** (`C-88`): the `if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { "Remote file move failed!" }` at `:230` is positioned to guard the remote file move at `:217`, and **cannot** — `:222`'s `ssh … test -f` overwrites `$LASTEXITCODE` first, and since **both** `$configDir` values ship an `appsettings.Production.json`, `:221`'s `Test-Path` is always true, so `:222` always runs. A genuine failure of the file move is **always** masked, and the message names the wrong step.

⚠ **The verification is the reason this got its own plan file rather than a plan-in-the-row: the obvious test — run the deploy and look — is the one that can destroy the secret.** The plan's §3 uses the script's own `-TargetPath` parameter to run against a scratch install path with a sentinel file, and **runs the pre-fix mutation first** so the defect is observed rather than read.

⚠ **`-TargetPath` does NOT isolate everything** — the WirePlumber block at `:299-318` is parameterised by neither `-TargetPath` nor `-NoRestart`, and can restart WirePlumber, cycling BT/audio on the box. Plan §3.2.

**Swept for the same shape elsewhere: one instance.** `Sync-WpRule` (`:268-297`) already guards per destination and is the exemplar the fix copies; the rsync at `:216-217` excludes the overlay from **both** directories and is correct; `setup.sh` has no seed; `Deploy-ToPi.ps1` is a pure wrapper that inherits the bug and needs no separate fix.

⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-05 — THIS ROW'S HEADLINE CLAIM IS FALSE OF EVERY BOX TODAY. It is appended rather than edited into the sentences above, because what the claim did is more useful than what it said.** *The row asserted, and still reads above:* **"can silently destroy `RotaryPhone:Gv:AuthKey`"**, and *"the web file is where `RotaryPhone:Gv:AuthKey` lives, which is the key `PHN-2` made load-bearing"*.

**Measured 2026-09-05, each by opening the file:** `src/Radio.Web/appsettings.Production.json` does carry a `RotaryPhone.Gv.AuthKey` slot, and its value is `""`; the deployed **web** overlay on `radio` is **75 bytes** holding only `RotaryPhone:Gv:MarkReadEnabled`; the deployed **api** overlay is 1,057 bytes of audio-device config; `grep -c AuthKey` returns **0 on both**; and `deploy/debian-x64/appsettings.Production.json` — the seed — has no `RotaryPhone` section at all. So the web overlay is **where the key belongs, not what it holds.** What the overwrite destroys **today** is *operator-authored config*, not a secret: `MarkReadEnabled` on the web side, and on the api side a `Devices:Radio:USBPort` of `AB13X` that appears in no file in this repo and is not reconstructible from it.

**The `AuthKey` exposure is prospective** — real once `PHN-2`'s gate is set, which `PHN-2`'s own row records as shipping default-off.

**The defect, the fix, the est. and the P2 tier are unchanged; only the stakes were overstated.**

⭐ **THE PROPAGATION IS THE FINDING, NOT THE CORRECTION.** The claim crossed **three documents before anyone opened the file**.

**(1) This row** asserted it. **(2) [`OPS-7`'s plan](../design/plans/OPS-7-production-config-seed-guard.md) repeated it** (*"`src/Radio.Web`'s Production overlay is where `RotaryPhone:Gv:AuthKey` lives"*) — and did more than repeat it, it **prescribed the code comment that would carry it onward**.

**(3) The Builder wrote that comment verbatim** into `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1` (*"That file holds RotaryPhone:Gv:AuthKey … the overwrite deleted the key … inter-service auth silently off"*) — **three lines from a comment it had already corrected on the same branch**.

⚠ **The Builder did not invent it; it transcribed the plan** — which is why `CLAUDE.md` § *Pre-Merge Review* makes the *reason* a claim is safe the thing to check, rather than the claim's plausibility. Every link here was plausible, and the first three all skipped the same thirty-second check.

**A second reviewer caught the leaf** and `039a1464` fixed the comment on #570's branch — **but correcting the leaf does not correct the root**, and the root is what the next Builder reads.

⚠ **THE PLAN AT ITS `:48` STILL CARRIES THE CLAIM AND IS DELIBERATELY NOT EDITED HERE.** That file is being modified on #570's open branch; correcting it from `main` would collide with an in-flight PR for no gain.

**It is follow-up work for #570's owner, recorded rather than silently left.** This is not an isolated lapse and this row does not invent a count it did not verify: the `TTS-11` row below carries its own *"CORRECTED AT MERGE"* block for **three** claims that did not survive the build, filed the same day.

⛔ **STATUS DELIBERATELY UNCHANGED.** `OPS-7` is in flight on [#570](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/570) awaiting the owner — this is a record correction, not a re-tier, and nothing here changes what that PR should do.
