### OPS-8 — SHIPPED as [#576](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/576), merged 2026-09-05.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#576](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/576) |
| Plan | *plan-in-the-row, as filed — [#576](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/576)* |
| Spec / handoff | [`design/FUTURE-WORK.md`](../design/FUTURE-WORK.md) §25 · punch list [§5](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · the `OPS-7` row above |
| Depends on | — **independent of `OPS-7`**: different file, different script, no shared lines |
| Branch | `fix/deploy-to-pi-preserve-production-overlays` |

✅ **SHIPPED as [#576](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/576), merged 2026-09-05.** Both `sudo rsync --delete` calls carry `--exclude='appsettings.Production.json'` and a per-destination seed block fills the file in from `deploy/raspberry-pi/`. The row's central claim held: the two flags alone were **not** the fix.

⭐ **THE ROW ASKED FOR A PORT OF THE POST-`OPS-7` SHAPE AND THE REVIEW PROVED THAT SHAPE IS ITSELF UNSAFE.** Ported faithfully, the probe infers absence from a **missing line**, so an unterminated remote banner, CRLF, or a masked remote-side error reads as “absent” and seeds **over** a present overlay — measured on this PR's own first commit (harness case F: `api` `3a49d9f5388e` → `68ba7537fa82`, **exit 0**; the CRLF case destroyed **both**). The merged form demands an explicit `PRESENT:`/`ABSENT:` verdict per destination.

**`Deploy-ToLinux.ps1` still carries the unsafe shape** — `FUTURE-WORK` §28, filed not queued.

⚠ **Verified off-box only**: the real region run under WSL with **real rsync 3.2.7**, `ssh`/`scp`/`sudo`/`chown` shimmed, 3 variants × 8 cases × 24 assertions, instrument validated against the pre-PR script first.

**The `chown radio:radio` was never executed**, and `OPS-7`'s real-`ssh` route was not taken.

⚠ **Fresh-Pi behaviour change disclosed**: `api/` now receives an overlay pinning `PreferredDeviceId` to `playback-12` and Cast to `DirectChannel` — convergent with `Deploy-ToPi.ps1`, which already seeds the same file. — *original finding:* **`deploy-to-pi.sh` destroys BOTH Production overlays on every run, and ships nothing that could restore them.**

🟡 **P2 — argued against punch list §1 in [§5](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) rather than asserted here, and it lands at P2 for the *opposite* reason `OPS-7` does.**

⭐ **IT OUTRANKS `OPS-7` ON MECHANISM AND IS NARROWER ON REACH; both halves are load-bearing.** `OPS-7` needs a divergent box state to bite — an api overlay absent while a web overlay is present. This needs **nothing.** `deploy/deploy-to-pi.sh:114-115` runs `sudo rsync -a --delete` into `$PI_PATH/api/` and `$PI_PATH/web/` with **no `--exclude` on either**, so it destroys operator-authored Production config on **every run, unconditionally**. Its twin at `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:217` applies `--exclude='appsettings.Production.json'` to **both** invocations on that one line; this script applies it to **neither**, and carries no seed block at all. `set -euo pipefail` does not help — rsync succeeds at doing the wrong thing.

**Not dead code:** `deploy/DEPLOYMENT.md` § *Verifying the Deployed Commit* calls it one of *"Both deploy scripts"*.

⚠ **THE TWO HALVES FAIL DIFFERENTLY, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE USEFUL PART OF THIS ROW.**

**`api/` is deleted outright** — `src/Radio.API/` ships no `appsettings.Production.json` (only `appsettings.json` and `appsettings.Development.json`), so `--delete` removes the operator's file and **nothing replaces it**.

**`web/` is overwritten** — `src/Radio.Web/appsettings.Production.json` **is** tracked and `Microsoft.NET.Sdk.Web` auto-includes `appsettings*.json`, so it reaches the publish output and rsync copies it over the operator's file, carrying `"RotaryPhone": { … "Gv": { "AuthKey": "" } }` — an **empty** key.

⭐ **THE DELETION IS LOUDER; THE SILENT REPLACEMENT IS THE ONE THAT WOULD PASS A CASUAL LOOK.** An absent file shows up in `ls`; a present, well-formed, tracked-looking file that has quietly zeroed the key does not — the service simply comes up with inter-service auth off, which is the identical end state `OPS-7` was filed over.

⚠ **Note the tense, and read the `OPS-7` row's correction above before repeating this claim in the present tense — four documents already did.** What this destroys **today** is *operator-authored config*: the api overlay is the one with real content (on `radio`, measured 2026-09-05, 1,057 bytes including a `Devices:Radio:USBPort` that exists in no repo file), and the web overlay on that box is 75 bytes of `MarkReadEnabled`. `RotaryPhone:Gv:AuthKey` joins the loss **only once `PHN-2`'s gate is set** — and when it does, this path replaces it with `""` rather than removing it, so the file still looks right.

⛔ **NOT FIXED BY ADDING THE TWO `--exclude` FLAGS, and that is why the finding Builder left it.** The exclude and the seed are a **matched pair** — `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1` carries both — so an exclude alone would leave a freshly provisioned Pi with no web overlay at all. The work is the two flags **plus** a seed block ported from the **post-`OPS-7`** shape (the block under the `GUARDED PER DESTINATION, NOT ONCE FOR BOTH` comment); ⚠ **a naive port of the pre-`OPS-7` block recreates the exact bug #570 is closing.** And the verification needs an **ARM target the finding Builder could not exercise**, with a live Pi run risking the very overlay under discussion — `OPS-7`'s off-box approach against a scratch `-TargetPath` is the honest route and ports here.

⚠ **`FUTURE-WORK` §26 is deliberately NOT folded into this row** — reasoning in `OPS-9` below.

**Technical detail is kept in `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` §25 rather than duplicated here.**

**Est. 0.5–1 d**, most of it the off-box harness rather than the nine lines of script.

---

### OPS-9 — A failed `scp` is masked by the `ssh` on the next line, twice, in `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#574](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/574) |
| Plan | *plan-in-the-row, as filed — [#574](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/574)* |
| Spec / handoff | [`design/FUTURE-WORK.md`](../design/FUTURE-WORK.md) §26 · the `OPS-7` row above (`C-88`, the same defect class) |
| Depends on | ✅ **UNBLOCKED 2026-09-05** — its condition was `OPS-7` / [#570](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/570) merging, which it did as merge commit `47056cda`. The overlapping region is settled: `OPS-7` rewrote the config-seed block and captured `$moveExit`; `OPS-9`’s two sites (`:194-198`, `:205-212`) are on the scp fallback path and were deliberately left untouched. Claimable. |
| Branch | `fix/deploy-tolinux-scp-exit-code` |

**A failed `scp` is masked by the `ssh` on the next line, twice, in `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1`.**

🟡 **P2, latent — the same `$LASTEXITCODE`-at-a-distance class as `OPS-7`'s `C-88`, in the same file, on the one path `C-88` did not touch.** On the scp fallback (`:192-199` for API, `:205-212` for Web) the script runs `scp -r …`, then an `ssh` whose remote compound ends in `rm -rf` and sends both `mv`s' errors to `/dev/null`, and *then* tests `$LASTEXITCODE`. The check's message names the **transfer** (*"API sync failed!"* / *"Web sync failed!"*) while the value it reads belongs to the **tidy-up**, which returns 0 whether or not the directory existed — so a transfer that moved nothing reports success. `$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"` does not rescue it: whether a native command's non-zero exit throws is governed by `$PSNativeCommandUseErrorActionPreference`, **`$false` on this box** (measured, PowerShell 7.6.5), and setting it `$true` would change every other `$LASTEXITCODE` check in the file — so it is not a local fix.

**Fix:** capture `$xferExit = $LASTEXITCODE` on the line immediately after each `scp` and test that, exactly as `OPS-7` did for the file move, keeping the tidy-up's own result separate.

⚠ **Reached only when `rsync` is absent from PATH** (`$useRsync` false), which is why it has not bitten — on the rsync path the last native call before the check *is* the rsync and the check is correct.

⚠ **Verify before tightening:** promoting a previously-ignored exit code to an `exit 1` can start failing deploys that currently succeed; exercise the fallback by making `rsync` temporarily unavailable rather than assuming.

⭐ **DELIBERATELY NOT FOLDED INTO `OPS-8`, and the reason is ordering rather than taxonomy.** Different file, different language, different trigger (latent-on-a-rare-path vs. unconditional-on-every-run), different blast radius (masks an error vs. destroys config) and a different verification environment (drop `rsync` from PATH on the x64 host vs. stand up an ARM target). Decisively: **this fix edits `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1`, which `OPS-7`/[#570](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/570) is rewriting right now** — folding it into `OPS-8` would make an otherwise independent row wait on that PR, and folding it the other way would put one row across two languages and two target architectures with nothing in common but the word *deploy*.

⚠ **🔒 NOT CLAIMABLE UNTIL #570 MERGES** — it touches lines that PR is moving. **Flip 🔒 → 📋 when #570 merges**, and re-verify the two line ranges against the merged file before starting; `OPS-7` already moved this region twice.

**Technical detail: `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` §26. Est. 2–3 h.**

### TTS-11 — The console keeps a plaintext copy of everything it says out loud.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | ✅ [#569](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/569) |
| Plan | [`TTS-11-no-user-text-at-rest-in-the-log.md`](../design/plans/TTS-11-no-user-text-at-rest-in-the-log.md) |
| Spec / handoff | punch list [§4.4](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` § *TTS seam* item 5 |
| Depends on | — |
| Branch | [#569](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/569) |

**The console keeps a plaintext copy of everything it says out loud.** 🟠 **P1 — punch list §4.4 (`docs/HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md:1076`).** Since `LOG-11` the API's Information lines go to the **file sink**, and `src/Radio.API/appsettings.json:17-18` sets `"Override": { "Radio": "Information" }` — so an SMS body the console reads aloud is left **at rest on an appliance in a family home**, and `GET /api/system/logs` renders it in Settings → Logs with `Info` one dropdown away.

⭐ **THE ROW WAS FILED FOR THREE PATHS. THERE ARE ELEVEN, AND THE TWO WORST WERE NOT AMONG THE THREE.** `AnnouncementService.cs:40` logs the message **untruncated** — every other site clips to 47 or 50 characters, this one does not, and it is the shared entry point for `NotificationsController` and phone-call announcements. And `SoundFlowMasterMixer.cs:101-103` logs `source.Name` from **generic mixer code that has no idea it is holding speech** — `TTSEventSource` is the only `IAudioSource` whose `Name` embeds user text (`$"TTS: {first 47 chars}"`). That reframes the defect: it is not three lines that each chose to log text, it is **one property — a display name that is really user content — plus a habit of logging payloads**, so any code that logs an audio source's name is a leak, present or future. Full inventory in plan §0.2.

⚠ **Two line numbers in the punch-list row are stale and are corrected in the plan, not in the punch list** (`C-82`, `C-84`): the ducking argument is at **`AudioManager.cs:515`**, not `:501` — `:499`/`:501` are comment prose after `PHN-1f`/`PHN-2` edited the method — and the whole-string line is **`TTSEventSource.cs:109`**, not `:92`. The row hedged correctly by quoting the statement, which is what let both be re-found.

**A second `AudioManager` site at `:523-525` (`C-83`) and a second mixer site at `:117-119` were never filed at all.**

⭐ **Decision — what replaces the content:** a hash prefix **plus** a length, `txt:9f2ab41c/142`, in the shape `GvMediaCache.MaskFor` already establishes; and for the four `.Name` sites, **drop the name and log `Type` + `Id` instead** (the mixer already logs `Id`, so `Name` is redundant there today).

**This makes diagnosis better, not worse:** today three sites truncate the same utterance at 50, 47 and not-at-all, so an operator cannot tell they are one event — a stable token makes the chain traceable for the first time, and `Id` joins a ducking line to its mixer line.

⚠ **The helper's doc must say it is a correlation token, NOT a confidentiality boundary** — announcement text has a small candidate space and a short utterance's hash is recoverable by enumeration.

**Est. 1.5 d, not the 0.5 h on the punch list** — that estimate was honest for three lines.

⚠ **`NeitherTheTextNorTheRawMediaIdReachesAnyLogLineThisSeamWrites` MUST NOT be widened** — `TTSFactory` news up its own `HttpClient`, so a real factory still cannot run offline and the harness still cannot observe the wider property. Plan §4.6.

⚠ **CORRECTED AT MERGE 2026-09-05 — three claims in this row did not survive the build, and they are corrected here rather than silently left.** (1) **There were TWELVE sites, not eleven.** `AudioController.cs:413` logged `GetActiveSources().Select(s => s.Name)` **at `Warning`** — the only one of the twelve above Information, so it reached journald as well as the file sink. Found by an adversarial reviewer during the build, not by the planning sweep. (2) **"The mixer already logs `Id`, so `Name` is redundant there today" is FALSE for one pair** — `Id` is `$"{Type}-{guid}"`, so `{SourceType}` adds nothing to that line, and `RadioAudioSource` and `SDRRadioAudioSource` **both** return `AudioSourceType.Radio` with different `Name`s, so the line lost the only field telling RF320 from RTL-SDR. The change stands; the loss is bounded because `AudioManager.cs:230` still logs `Name` on the primary path. (3) **"`Id` joins a ducking line to its mixer line" is FALSE** — no path emits both lines for the same source (`AnnouncementService` and `EventPlaybackService` duck but never `AddSource`; `SourcesController.PlayTTSEvent` adds but never ducks). `Id` was kept as independently useful; the justification was wrong. Plan `C-90`/`C-91`/`C-92`.

## Historical narrative

> Moved verbatim from `BUILDER_QUEUE.md`. These sections documented a resolved blocker and the
> reasoning around it; they are kept for the record.

## ✅ Resolved — `PHN-1e`'s blocker, and what each half cost

**Filed 🚫 2026-09-04 (Builder). Cleared 2026-09-04 (Builder) — the row shipped as
[#561](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/561).** ⭐ **Kept in full rather than deleted, and
kept because the two halves resolved in OPPOSITE directions — one against the design, one against the
code — and a reader who sees only "resolved" would learn nothing from either.**

| | What was blocked | How it resolved | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | A browser refresh stops attended playback (ADR-029 §7.3) | ⭐ **Owner decision `D30`: that is the WANTED behaviour.** The code was right; §7.3's *reasoning* was wrong | **Zero code.** The `CircuitHandler` shipped byte-for-byte as built |
| **B2** | §7.5's `/sleep` rule never fires on the idle timer, the case it was written for | **A second edge**, on the `ENC-6` seam `SetSleepScreenVisibleAsync(true)` | One method, one interface signature, 11 tests |

**The instrument that found both is the same one, and it is worth naming.** B1 came from an on-box
check the plan declared *unverifiable in a test host* and attached *"stop and re-plan"* to. B2 came
from the **comment-accuracy reviewer**, which is the second brief this cycle runs because Copilot is
out of quota. Neither is a thing a green test suite can produce.

### B1 — a browser refresh stops attended playback. ⭐ Resolved by owner decision `D30`: it is supposed to.

**What was measured, on the box, twice, at `dd8e85ec`:**

```
18:21:54.342 [INF] Last circuit closed with attended playback evp-23da… still live; stopping it (ADR-029 §7.3)
18:21:54.701 [DBG] Circuit opened; 1 live
```

`GET /api/audio/events/current` went `Playing` → `Stopped` **seven seconds into a forty-nine second
recording**, because the kiosk was reloaded. Deterministic, not a race: with two tabs the sequence is
**`2 → 1 → 2`** — the close arrives *first* — so with a single browser it is **`1 → 0 → 1`**.

**Why the plan's U3 was wrong.** It predicted `1 → 2 → 1`, the replacement circuit opening before the
old one closed. Inverted. ⚠ **And note that U3's *prediction* being wrong is not the point** — the
value was in declaring the ordering unverifiable and naming the observation that would settle it. A
plan that had asserted the same ordering with more confidence would have produced identical code and
no discovery.

**Why it is immediate rather than late.** `DisconnectedCircuitRetentionPeriod` (10 minutes here)
governs **unexpected** disconnects. A reload, a navigation or a tab close is a **graceful**
termination and Blazor disposes the circuit at once. The retention window never enters the refresh
path — so `OnCircuitClosedAsync` is **bimodal, immediate or ten minutes, with no indication of which**.

**⭐ THE RESOLUTION, AND IT WENT THE OTHER WAY FROM EVERY OPTION THIS SECTION ORIGINALLY LISTED.**
Owner decision **`D30`**, 2026-09-04: *"If the page reloads mid-voicemail, the audio should fail. If
the user wants to hear it they can replay."* So:

- **`AttendedPlaybackCircuitHandler` ships EXACTLY as built.** No grace period, no circuit-identity
  matching, no survival mechanism — ⛔ **and none to be proposed as a future row.** All three options
  this section originally offered the Planner (drop §7.3; a grace period; distinguish graceful from
  dropped) are **withdrawn**, refused on the decision rather than on their merits.
- **What fell was the ADR's prose, not the branch's behaviour.** §7.3's worked example, its
  retention-window claim, and its self-description as *"the weakest of the three defences"* are all
  struck — under `D30` the rule is **promoted** to the mechanism that implements the decision in this
  box's resting configuration, with the 300 s cap as the backstop behind **it**.
- ⚠ **One divergence recorded rather than decided:** with a second browser open a reload is
  `2 → 1 → 2` and stops nothing, so the rule implements `D30` literally only in the single-browser
  case. The only mechanism that would close it is the ownership model ⟨A1·4⟩ deleted; the divergence
  is benign, because a client still holds the transport.

**⚠ The claim this section made that did NOT survive.** It said *"as built, every reachable firing of
this backstop is a false positive."* That was correct **against §7.3's stated goal** and is **false
against `D30`'s** — the identical firing is now a true positive. It is preserved because the way it
stopped being true is the whole content of the amendment: nothing measured changed, only the
objective.

**Record:** ADR-029 §16 (Amendment 2), `design/DECISION-LOG.md`, and the `D30` register entry in
[`docs/HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) §7 — which the ADR deliberately left for
this cycle to file, and which this cycle filed.

### B2 — ADR §7.5's `/sleep` rule did not cover the idle timer, which is the case it was written for. ✅ Fixed in this row.

Found by the comment-accuracy reviewer and confirmed. §7.5's premise is *"the console can put itself
into a layout with no stop control, **on an idle timer**, while a voicemail is playing."* The rule
shipped in `SleepService.EnterSleepAsync`, and **the idle path never reaches it**: `idle-dimmer.js`'s
`navigateToSleep('idle')` only sets `window.location.href = '/sleep'`, carrying its own comment that it
deliberately does not call `SetSleepAsync` because idle navigation must not pause playback. So
`IsSleeping` is **false** on that path and the obvious predicate is the wrong one.

**Fixed with a second edge, per ADR-029 §16.5 — and the two are not redundant:**

| Edge | Expresses | Is the ONLY stopper for |
|---|---|---|
| `SleepService.SetSleepScreenVisibleAsync(true)` | the **no-transport surface** went up | the **30-minute idle timer** and a **direct navigation** — the routes that reach `/sleep` without parking audio |
| `SleepService.EnterSleepAsync` | the **room is being parked** | the Sleep pill, the server push, and the entries with **no browser at all** — `POST /api/system/sleep`, the encoder long-press |

⚠ **Read the second column carefully, because a first draft of this row got it wrong** and the
adversarial review caught it. The screen edge does not *stop anything* on the pill or the server
push: both park audio first, so by the time `Sleep.razor` reports itself there is nothing left to
stop. Every route is covered; which edge covers it is what matters when one of them changes.
⚠ **ADR-029 §16.5's own table says this edge "covers rows 1, 2, 3, 5"**, which is true of *producing
the fact* and false of *stopping the playback*. Worth an Architect's eye on the next pass — this row
did not amend the merged ADR.

⭐ **AND THE PREDICATE IS THE REPORT, NOT A TRANSITION OF `ConsoleWakeState` — a pre-merge
correction, and the sharpest finding of the cycle.** The rule was first written as *"stop when
`WakeState` leaves `Awake`"*, with a comment arguing it *"cannot suppress a stop"*. It can:
`Sleep.razor`'s dispose report is best-effort behind a 2 s timeout and `MainLayout`'s corrective
`false` is fire-and-forget with the failure swallowed, so **losing both leaves the flag reading
`true` while the console is genuinely awake on Home** — the tree documents both halves in comments.
A voicemail then starts, the idle timer lands on `/sleep` thirty minutes later, the page reports
`true`, nothing *changed*, and a transition rule **does not fire**. That is §7.5's own failure,
produced by the rule written to prevent it. A second, independent loss: with no lock, the "after"
re-read could observe a *concurrent* report's write, which a kiosk reload while on `/sleep` produces
naturally. Deciding from the report's own argument closes both by construction.
⚠ **"Never polled" is untouched** — still driven by a client's report, no loop, no timer, and
`WakeState` is now not consulted at all, which §16.5 also asks for. Idempotence moved somewhere
stronger: the stop decides on the playback's **state**, so a repeat report finds a terminal snapshot
and does nothing — and that holds across *different* clients, which an edge on one global flag
cannot see.

⚠ **`ISleepService.SetSleepScreenVisible` became `SetSleepScreenVisibleAsync` and the stop is
AWAITED.** §16.5 left that choice open and argued for awaiting on **C-49**'s own lesson — this arc has
already paid once for a stop that nothing observed. `TheReportDoesNotAnswerUntilTheStopHasCompleted`
reds under `_ = StopAttendedPlaybackAsync()`.

⚠ **How B2 survived design review, which is the transferable half.** The false claim propagated
through **four** documents — ADR §7.5 → plan constraint **C-51** → the branch's comment in
`EnterSleepAsync` → the PR body — each restating the one before it. **Not one of them checked
`idle-dimmer.js` against the claim**, and that file contradicts it in a comment written for exactly
this reason.

### What the two reviewers found, and the one thing deliberately NOT fixed

Copilot is out of quota, so two adversarial reviewers ran with different briefs — **lifecycle,
concurrency and the wire contract**, and **comment-and-test accuracy**. Third cycle running that this
pairing has earned its cost.

**Fixed in this row** — one behavioural defect in the day's own change, three latent hazards, four
tests that could not fail, and seven comments that asserted more than the code did. The defect and the
hang are the two worth remembering:

- ⭐ **The sleep edge could be SUPPRESSED by a stale flag** — see the predicate note above. Found by
  the comment reviewer, attacking the sentence *"it cannot suppress a stop"* rather than the code.
  ⚠ **The comment was written the same day, by the Builder, in the fix for the previous cycle's
  finding.** That is the shape `CLAUDE.md` § *Pre-Merge Review* names: a comment offering a reason a
  thing is safe, where the reason is the claim to check.
- ⚠ **A wedged stop made the console UNWAKEABLE.** `EnterSleepAsync` calls the attended-playback stop
  while holding `_lock`, and `WakeAsync` takes the same `_lock` — so an unbounded
  `IEventPlaybackService.StopAsync` parks the encoder, the screen tap and the REST wake behind it at
  once, and this repo has a documented class of hang in exactly that layer. Time-boxed at 3 s.
  Falsified by removing the bound: **the test hangs rather than failing.**
- The cap's timer callback was a **process kill** (unguarded `TimerCallback`; `ILogger` rethrows
  provider exceptions), `OnCircuitOpenedAsync` could **strand the circuit count** so `D30`'s rule
  silently never fires again, and `MaxPlaybackSeconds` had **no upper clamp** — over ~49.7 days
  `CreateTimer` throws *after* `PlayAsync`, failing every attended playback under a generic reason.

⛔ **DEFERRED, deliberately, and it is a real defect: `AudioStateStore.NotifyAsync` awaits only the
LAST subscriber of a multicast `Func<Task>`.** `await handler.Invoke()` on a multicast delegate returns
one task, so earlier subscribers run to their first `await` and their continuations are never observed
— the `try`/`catch` around it protects exactly one of N. The store is a **singleton** and every
circuit's components subscribe, so N > 1 is the normal multi-client state.
**Why deferred rather than fixed here:** it is **pre-existing and shared by all eleven events on that
store**; `EventPlaybackChanged` merely inherits it. Fixing it correctly means iterating
`GetInvocationList()` and awaiting each, which changes the failure semantics of ten events this row
does not own — the kind of unrelated blast radius the `PHN-1e`/`PHN-1f` split exists to refuse.
⚠ **It is worth a Planner row, and PR 6 is the deadline that matters**, because PR 6's stop chip is the
first consumer that will hang off `EventPlaybackChanged` on every circuit at once.

### `UI-6` — the tiering argument, and the two counts the deferral note got wrong

✅ **FILED 2026-09-04 (Builder, `PHN-1f` cycle) as queue row `UI-6` and punch-list §5 `UI-6`.** The deferral note
immediately above is what proposed it. Its *defect* description is right and is carried forward unchanged; its
**tiering and its deadline are not**, and `PHN-1f`'s planning pass corrected both against merged code at `4ec0fb85`.
Recorded here rather than by editing the note, because the note is the record of what the `PHN-1e` cycle believed.

**1. "Shared by all eleven events" overstates the reach: nine of the eleven have ZERO production subscribers.**
`AudioStateStore` is injected in exactly **one** place in the whole tree — `MainLayout.razor:20` — and subscribes
exactly **two** of its events: `EncoderConfigStatusChanged` (`:402`) and `EncoderConnectionChanged` (`:403`). Its only
other consumer, `AttendedPlaybackCircuitHandler`, subscribes to nothing; it reads `EventPlayback` directly.
⚠ **The count that looks alarming comes from `AudioStateHubService`, a different type** — most `@inject`ed fields
named `AudioState` in this repo (e.g. `Sleep.razor:13`) are the **hub service**, not the store. That naming collision is
almost certainly what produced the "all eleven" reading.

**2. PR 6 is NOT the deadline.** `EventPlaybackChanged` has **zero** subscribers today, and PR 6 takes it to **one**.
A multicast defect needs **N ≥ 2** to bite: with one subscriber, `Invoke` returns that subscriber's own Task, it is
awaited, and its exceptions are caught — **no defect at all.** What crosses the threshold is a **second circuit**: the
store is a singleton and `MainLayout` is per-circuit, so two open browsers already give `EncoderConnectionChanged` two
subscribers **today** — the box's documented second-browser case (ADR-029 §16.3 P4), not a hypothetical.

**3. P2 on punch list §1's own criteria**, which tier by consequence in the cabinet:

- **(a)** wrong or dangerous on day one — no. **(d)** substrate for verification — no. **(e)** permanent at install — no.
- **(c)** unrecoverable without a laptop — **no.** `ThrowUnobservedTaskExceptions` is **not set anywhere in the tree**, so
  on .NET 10 an unobserved task exception is swallowed at finalization. There is no process-crash path and nothing wedges.
- **(b)** *"the machine reports success and does nothing; a control does nothing"* — **not met by the async half.** Every
  subscriber still runs synchronously up to its first `await`, and both live handlers reach `await InvokeAsync(…)` there,
  so the re-render **is** dispatched on every circuit regardless. What is lost is a log line on a failure, plus the fact
  that `NotifyAsync` returns early. **The synchronous-throw half COULD meet (b)** — one bad subscriber starves the rest —
  but neither shipped handler can throw synchronously: both open with a null-guarded property read
  (`MainLayout.razor:1329`, `:1345`) before their first await.

⚠ **What would move it to P1, stated so the tier can be re-argued rather than re-guessed:** a store subscriber whose
handler either (i) can throw **before** its first `await`, or (ii) does work after an await that the *caller* of
`UpdateX`/`NotifyAsync` depends on having finished. Neither exists today, and **PR 6's chip is very unlikely to be
either** — a chip handler is `await InvokeAsync(StateHasChanged)`, the same shape as the two that already exist.

**Ordering call: it can land AFTER PR 6, and it should not block it.** PR 6 does not change the reachability threshold,
and the fix touches the failure semantics of ten events PR 6 does not own — exactly the unrelated blast radius the
`PHN-1e`/`PHN-1f` split exists to refuse. ⚠ **But it should land before whatever adds a SECOND subscriber to any one
store event**, and PR 6 is the row most likely to be followed by one. **Queued now at P2, unblocked; clear it in any
cycle with slack** — including before PR 6 if one appears, since it is half a day and touches no file this arc needs.

### What was never blocked, and is verified on the box

All at `dd8e85ec`, on the appliance. Recorded so nobody re-runs them:

- **U1 SETTLED — the payload survives the real `JsonHubProtocol`.** Two `Received EventPlaybackChanged event` lines in the Web log, emitted *inside* the typed `On<EventPlaybackSnapshotDto?>` handler, so deserialization succeeded; zero errors. `GET /current` returned `"state":"Playing"` as a string on the same playback. **C-47 is correct and this is the half that makes `PHN-1f`'s new state deployable without a lockstep Web build.**
- **U2 SETTLED — a singleton `CircuitHandler` receives every circuit's callbacks.** Count went `1 → 2` across two independent circuits. No fallback to a scoped registration is needed; the plan's Task 7 shape is right.
- **The max-duration cap works on real hardware.** Cap temporarily set to 20 s against a 42 s recording: `Playing` at t+17 s, `Stopped` by t+27 s, with `[WRN] … reached GvMedia:MaxPlaybackSeconds (20s)` in journald. Config restored afterwards. ⭐ **And it is now provably load-bearing in a test host too**, which matters because Amendment 2's P6 reasoning depends on the cap firing: removing `ArmDurationCap` reds 2 tests, and keeping the timer while dropping its dispatched `StopAsync` reds the same 2 — so the tests pin the **stop**, not merely the timer.
- **The `/sleep` rule works for the path that reaches it.** `Playing` → `POST /api/system/sleep` → `Stopped`, and waking does **not** resume — C-51's surviving half.
- **U3 is not on this list, and that is the point of the list.** It is the one that resolved against the design; see B1.

### On-box UAT of the new edge, 2026-09-04 — including the latency ADR §16.5 asked for

Deployed to `radio` and driven through the real controller, real DI and real JSON; both services
SHA-verified, kiosk relaunched and live each time.

⚠ **RUN TWICE, AND THE SECOND RUN IS THE ONE THAT COUNTS.** T1–T4 were first measured at `947fb2e`,
**which carried the transition predicate the pre-merge review then replaced.** Publishing those
numbers for the shipped build would have been the "measured several commits back" mistake this queue
has had to retract before — so T1 and T3 were re-run at `01dec7f`, and **T5 exists only at `01dec7f`,
because it is the case the change was for.**

| # | What was driven | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **T1** | `POST /api/system/sleep-screen {visible:true}` with a live playback, **no browser and no `EnterSleepAsync`** — re-run at `01dec7f` | `Playing` → `Stopped`. `isSleeping` stayed **false** throughout — the idle-path state, where the old rule did nothing. The API log carries `Sleep stopped attended playback evp-52d6…` with **no preceding `Entering sleep mode`**, which is what isolates the new edge from the old one |
| **T2** | The idle timer's own mechanism — `window.location.href = '/sleep'` driven over kiosk CDP — with a live playback | Stopped **592 ms** after the navigation. ⚠ **The CIRCUIT rule won on this path**, logging `Last circuit closed with attended playback … still live`. That is ADR §16.1's predicted overlap, which it marked *"derived from the mechanism, not measured"* — **now measured.** The two rules are not redundant; they overlap on exactly this path |
| **T3** | `{visible:false}` (what `MainLayout` reports on every navigation home) with a live playback — re-run at `01dec7f` | Still `Playing`. The inverse holds |
| **T4** | ⭐ **The latency §16.5 item 2 called "plausibly seconds" and left UNMEASURED** — hard navigation → the sleep screen reporting itself | **112 ms / 113 ms / 140 ms** over three runs, verified with the page really at `/sleep` and Blazor connected. It is milliseconds, not seconds |
| **T5** | ⭐ **THE STALE-FLAG CASE, at `01dec7f`** — flag already `true`, a **new** playback starts, then a report that is **not a change** (what the idle timer sends when the flag was never cleared) | `Playing` → **`Stopped`**. ⛔ **Under the transition predicate this stops NOTHING** — it is the defect the comment reviewer found, and this is it reproduced and closed on real hardware rather than only in a unit test |

⭐ **T2 also settles a MEDIUM the lifecycle reviewer could not settle from the tree.** This row moves
`IEventPlaybackService` (and `GvMediaClient`, `AudioFileEventSourceFactory`, `ITTSFactory`,
`IDuckingService` with it) from lazy construction to **eager, at host start**, because
`AudioStateUpdateService` is a hosted service that now resolves it in its constructor — so a
construction failure changes from *"a 500 on the first voicemail"* to *"`radio-api` fails to start"*,
i.e. a restart loop on a headless appliance. `CustomWebApplicationFactory` strips every
`IHostedService`, so **no test in the suite covers it**. The deploy did: `radio-api` came up, answered
`/api/health/version` at the right SHA, and served every check above.

⚠ **T2 and T4 were not re-run at `01dec7f`, and that is defensible rather than an oversight:** T2's
result is that the **circuit** rule wins the hard-navigation path, and the circuit rule is byte-for-byte
unchanged by the review; T4 measures how fast `Sleep.razor` reports itself, which is a Blazor render
latency the predicate does not touch. Said here rather than left for a reader to assume.

⚠ **What was NOT re-run at all, and why:** `U1` (the payload survives the real `JsonHubProtocol`) and `U2`
(a singleton `CircuitHandler` sees every circuit) were settled on the box by the previous cycle and
are unchanged by this one. `U3` needed no re-run because `D30` settled it as a decision rather than a
measurement.

### Two plan defects found by the same cycle, both fixed here

- **Task 12's scope gate contradicted Task 10c.** Gate 2 grepped the diff for the queued-state literal and expected nothing; Task 10c ⭐ *required* that literal in `AttendedPlaybackCircuitHandlerTests`. Scoped.
- **Task 8's `ANaturalEndDisarmsTheDurationCap` could not fail as specified.** Its `StopCalls` assertion held whether or not the disarm existed, because `ClaimTerminal` absorbs the late stop before it reaches the source — measured, **62/62 green with the disarm deleted**. Rewritten to assert on the cap's own log line, which goes red under that mutation. The plan's stated reason (*"it would only touch the source a second time"*) was wrong.

That is a `PATH` result and **discharges no `SOUND` item**. 🆕 **`OPS-7` filed 📋** (the deploy's Production-config seed can destroy `RotaryPhone:Gv:AuthKey`) and **`FUTURE-WORK.md` §24 added** — re-expanding a collapsed voicemail row loses `_playbackId`, so the transport reads idle for audible playback and play restarts from the beginning; the chip still stops it, and the fix needs a `MediaId` on the wire, so it is the owner's call. **`PHN-1f`'s stale 🚧 row corrected to ✅ #564**, and `UI-6`'s three code citations fixed for lines this diff moved.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-04 (Builder):** **`PHN-1c` shipped ✅ as [#556](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/556). ADR-029 PR 3 is in: `EventPlaybackService`, the six `/api/audio/events` routes, and both arms. This is the first PR of the phone arc a user can reach.** ⭐ **The find of the cycle is a test that could not fail.** The deadlock invariant — `PlaybackCompleted` is raised *synchronously* from inside `StopCoreAsync`, which `TearDownAsync` calls **while `_gate` is held**, so an `OnSourceCompleted` that waited on `_gate` deadlocks the appliance on every user stop — was documented in two places and **tested nowhere**, because the test fake never raised on `StopAsync`. Proven in both directions: with the fake fixed and the deadlock injected the run **hung and was killed at 90 s**; with the old silent fake and *the same deadlock present*, **39/39 passed in 3 seconds**. ⚠ **A resource leak found by reading committed code, not plan text:** `TearDownAsync` could only release a source the playback already *owned*, which it did not for the whole of acquisition — so a stop mid-fetch released nothing and the finished source was dropped undisposed (on the RemoteMedia arm, an open `FileStream` that also blocks `GvMediaCache`'s evictor on Windows). Fixed with single-owner handover (`TryAdopt`/`ClaimSourceForRelease`); the adjacent ducking→play window is **closed**, not argued benign, because PR 4's preemption lands on it. ⚠ **The plan contradicted itself and Task 9 was the correct half:** `Current` now **RETAINS** the terminal snapshot, because the `202` shape leaves no response to carry an acquisition failure — so `204` means *"nothing started since boot"*, not *"nothing playing"*, and a transport call against a just-completed id answers **409, not 404**. Ten tests failed on this before it was spotted; `IEventPlaybackService.Current` carries a `<remarks>` so the shipped summary did not silently become false. **PR 5 must know: seed `AudioStateStore` from `/current` reading `state`, not nullness.** ⚠ **Three plan defects worth not rediscovering.** (1) The plan's engine check used `Enum.TryParse` alone — which **accepts numeric strings**, and `TTSEngine` is numbered from 1, so `engine: "0"` would have passed validation and thrown later; `Enum.IsDefined` is required and is proven at the route. (2) The plan's C-25 fixture used `Speed`/`Pitch` of `1.0f`, **identical to `TTSParameters`' own initializers**, so it would have passed against the exact bug it exists to catch; it now uses `1.15f`/`0.85f` and was falsified. (3) Task 13's gate 1 (`grep "AddSource"` must be 0) **contradicts Task 4's own prescribed comment** — the substantive gate is *no call site*, and there is none. ⚠ **`TTS-9` had made parts of this row false and the row is now corrected in place:** the eSpeak argument-injection item is **discharged, not re-filed** (`GenerateESpeakAsync` no longer exists; filing a hole that does not exist costs a future session a cycle), and the `TTSParameters` trap is **two fields wide, not four** — `Engine`/`Voice` are nullable since #548, only `Speed`/`Pitch` still pin. The surviving class question is **`SEC-5`** and belongs to Planner. ⚠ **`INTEGRATIONS.md:566` does not exist as three merged plans claim** — `:566` is a code fence; the ducking-priority claim PR 4 owns was at `:932` and this PR's own insertion moved it to `:980`. **Cite it by content, never by line number.** ⚠ **A real residual filed, not fixed:** the *real* `TTSFactory` and `TTSEventSource` log the utterance — one of them **in full** — and since `LOG-11` that is private SMS text at rest in the log file. The seam itself is clean; `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` § *TTS seam* item 5 has it. **UAT was live, not claimed:** 202 `Preparing` → `Failed`/`MediaTransport` through `GET /current` with no network, all four 400s returning **named** reasons, and the raw media id appearing in **no** log line — only the mask `gvm:3886337d`. **Not verified and not claimed: that a voicemail is audible.** That needs the box and is PR 6's, along with `./data/gvmedia` writability, which is **re-carried** because `GvMedia:Enabled` still ships `false`.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-03 (Builder):** **`ENC-20` shipped ✅ as [#555](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/555). The first defects the owner found with his own hand on the panel — and one of them was a safety justification whose arithmetic was wrong by a factor of two.** ⭐ **`ENC-17`'s harness paid for itself on its first real job.** It measured **4 volume points per detent before and 1 after**, and a per-event clamp of **12 points before and 4 after** — the first points-per-detent figures in this repo that came from a measurement rather than from arithmetic. Every prior encoder row reasoned about this number; the owner's report is what that cost. ⚠ **The wrong claim, so nobody re-derives it as new:** `RotaryEncoderConfigDefaults` promised *"a maximum slew of 6 points per 80 ms"* and *"silence to full takes at least 1.33 seconds"*. Real figures: **12 points**, **0.67 s**, and **0.33 s** at the fastest rate tier 2 actually fires at. It was **not a typo** — handoff §5.4's table computes points as `multiplier × 2`, true only at `step_size 1`, and **`ENC-11` [#509](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/509) shipped `step: 2` while inheriting that justification**. Code and spec disagreed from that commit onward and nothing mechanical noticed. ⚠ **Two corrections now recorded wherever the claim lived** (code, handoff Rev 8, punch list, `HANDOFF-NEXT-SESSION`, `INTEGRATIONS.md`, the harness, the tests): **a tier threshold is a maximum interval, not the rate the user turns at**, so no rate-based floor follows from it — the honest bound is points **per detent**, and any figure in seconds must name the spin rate it assumes. ⚠ **The boot window is now strictly *safer*, not merely nicer:** a factory `×50` event was clamped to 12 points and is now clamped to 4. `VolumeClampUnverified` stayed at `2` and thereby *tightened* — it bounded 4 points, it now bounds 2. **Do not raise it to 4 to "match" the new clamp**; that would equal the normal clamp and destroy the tightening while still being classified, reported and tested. ⚠ **The dim-wake fix is NOT an `ENC-6` regression** — `ENC-6` owns the `/sleep` model at the **30-minute** boundary; this was the separate **5-minute** dim, which it correctly never touched. Encoder input is a SignalR push and **never becomes a DOM event**, so `idle-dimmer.js` could not see it. The input is **not consumed** (deliberately unlike ENC-6 Rule 1), and the two stay separate **by construction**: `Sleep.razor` is on `EmptyLayout`, so `MainLayout` is not mounted on `/sleep`. ⚠ **A UAT-instrument trap worth not rediscovering:** wrapping `window.radioSleepManager.wake` to count calls records **nothing** if any encoder input happened earlier in the page's life — Blazor caches the resolved JS function on first `InvokeVoidAsync`, so a later wrapper is never reached. The first run showed an empty log against a screen that had visibly undimmed. Reload, wrap, *then* turn. ⭐ **The harness also under-reported the real knob by exactly 2× until this row**, because `turn 0 1` emits one *unit* while VOLUME's `step_size` was 2 — and its own docstring claimed the designed config used `step_size 1`. A mechanical instrument existed and still missed the defect a hand found in seconds. Verified on `radio` at `6b2e580`: read-back `Configured` with **0 fields disagreeing**, `flash = MatchesCurrentDesign`, and the real encoder rebound with no harness residue.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-03 (Builder):** **`TTS-9` shipped ✅ as [#548](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/548). eSpeak is gone, and with it `SEC-4`, `TTS-7` and the root of `TTS-3`.** The owner chose removal over remediating `SEC-4` in place: comparable effort, three rows closed instead of one guarded. `GenerateESpeakAsync` — the `$"-v {voice} …"` interpolation reachable **unauthenticated** and capable of an arbitrary file write via `-w <path>` — no longer exists, along with `IsESpeakAvailable`, `GetESpeakVoicesAsync`, the `TTSEngine.ESpeak` member and `TTSOptions.ESpeakPath`. **`O11` is recorded as DISCHARGED, not deleted**, because it is the record of a hazard that existed and how it was retired. ⚠ **Three things a future session must not rediscover as bugs.** (1) **There is now no TTS at all without network** — eSpeak was the only `IsOffline` engine. Accepted trade-off, owner decision `D26`: announcements are smart-home events, so if the network is down the events are not arriving either. (2) **The eSpeak defaults did NOT become Google.** Several were defaults *precisely because* eSpeak needed no API key, so substituting a cloud engine would have converted them into defaults that fail with no key configured. `TTS:DefaultEngine` / `DefaultVoice` are now **empty**, and an unset or unknown value **throws naming the valid set** — explicit selection over a silent substitution. `ParseEngine`'s silent ESpeak fallback (the `TTS-1` failure shape: the console announces nothing and reports success) now throws as well. (3) ⚠ **The enum is deliberately numbered `{ Google = 1, Azure = 2 }` — do not renumber it from 0.** `ESpeak` was the zero value; letting the remaining members renumber would make `default(TTSEngine)` silently equal `Google`, a cloud engine reached by accident rather than by choice. `TTSParameters.Engine` is now `TTSEngine?` so "unspecified" is representable and resolved once — which is the cleanup **ADR-029 §9.3 had already flagged as "the root cause"**, so **`PHN-1c` branches cut before 2026-09-03 must rebase onto it.** A regression test pins the numbering: `Assert.False(Enum.IsDefined(default(TTSEngine)))`. ⚠ **The stored `TTS:LastEngine = "ESpeak"` was left in place deliberately and needs no migration** — it is a `string` with **zero readers**, and nothing parses it into a `TTSEngine`, so it binds harmlessly. Verified on the box rather than assumed: startup clean, Settings page clean, `tts:eSpeakPath` now orphaned and inert. ⭐ **One new row, `SEC-5`, found by this cycle's own pre-merge pass and not by the reviewer:** `BuildAzureSsml` interpolated the caller-supplied voice id raw into `<voice name='{voice}'>` while carefully escaping the *text* one line below, so the **same unauthenticated route** could break out of the single-quoted attribute with `engine=Azure`. The one-line escape shipped here — **falsified before being trusted**: reverting it makes the new test fail with `</voice><voice` present in the rendered document. **The allow-list decision is `SEC-5` and belongs to Planner.** `SEC-4` closed the *instance*, not the *class*, and the very next engine having the same defect shape is the evidence. Deployed and verified on `radio` at `5e571b8`: the engines endpoint lists only Google and Azure, and a Google announcement was confirmed **in the file sink** — not by its `200`, which proves nothing while `TTS-2` remains open by design. ——— **Appended 2026-09-03 by the tracking audit, because the entry above does not carry these and a reader stops at the newest banner.** ⚠ **The encoder arc is COMPLETE — all 12 encoder P0s have shipped**, so no `ENC-*` row below is claimable as new work; `ENC-17` (input injection via `/dev/uhid`), `ENC-18` (the `ENC-0` presence seam) and `ENC-19` (the firmware re-flash check) are P1 follow-ups, not part of the arc. **P0 remaining is 2, both in the phone arc** — `PHN-1` (PRs 3-7 of seven) and `PHN-2`; **`PHN-1c` is the claimable row.** ⚠ **`ENC-15`'s gate FAILED, so panel blanking is withdrawn permanently — do not reinstate it.** ⚠ **Roughly half of every encoder row's UAT could not be run** (there is no software path to inject encoder input); every row recorded that as an uncovered gap rather than a pass, which is why `ENC-17` exists. ⚠ **"0 warnings" has never been true of this tree, and the number depends on the command.** `dotnet build RadioConsole.sln -c Release --no-incremental` produces **53** `IDE0011` warnings across **15** files — which **confirms** the figure this banner has carried, measured 2026-09-03 at `5e571b88`. ⚠ **Without `--no-incremental` the same commit reports 30 across 13**, because MSBuild skips up-to-date projects and does not re-emit their analyzer warnings — so always quote the number with the command. All 53 are deliberately exempted from warnings-as-errors at `Directory.Build.props:6-7`; a warning of any other class still fails the build. **The honest local test gate is "adds no new failures", not "zero failures"**: four `SrcVariableResamplerTests` fail on every Windows dev machine because the native library is `libsamplerate.so.0`. See punch list `TEST-5` / `TEST-6`.

That single change also collapses the two `/sleep` states into one: reached by the 30-minute idle timer the server previously knew **nothing at all**, so every knob acted normally and invisibly overnight. ⚠ **The blanking half did NOT ship and must not be reinstated** — `ENC-15`'s gate failed on the box. ⚠ **The plan's `_currentValuePublishers` literal was wrong and would have shipped the exact bug its own test exists to catch.** It spelled the **pre-`ENC-5`** order `[Volume, Tuning, Source, Viz]`; the merged tables are `[Volume, Source, Viz, Tuning]`, so following it verbatim would have put a TUNING readout on the SOURCE band. Worse, **the plan's four-array guard would not have caught it** — it asserted only `card.EncoderIndex`, which every publisher threads through from its own parameter, so it reads correctly no matter which publisher ran. Pre-merge review caught that; the test now pins the **label** per index and was falsified against the plan's ordering (3 of 4 fail) before being reverted. ⚠ **Two more plan/tree mismatches worth carrying forward:** `SystemControllerTests` is an `IClassFixture<CustomWebApplicationFactory<Program>>` HTTP class, not the unit class the plan assumed — its `CreateController`/`CreateSleepService` helpers do not exist — and **"0 warnings" has never been true of this tree**: `main` builds with **53** pre-existing `IDE0011` warnings across 15 files. The warning set was captured before and after and is byte-identical, so the honest gate is "adds no warnings", not "zero". ⚠ **Recorded as follow-up, not fixed:** `IsSleepScreenVisible` lives in memory on `radio-api`, so an API restart while the kiosk sits on `/sleep` silently reinstates this row's own headline defect until something re-renders — and **every deploy restarts `radio-api`**. `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` §7 carries it with the mechanism that closes it; `SetSleepScreenVisible` was made edge-triggered on this row specifically so that heartbeat cannot wipe an in-flight wake claim.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Builder):** **`ENC-12` shipped ✅ in [#535](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/535). `ENC-11`'s safety response finally has a voice:** an amber/red fault badge on the Settings nav pill that is correct on every route, and one Radzen notification per browser session on escalation only. A healthy boot stays completely silent. ⚠ **The defect worth not rediscovering: the badge seeded only from change-only SignalR broadcasts, and that made it dead exactly when it mattered.** On a deploy `radio-api` and `radio-web` restart together, so the API's boot config push broadcasts the tier while the web hub is still in its connect-retry loop — the broadcast lands on nobody, the cache stays null, and the badge never appears for the life of the process. Pre-merge review caught it; the fix pulls `GET /api/integrations/encoder/provisioning` (`ENC-8`'s endpoint, no new one) and treats broadcasts as the update channel, not the source. **Verified live: the tier changed before the browser existed and the badge + toast still appeared.** ⚠ **`AudioStateStore` had never been constructed in its life** — zero consumers in `src/Radio.Web`, so its hub cache had never once run. Anything else that plans to "read the cached state" from it needs a consumer first. ⚠ **How to induce the fault tiers on hardware, so nobody re-derives it:** set `RotaryEncoderConfigDefaults` TUNING `max: 99` → `max: 0`; the firmware validates `min >= max` and rejects the **entire** config atomically, so the device keeps its flashed (correct) config and the only mismatch is that one non-safety field → **`Degraded`**, with the knobs still behaving correctly. Then `PUT /api/integrations/encoder/reverse/{i}` → the rejected push also disagrees on a safety field → **`HardFault`**, live, with no redeploy and no lost browser circuit. `sudo sh -c 'echo 3-2.3:1.0 > /sys/bus/usb/drivers/usbhid/unbind'` (and `bind`) gives a genuine mid-session disconnect. ⚠ **A `Degraded` console has its volume clamped exactly as hard as a hard-faulted one** — `VolumeClampFor` returns the tight 2-unit clamp for every tier except `Configured`, while handoff §7.6's table shows it only on the Hard-fault row. The toast copy is spec-verbatim and was **not** changed; the spec defect is logged for Planner in `design/FUTURE-WORK.md`.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Builder):** **`PHN-1b` shipped ✅ as [#534](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/534). ADR-029 PR 2 is in: `GvMediaClient`, the bounded LRU cache, the API-side `X-RotaryPhone-Auth` handler, and Radio.API's `GvMedia` config block.** Still no endpoint and nothing that plays a sound — PR 3 is the first PR a user can reach. ⚠ **Pre-merge review found 3 HIGH and 5 MEDIUM, and the two worst were in the plan's own literal code.** (1) `File.WriteAllBytesAsync` wrote straight to the final cache path, so a cancelled fetch left a **0-byte file that `TryGetPath` served forever** — and because every hit touches `LastWriteTimeUtc`, the poisoned entry became most-recently-used and was the **last** thing eviction removed. Self-preserving, and PR 3 will pass `HttpContext.RequestAborted`, so a kiosk reload was enough to trigger it. Now staged to `.tmp` + `File.Move(overwrite: true)` under the write lock. (2) The client's `try` covered only `GetAsync`, so **body-phase failures escaped the taxonomy entirely** — a mid-body reset threw raw `IOException`, and `HttpClient.Timeout` elapsing during the body (the *common* case under `ResponseHeadersRead`) threw a bare `TaskCanceledException`; neither carried a `Reason`, bypassing C-18 for the likeliest failure and making the class summary's *"failures throw … carrying a reason"* false. ⚠ **C-14 was half wrong and the box settled it.** The plan says the deploy copies **one** per-machine overlay to both services, so the auth key is *"two keys in one file"*. Measured on `radio`: `api/appsettings.Production.json` is **1057 B, mtime 2026-03-05**, `web/`'s is **75 B, mtime 2026-07-31** — different files, long diverged, and the `test -f` seed guard means neither is re-seeded. So it is **two keys in two files**, setting it is **two** hand edits plus two restarts, and `GvMediaStartupCheck`'s *"both services load the same file"* justification was corrected before merge. What C-14 got right: `src/Radio.API/appsettings.json` **is** replaced wholesale every deploy (rsync excludes only the Production overlay — confirmed live: the deployed file's mtime was that day's deploy, the overlay's six months older), so the non-secret `GvMedia` defaults do reach the appliance. ⚠ **The boot check cannot detect the failure it was written for** — it fires only on an *empty* `AuthKey`; two differing non-empty keys, which is what a mismatch means and the only state producing the 401, warn about nothing. Now stated in the code and pinned by a test rather than left as prose. 🎁 **First container guard in the repo with eager validation** (`ValidateOnBuild` + `ValidateScopes`) — but note honestly that `AddHttpClient<T>` registers the typed client through a **factory**, so `ValidateOnBuild` cannot introspect its constructor; the real constructor exercise is the `GetRequiredService` call beside it. ⚠ **"ADR-029 §17" does not exist** — the ADR's last numbered section is **§15**. The prior entry below cites "§4.2/§17" for the truncation instruction; §4.2 is real, §17 is not, and `MaxSpeechChars` ships as **rejection** deliberately (`PHN-3` owns visible client-side truncation). 📝 **Filed `PHN-5`:** `PhoneContactLookupService` logs raw phone numbers on **four** lines — including `:62` at **Information**, with the contact's real name, into the file sink that persists since `LOG-11` — and the one line it calls masked still logs `contact.Name` in full. ADR-029 §5.1 cites that file as the masking *example*, which is how it propagates. Latent today (its only caller sits behind `PhoneIntegration:Enabled=false`) but one config flip from live. ⚠ **No UAT was run and none was owed** — this PR has no route, no UI and no sound. What *was* verified live: `radio-api` boots with the new registration and `GvMediaStartupCheck` emits its Warning. **Carried to PR 3:** the real gvbridge route's status codes and whether it sends `Content-Length` — the size bound takes a different branch either way, and only the live service settles it. **Still PR 6's:** `PHN-1a`'s three device-only checks.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Builder):** **`PHN-1a` shipped ✅ as [#528](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/528). The head of the seven-PR phone arc is in, and the ADR claim that scoped it was false.** ADR-029 §8.3 calls D4 *"a lift rather than an invention"* because `FilePlayerAudioSource` *"already implements seeking through `SoundFlowPlaybackService`"*. **Both halves are false**, re-verified against the tree: `SoundFlowPlaybackService` had **no seek method of any kind** across its complete 20-member public surface, and `FilePlayerAudioSource.SeekCoreAsync` assigns `_position = position` — the same field `Position` reads back — under an `IsSeekable => true`. So PR 1 **built** the primitive instead of lifting one; `GetPosition` had **zero callers repo-wide**, confirmed independently, so the fix carried no blast radius. `FilePlayerAudioSource` is **not** fixed — logged to `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` §14a with its own UAT requirement, and it is a candidate row of its own. ⚠ **Pre-merge review found 2 HIGH and 4 MEDIUM, and 7 of the 14 findings were defects in the PLAN, not the implementation.** Both HIGHs were in the plan's own literal code for `AudioFileEventSource`'s completion wait: a `CancellationTokenSource` disposed outside the lock that a waiter was about to read `.Token` from (`ObjectDisposedException` → `Error` state), and `SignalTransportChange()` firing *before* the base class assigns `State`, so the re-armed wait read the pre-transport state — which would have fired `EndOfContent` **while paused**, the exact defect the mechanism was written to prevent. A MEDIUM found the plan's `ValidateMediaId` deny-list defeated by a **scheme-bearing** id (`http:evil.example`): under RFC 3986 a relative reference carrying a scheme resolves as **absolute**, so `new Uri(base, id)` in PR 2 would have escaped the base — an SSRF hole inside the validator written to prevent one. All fixed; an allow-list backstop was added **after** the named checks so every pinned rejection reason is unchanged. **All four `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests` passed as written, so PR 4's premise is intact** — ducking is still binary and transition-only, and priority still changes no duck level. **Update those tests in PR 4, never delete them.** ⚠ **No browser UAT was run and none was owed** — PR 1 ships no user-visible surface. Three device-only checks (that `Seek` repositions a real MP3, that `Time` advances, that a paused TTS source no longer reports completion) are carried to PR 6; all three degrade benignly. **PRs 2-7 remain unplanned and need a Planner pass each.** Two obligations recorded on them: PR 2 must **mask on every line** (ADR §5.1's cited example masks on one and leaks on two) and must add the first build-and-resolve DI guard in this arc; PR 3 must mint its **own** `playbackId` (`IAudioSource.Id` and `AudioFileEventSource._playbackId` are not equal, while `TTSEventSource` uses `Id` directly) and should settle whether `IEventAudioSource.SeekAsync` widens to `Task<bool>` so a player-refused seek is visible at the seam. Also for Planner: `MaxSpeechChars` ships as a **rejection**, while ADR §4.2/§17 specify **truncation** — the plan governed, but PR 3 should make that call explicitly.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Planner):** **`ENC-5` and `ENC-7` queued 📋 as one planning pass producing two sequenced PRs.** The punch list is explicit that these are *"one component with two lists … build them together or back to back; building them apart is how they drift"*, so `ENC-5` builds the shared selector component and `ENC-7` consumes it unchanged. Plans: [`ENC-5-source-overlay.md`](../design/plans/ENC-5-source-overlay.md) (18 tasks) and [`ENC-7-presets-knob.md`](../design/plans/ENC-7-presets-knob.md) (11 tasks). **`ENC-4`'s implementation landed on `main` at `29acc01` while these plans were being written**, so `ENC-5` is claimable as soon as that row is marked ✅; `ENC-7` is hard-blocked on `ENC-5`. `ENC-5` edits four `ENC-4` files, so **Builder should re-read them as merged before Task 1** — where the plan and the shipped code differ, the code wins and the PR should say where. ⭐ **The router remap belongs to these two rows and is sequenced so the tree is never in a state where a knob points at a handler that is not there:** `ENC-5` takes indices 1 and 3 and parks the visualiser on index 2 as a seat-warmer (leaving the old source cycler there would give two adjacent knobs two divergent copies of the source selection); `ENC-7` takes index 2. End state `0 = VOLUME · 1 = SOURCE · 2 = PRESETS · 3 = TUNING`, closing the deliberate mismatch recorded in `HANDOFF-NEXT-SESSION.md`. ⚠ **`ENC-4`'s mapping test goes red in `ENC-5`, and `ENC-5`'s goes red in `ENC-7` — both by design.** Each plan says which assertion moves and forbids chasing the red by reverting the remap. **Six findings from planning that Builder must not rediscover.** (1) ⚠ **There are no 7 slots in the preset bank** — the cap is **50**, `RadioPreset` has no `SlotNumber`, the ordinal is re-derived per request, and there is no next-free-slot logic because gaps cannot exist. The design survives intact; `ENC-7` §0.3 has the mapping. (2) **No tuner reports which bands it supports** — `IRadioControl` has no capability member and `GetAvailableBands()` is device-agnostic, so "composition resolves once per tuner" is unimplementable as written; `ENC-5` adds `SupportedBands` with a default interface implementation so the existing test double keeps compiling. (3) **Per-band last-tuned frequency does not exist** — `RadioPreferences` holds one flat `LastFrequency`, and today a band switch clamps the *previous* band's frequency into the new band, landing FM→AM at 1710 kHz every time. It gets **its own configuration section**, because `RadioPreferences` and `RadioOptions` both declare `SectionName = "Radio"` and that section already has two writers with different field sets. (4) **Recall cannot use `POST /api/radio/presets/{id}/load`** — it 400s when radio is not the active source, which is exactly the case the PRESETS knob exists to serve. (5) **`RadioControlPanel` already subscribes to `RadioStateChanged`**, so the "knob and band pills are one state" requirement is met today and the task is a regression guard, not a rebuild. (6) ⚠ **`ENC-9a` did not remove `VisualizerPanel`'s local `_currentMode`** — the punch list cites it as the template for exactly that and it added the missing *subscription* instead; the real template is "one authoritative owner + typed broadcast + a component that re-syncs without echoing". ⭐ **`ENC-7` carries a declared, deliberate deviation forward:** the on-screen bank becomes `PRESETS · n saved`, recorded in Rev 3's header, Designer §4.4 and punch-list §6 so a later consistency pass does not revert it — **Builder puts it in the PR body; Polisher does not flag it.** **One addition to the spec, surfaced rather than silently resolved:** holding PRESETS on a station already saved currently throws a duplicate error the handoff never covers, so `ENC-7` adds `ALREADY SAVED · slot NN`.
> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Planner):** **`ENC-4` queued 📋** with a plan at [`design/plans/ENC-4-encoder-hud.md`](../design/plans/ENC-4-encoder-hud.md). **Both dependencies are met** (`ENC-1` and `ENC-3` shipped 2026-09-01/02), so it is claimable now and is the next row in the punch list's §2 order. **Three things came out of planning that Builder must not rediscover.** (1) **The existing `VolumeChanged` broadcast cannot carry this.** Its only call site is a 500 ms change-detecting poller (`AudioStateUpdateService.CheckVolumeAsync:453-476`) — 2 Hz against a 100 ms requirement — and it says *what the volume now is*, not *which knob moved*, which is the entire trick. The row adds a dedicated push channel with its own ≥50 ms coalescer; that does **not** contradict the "do not add a second throttle" note in `HANDOFF-NEXT-SESSION.md`, which is about `VolumeChanged` and stays true. (2) **The Sleep-variant HUD is observable today, but only on the idle path.** `idle-dimmer.js:73-81` navigates to `/sleep` *without* calling `SetSleepAsync(true)`, so `IsSleeping` is false there and a knob turn acts and renders in place; reached via the Sleep pill it is true, the input is consumed by the wake, and the browser leaves the route — so UAT via the pill would produce a false failure. (3) **The handoff's “reuse `RadioControlPanel.LongPressThresholdMs`” is not literally possible** — it is a private const in a Razor component in `Radio.Web`, while the synthesis has to run in `Radio.API`. The plan promotes the value to `Radio.Core` and repoints the component at it. ⛔ **The row does NOT remap `RotaryEncoderActionRouter`'s index→handler table** — that stays with `ENC-5`/`ENC-7`, and the plan adds a test pinning the current mapping so a later change is deliberate.

> **Prior entry — 2026-08-18 (Builder):** **`KIOSK-2` shipped ✅ as [#480](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/480). The kiosk tranche is clear.** Verified on the live box across four `setup-kiosk.sh` runs, reading the installed copies under `/usr/local/bin` rather than the checkout: the healthy path is **silent** (`TIER=silent`, no dialog), a stopped `radio-web` was repaired by one tap with the kiosk up at **4 established connections** and the dialog reading *"The console wasn't running — it's started now."*, a hard failure **held the kiosk back** (dialog up, kiosk `inactive`, **0 processes, 0 connections**) with `Try again` producing a genuine second run and `Open anyway` then launching it, and the shutdown confirm sat untouched for **90 seconds** — GNOME's own powers off at 60 — leaving `uptime -s` unchanged. **The Google Voice bridge stayed at 13 processes with CDP `:9224` answering at every step**, including across `Exit to Desktop`, which took the kiosk 10 → 0. **⚠ Two things this row found that the plan had wrong, both worth carrying forward.** (1) **The accidental-shutdown mitigation was not actually being enforced.** Clearing the persisted DING icon positions works — and DING rewrites them moments later, so the one-shot unset races a writer that always wins. `keep-arranged true` is the setting that makes it stick, and it is now set alongside `arrangeorder 'NAME'` (which was **already** `'NAME'` and had never applied, because `keep-arranged` was `false`). **The live layout before this row genuinely had `Shutdown System` and `Exit Browser` adjacent** with `Radio Console` at the far end — the exact arrangement handoff §2.1 exists to prevent, so this was not a hypothetical risk. The durable framing, since the sort *direction* proved unstable across installs: `Radio Console` is the **middle of three**, and the middle of a three-element sort is the same element whichever way the sort runs — so any future rename must keep it sorting between the other two, not merely preserve `E… < R… < S…`. (2) **`--icon-name` being dropped from zenity 4 cost nothing.** `PROBES.md` framed it as a forced choice between the right glyph and two live buttons; zenity 4 accepts **`--extra-button`** on the stock types, so `--error --ok-label="Try again" --extra-button="Open anyway"` renders the red glyph **and** both buttons in the mockup's own order. **Handoff §6.1 shipped exactly as designed** — no glyph traded, no button dropped, no design decision needed. (`--icon` still parses and is **silently ignored**: the image node is byte-identical with a valid name, a bogus one, and none at all.) **⚠ One of the two "owner's eyes" checks was retired by finding a better instrument, and it generalises well beyond this row: AT-SPI works on this box.** `python3-gi` + `Atspi` expose every dialog's widget tree, **rendered label text, screen extents in pixels, and a working `click` action** — on Wayland, with no screenshot tool. That is how the 56 px buttons became a measurement (**58 px as shipped**; GTK 4's default is 44 px, the plan's `min-height` alone overshoots to 76 px, and `GTK_THEME=Yaru-dark` adds the last 2 px) and how `Try again` / `Open anyway` / `Cancel` were actually pressed. `CLAUDE.md` currently records this box as having **no** remote UI driving at all; that is now too strong. **Only icon legibility at the panel still needs the owner.** **⚠ The 24 px inter-button gap is NOT shipped and could not be** — GTK 4's message-dialog action area keeps a 1 px hairline under three different selectors. Its intent is met by a wider margin than the spec asked for (~444 px buttons, aim point ~222 px from the seam against ~100 px in the spec's model), and it is flagged for the owner rather than quietly worked around. **⚠ `T15` could not be observed live and is not claimed as if it were:** across 17 samples spanning two full refresh cycles the maximum `psidtsAgeSeconds` was **477**, i.e. the bridge is currently refreshing on a **~10-minute** cadence and never entered the 660–1200 trough at all. The trough is verified by **direct threshold testing** instead — 17 synthetic bodies including the exact body captured during a confirmed outage (`psidtsAgeSeconds: 707`, all three liar fields healthy) classifying as `online`/silent. **Do not tighten the `>1200` test to match today's ~500 reset**; a shorter cadence makes `>1200` more conservative, and matching the observation would misreport a dead session the moment the 20-minute cadence returns. **Five pre-merge findings were fixed and two of them made a tool lie:** `--print-status` reported `TIER=silent` while a service was down (`compute_tier` had no `down` arm) while `--dry-run` said `WOULD-START` about the same run; the RotaryPhone-owned ensure script was the one **unbounded** call, so a hang would have wedged the launcher with a `--no-cancel` dialog up and the `flock` held, killing the icon until a reboot (now `timeout 20`); and `· started just now` was being claimed for units that were already running but slower than the 2 s probe. **⚠ Also closed, and it explains a latent trap: `.gitignore`'s `[Bb]in/` was swallowing `deploy/debian-x64/kiosk/bin/`** — `KIOSK-1`'s scripts are in the repo only because they were force-added, and the next helper would have shipped to the box but never to the repo. **Merging alone changes nothing — `setup-kiosk.sh` must be re-run on the box; that has already been done on `radio`.** **Gates:** build 0 errors, `bash -n` clean on all five scripts, and 5 test failures that **reproduce identically on `main`** (4 × `SrcVariableResamplerTests`, a native `libsamplerate` P/Invoke absent on Windows; 1 × a real external NWS API call) — this branch changes no C# at all, which is the `TEST-1` false-signal problem in miniature.

> **Prior entry — 2026-08-18 (Builder):** **`KIOSK-1` shipped ✅ as [#479](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/479).** Verified on the live box, not just by gates: `radio-kiosk-exit` left the Google Voice bridge at **13 → 13 procs** with CDP `:9224` still alive; `radio-kiosk-launch` brought up the transient `radio-kiosk.service` with **2 established connections to `:5002`**; and kiosk CDP `:9223` went **`NOT_REACHABLE` → returns version JSON**, which is the claim `CLAUDE.md` now makes. A full `-Runtime linux-x64` deploy printed the new green line `Kiosk is live (14 established connections to :5002, radio-kiosk.service=active)`. **Two HIGH review findings were fixed before merge and both were GV-bridge safety:** the `Exit Browser` icon still ran `pkill -f chrome` (which kills the bridge), and `radio-console.desktop` carried a second, divergent kiosk command line pointing `--password-store=basic` at the **default** profile. **`KIOSK-2` is left 🔒 for Planner to reconcile** — its blocker (the dedicated `--user-data-dir`) is now merged, so it is ready to unblock. **One-time cutover, already applied to `radio`:** a kiosk started the old way on the default profile is deliberately *not* matched by `radio-kiosk-exit` and must be stopped once by hand — never with `pkill -f chrome`. **Prior entry — `KIOSK-1` claimed 🚧 on `fix/kiosk-launch-contract`, and moved above `TEST-1` with the owner's approval.** The promotion is not a re-ranking of the 2026-08-10 tranche: `KIOSK-1` fires on **every deploy** and leaves a dead kiosk screen each time, so it is a tax on every other row's verification — you cannot confirm anything at the panel while the panel is dark. `TEST-1` keeps its place at the head of that tranche. **`KIOSK-2` is left exactly where it was** and stays 🔒 until `KIOSK-1` merges — its scoped exit and single-instance guard both match on the dedicated profile path `KIOSK-1` introduces. **Plan Task A0 is now resolved rather than a gate:** the owner read all four unknowns off the live box on 2026-08-18 (zenity **4.0.1 / GTK 4** → the 56 px CSS belongs in `gtk-4.0/`; `--timeout` / `--default-cancel` / `--width` / `--height` supported but **`--icon-name` is NOT** — zenity 4 dropped it, so per-tier iconography in `KIOSK-2` must come from the built-in `--info` / `--warning` / `--error` glyphs; `systemctl --user show-environment` **does** carry `WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-0`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS` and `DISPLAY=:0`, which is the inheritance the entire relaunch fix rests on; rotary unit is **`rotary-phone.service`**). Recorded in [`docs/uat/2026-08-18-kiosk-launcher/PROBES.md`](uat/2026-08-18-kiosk-launcher/PROBES.md) so `KIOSK-2` does not re-derive them.
