> **Prior entry — 2026-08-18 (Planner):** **two rows out of one approved design handoff, and the split between them is the main judgement in this pass: `KIOSK-1` (the kiosk launch contract) and `KIOSK-2` (the three-icon repair-and-open desktop).** The handoff is [`HANDOFF-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md`](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md), now **`[APPROVED 2026-08-18]`** with all five §11 questions answered in its new §12 — **none of it is to be re-brainstormed.** **⚠ Why two rows and not one, since this arrived as one approved scope.** `KIOSK-1` is an **ops-correctness** row that stands entirely on its own: `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:376` relaunches the kiosk with `DISPLAY=:0` on a **Wayland** box — a defect its own comment at `:369-374` names and declines to fix — **so every deploy leaves the screen dead**, which the owner hit live today. Its fix is objectively verifiable end-to-end with no UI at all, and it is also the **single riskiest change in the body of work** (a brand-new Chrome profile), which is exactly why it should not be entangled with ~600 lines of new dialog shell. `KIOSK-2` is the **UX** row. Different failure mode, different verification, different fix shape — the same test this file already applies when it keeps `AUD-6`/`AUD-7` apart. **⚠ THE ORDERING BETWEEN THEM IS LOAD-BEARING AND IS NOT A PREFERENCE: claim `KIOSK-1` FIRST.** Every piece of `KIOSK-2` — the scoped exit, the single-instance guard, the launcher — depends on the dedicated `--user-data-dir` existing. Landing `KIOSK-2` first would force matching the kiosk on the **absence** of a flag, which the design explicitly rejects as fragile, and would leave `Exit to Desktop` still able to kill the Google Voice bridge. `KIOSK-2` is therefore **blocked** until `KIOSK-1` merges. **The verified replacement, so nobody re-derives it:** `systemd-run --user --collect google-chrome --kiosk … --ozone-platform=wayland …` inherits the graphical session's environment because the transient unit is started by that session's own service manager. **Confirmed by 2 established connections to `:5002` afterwards — and that count is the meaningful liveness check, not process existence: during the 2026-08-02 outage Chrome was running and `radio-web` returned 200 for ~33 hours while the count was 0.** **⚠ One finding is worth more than either row and should not be read as a side effect: nothing has ever installed the repo's `.desktop` files to `~/Desktop`.** The in-tree `radio-console.desktop` has carried `--password-store=basic` since Aug 11; the live copy still does not. **Three separate instances of that same drift turned up in one day** — the flags, the missing icon, and the 775 mode bit — and they are one root cause, not three bugs. Closing that loop is a first-class goal of `KIOSK-1`. **⚠ A fourth instance of the comment-accuracy pattern, recorded here rather than as its own row.** `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:146-154` explains, correctly today, why it clears `~/.cache/google-chrome/Default/Cache` and `~/.config/google-chrome/Singleton*`. A `--user-data-dir` profile keeps **both inside itself**, so `KIOSK-1` makes that comment describe paths the script no longer touches **and** silently clears the wrong cache while leaving the kiosk's own lock in place. Written onto the row as a required hunk. **⚠ Recorded so it is not "finished" by a well-meaning Builder: the in-app Blazor favicon (`App.razor:9`) is OUT OF SCOPE.** The owner initially approved it, then **reversed on evidence**: `--kiosk` hides the tab strip, address bar and title bar, so that favicon is **never visible on the appliance** — it would only ever show in a remote tab or a bookmark, which does not justify carrying the dark-surface and 16–32 px legibility risks inside a launcher cleanup. Deferred to its own pass (handoff §12, Q4). **The *desktop* icon is unaffected and fully in scope** — that is the one the owner actually taps and the one that fixes the generic-document-glyph complaint. **⚠ Cross-repo boundary, and it is narrow on purpose:** `gv-bridge-ensure.sh`, its watchdog timer and its nightly restart timer are **RotaryPhone-owned** and a Builder is bringing them under version control in `D:\prj\RotaryPhone` **right now**. `KIOSK-2` **invokes and probes only** — never owns, reimplements, edits, installs or `pkill`s bridge startup — and resolves the script from a candidate list at runtime because its canonical path may move under it. The new-consumer note for the boundary doc's Change Log is a commit in *that* repo, so per the 2026-08-10 precedent it is § Cross-repo handoffs **#8**, not a claimable row. **Also folded in and already decided:** `Exit Browser` → `Exit to Desktop` (Q1); a shutdown confirm that **never auto-proceeds**, replacing GNOME's 60-second countdown that powers the box off on inaction (Q2, overriding the earlier “leave as-is”); the **stateless `psidtsAgeSeconds > 1200`** dead-session test with **no Architect escalation** (Q3 — owner confirmed the field live at `310`); and the four reported components stand at four (Q5). **Placement: appended at the end of the Queue. `TEST-1` stays first — the owner set that — and no existing row moved.** But flagging rather than acting on it, per the standing rule that Planner appends and the owner ranks: **`KIOSK-1` fires on *every* deploy and costs a dead screen each time**, which is a stronger case for promotion than anything else currently below `TEST-1`. Say the word and it moves. **One task in the plan is a gate, not work: Task A0 probes the box and pins four unknowns** — zenity's GTK major version (which decides whether the 56 px CSS goes in `gtk-3.0/` or `gtk-4.0/`), `--timeout` / `--icon-name` / `--default-cancel` support, whether `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` is present in `systemctl --user show-environment` (which the whole relaunch fix rests on), and the `rotary-phone` unit name. **Several later choices branch on its output; it must run first and its answers get committed as `docs/uat/2026-08-18-kiosk-launcher/PROBES.md`.** _Nothing in the entry below is superseded — this pass adds rows and notes only._

> **Prior entry — 2026-08-11 (Planner):** **one defect from today's production deploy, queued as TWO rows rather than one: `AUD-6` (output device identity is an enumeration ordinal) and `AUD-7` (the reported active output diverges from where audio physically goes). The diagnosis is complete and neither row is to be re-brainstormed.** **The measurement, taken on the box across today's deploy restart:** on **Aug 10 16:17** MiniAudio enumerated `[0] AB13X USB Audio Analog Stereo` / `[1] Built-in Audio Analog Stereo (IsDefault=True)`; on **Aug 11 15:04** it enumerated `[0] Built-in Audio Analog Stereo (IsDefault=True)` / `[1] AB13X USB Audio Analog Stereo`. The persisted value in the config store is the string **`playback-1`** on both days. **On Aug 10 that meant the Soundbar; on Aug 11 it means USB Audio Out. Nothing in the store changed — the meaning did.** **Why two rows and not one, since this arrived as one report — the split is deliberate and is the main judgement in this pass.** `AUD-6` is a **persisted-format defect**: `SoundFlowDeviceManager.cs:517` mints `Id = $"playback-{i}"` straight off the enumeration index and that string is what reaches storage, while resolution back to hardware is **pure string parsing, not a lookup** (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.GetDeviceIndexById:1030-1043` is an `int.TryParse` and nothing else). Its fix is a stable key **plus a migration**, because every existing store holds ordinals. `AUD-7` is a **missing-call defect** with an entirely separate mechanism, and investigating it sharpened the owner's diagnosis in a way that matters: it is **not** that the two concepts "are tracked separately and not reconciled at startup" — it is that **the native device switch is reachable only from the interactive HTTP path.** `SoundFlowAudioEngine.SwitchPlaybackDevice:838` — the only code anywhere that stops, disposes and re-initializes `_playbackDevice` — has **exactly one caller**, `DevicesController.SetOutputDevice:250-254`. The startup path never calls it, and `SoundFlowDeviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync:213-244`, which the gate's own comment nominates as the thing that performs the swap, **does not touch the native device at all** — it validates the id against a cache, assigns a string field, and persists. **Consequence, and it is the reason `AUD-7` is not merely the second half of `AUD-6`: `AUD-7` is DETERMINISTIC, not intermittent.** It fires on **every** restart where the persisted preference is not the system default, with or without any reordering. Only `AUD-6` needs the enumeration to shuffle. Different mechanism, different trigger, different fix shape, different verification — which is the same test this file already applies when it keeps `GV-6`/`GV-8` apart. **⚠ THE ORDERING BETWEEN THEM IS LOAD-BEARING AND IS NOT A PREFERENCE: claim `AUD-6` FIRST.** `AUD-7`'s fix makes startup **act** on the persisted preference. Acting on an unstable key is strictly worse than ignoring it — shipping `AUD-7` alone against today's store would convert a **mis-report** into a **mis-route**, sending audio to USB because `playback-1` now resolves there. Full reasoning on the rows and in § Dependency / ordering notes. **⚠ RECORDED SO IT IS NOT REOPENED: this exact fragility was raised as a hypothesis during the 2026-08-10 debugging session and was CORRECTLY FALSIFIED as the cause of the soundbar silence.** The app had opened the right device; the real cause was the Cast startup mute, shipped in **#468**. **That falsification stands and must not be relitigated.** What is new is that the *fragility itself* has now been observed firing — a different claim from the one that was tested and rejected. **Thematically these are the siblings of `AUD-2` and `AUD-4`,** which turn on **source** key identity (`sdr-radio-<guid>` vs `Radio-<guid>`); this pair is **device** key identity. **Four rows now turn on an identifier that is not stable being used as if it were** — cross-referenced in both directions. **⚠ A FIFTH INSTANCE OF THE COMMENT-ACCURACY PATTERN, found while verifying these rows and recorded in § Dependency / ordering notes rather than as its own row.** `CLAUDE.md` § Pre-Merge Review currently enumerates three worked examples of a comment asserting something the code does not do; `AUD-7` supplies a fourth (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:222-224` assigns a responsibility to a method that does not discharge it) and its pinning test supplies a fifth (`StartAsync_PersistedLocalDevice_RoutesThroughGate:81-83` says *"not just the device-manager swap"*, naming a swap that does not exist). **Placement: `AUD-6` then `AUD-7`, immediately after `AUD-1` and above `AUD-5`. `TEST-1` is untouched at the top — the owner set that — and no existing row moved.** Rationale on the ordering note below. **⚠ Evidence-handling note, because the owner is about to invalidate half of it on purpose: the live `/api/devices/output` capture on these rows is a snapshot as of `0b2ebcb`, not permanent state.** The owner is about to run hands-on UAT and will re-select "Soundbar" by hand, which writes `playback-0` and makes the UI truthful again. **Do not read a later truthful `/api/devices/output` as evidence that `AUD-7` is fixed** — re-check after a restart, which is the only condition under which it fires. **All citations in both rows were re-verified against `main` @ `0b2ebcb`** after PRs #463 and #464 merged; neither touched any file either row cites. _Nothing in the entry below is superseded — this pass adds rows and notes only._
>
> **Prior entry — 2026-08-11 (Planner):** **one new row: `AUD-5`, a genuine pre-existing defect surfaced by PR #473's review — plus a staleness pass, because #471, #472 and #473 have all merged since the entry below was written.** **`AUD-5`: a Cast connection that is no longer current can persist its volume as the system master volume.** `GoogleCastOutput.SyncInitialVolumeAsync` is a *read* of device status, yet its success path fires `CastVolumeChanged`, whose **sole subscriber** (`AudioStateUpdateService.OnCastVolumeChanged`) unconditionally sets **and persists** `AudioManager.MasterVolume` — and **nothing re-checks `_connectionGeneration` between the status response and the event fire.** `IsInitialSync` exists on the event and is **never branched on**; its only two uses in the codebase are log-message interpolation, so an initial status read is treated identically to the user turning the knob on the Chromecast. **Two things the fix must respect, both settled during the assessment and both written onto the row so they are not re-litigated: (1) widening `_lifecycleLock` will NOT close this** — the exposure is the *event fire*, not the field read, and widening it would hold a lock across a SharpCaster network call, i.e. re-create the multi-second output-picker hang `AUD-3` was designed to avoid; the correct shape is a **generation re-check immediately before the fire**, plus a deliberate decision on whether to branch on `IsInitialSync` at all. **(2) It PREDATES #468** — all three links landed together in `b420edc` (2026-02-11), six months earlier, verified via `git log -S "IsInitialSync"` and `git merge-base --is-ancestor`; **do not "fix" it by unpicking the Cast synchronization work.** **Also recorded on the row: what is NOT wrong.** The three unsynchronized sibling reads (`SyncInitialVolumeAsync` / `SetCastVolumeAsync` / `SetCastMuteAsync`) were assessed and found **safe** — `_client` is never nulled after assignment, each dereference sits in a `try`/`catch`, and the client-identity race self-neutralizes because `ConnectAsync` disconnects any old client; **PR #473 wrote that reasoning into `GoogleCastOutput.cs:32-104`.** The bug is *downstream* of those reads. **Placement: `AUD-5` sits immediately after `AUD-1` in the correctness band — `TEST-1` is untouched at the top, and no existing row moved.** Rationale on the ordering note below. **⚠ Three corrections to the entry beneath this one, all now stale: (a) PR #471 has MERGED (`61187bb`)** — it is no longer 🚧 in flight, and its predicted anchor churn **did not materialize**: every anchor in `AUD-1`, `AUD-2` and `AUD-4` re-verifies **byte-exact** on `0870410` (`FilePlayerAudioSource.cs:1988`/`:2112` survived #471's edits to that very file, and #471 touched **none** of the files `AUD-2` or `AUD-4` cite). **(b) PR #473 (`0870410`) shifted every anchor below `:32` in `GoogleCastOutput.cs` by exactly +66** — so `AUD-3`'s ~30 historical citations in that file are pre-#473 and must be re-sited before reuse; `AUD-5`'s are post-#473 and verified. **(c) `AUD-3`'s closing observation that the class comment "asserts an invariant the code does not hold" is now FIXED** — that is precisely what #473 corrected, and the corrected comment goes on to document `AUD-5`'s exposure at `:95-104`. **⚠ Also recorded, and it may matter more than it looks: `docs/BUILDER_PROMPT.md` has NEVER existed, and the "Handoff Protocol at the top of `docs/BUILDER_QUEUE.md`" that the Builder role definition points at is not there either — so every Builder dispatched at this project is being pointed at two documents that do not exist.** Deliberately **not** made a queue row; Refresh pass, not a re-plan — and it surfaced one real gap plus one decision the owner should bless before Builder claims the row.** GV-5's plan was written 2026-07-30, before PR #461 merged; every ADR-028 decision **still holds and was re-sited, not revisited**. **(1) Line numbers moved in the three files GV-8 touched** and every anchor in Chunks 5/6/6b was re-verified against `main` — `PhonePage.razor` **+109** (all above `:686`), `PhoneTextsPanel.razor` **+51** (`+47` before `@code`), `PhoneMessagesPanel.razor` **+12**. Re-verified byte-exact and *unchanged*: `ApiModels.cs:1187-1188`, `PhoneHubService.cs:25`/`:94`, `MessageBubble.razor:38`, and **`GvBridgeSendService.cs` in full** — GV-8 never touched it, so Chunk 2's whole-file replacement stands as written. **(2) `GvResult<T>` is now adopted for the send path, and it needed a small extension first — new Chunk 0.** GV-8 built `GvResult` to be reused, but it models *"success carries a value, failure carries none,"* which is right for a read and wrong for send: **`HttpError` sets `Value = null`, and that is a documented, test-pinned invariant** (`GvResult.cs:58`; `GvResultTests` asserts `Assert.Null(result.Value)`). GV-5's mapping is driven off the **`Code` in the body** (nine codes, ADR-028 §2) and needs **two** fields off a non-2xx — `Code` and `Error` — where `GvResult` carries one. Chunk 0 adds a **separate `FailureBody` + `HttpErrorWithBody`**, deliberately leaving `Value` null so GV-8's invariant and **GV-6's branch idiom survive untouched**; widening `Value` was considered and **rejected on the record**. ~50 lines src + ~25 test, additive only, no existing factory/property/test changes. **⚠ (3) A trap for anyone adopting the idiom the obvious way, recorded loudly because it fails SILENTLY.** `GvBridgeApiService.ReadErrorCodeAsync:312` tries `{ "error", "Error", "code", "Code" }` **in that order, first match wins**. Correct for the read routes (`{"error":"upstream_error"}`) **and for GV-6** (`{"error":"markread_disabled"}`) — **wrong for `/sms/send`**, whose body carries **both with opposite meanings**: `code` is the machine taxonomy, `error` is human prose. Routing send through that helper returns the **prose**, all nine codes fall through `MapCode`'s `_ =>` arm, and the taxonomy collapses to one generic failure — the exact class of quiet bug GV-5 exists to remove. GV-5 therefore deserializes the whole typed `SendSmsResponse` on non-2xx and does **not** reuse the helper; a warning comment is added at the helper rather than reordering it (two shipped call sites depend on the current order). **🔵 (4) ONE OPEN DECISION — the composer during a failed load (GV-8 UAT `O-2`). Recommendation written, owner to bless.** `@ComposeBar()` (`PhoneTextsPanel.razor:101`) renders **unconditionally**, below whichever of GV-8's four branches won — so a pane that just said *"Couldn't load messages."* still offers a Message field and Send. GV-7 is forbidden from re-deciding composer behaviour, so **this is GV-5's to answer**. **Recommendation: disable with a stated reason** (`"Reply once this loads."`), **not hide, not leave live** — and it is **not a taste call**: `PhonePage.OnOptimisticAppend:856` does `_openThreadMessages ??= new()`, so **one send flips `Messages` non-null, GV-8's `Error && Messages == null` guard goes false, the pane falls through to the LIST branch, and the error state silently vanishes — replaced by a single outbound bubble that reads as the whole conversation.** Precedence for all four composer reasons is now specified once (most-permanent-first: un-repliable → failed load → degraded/dark → normal), extending the rule the Test Plan already had at §D2/31. **(5) `BumpThread`'s fix got a companion guard.** GV-8 added three sites keyed on `_openThreadId`; Chunk 5 Step 4 lets a row's `ThreadId` change under an open conversation, and all three would then no-op **silently**. **(6) Three pre-existing plan defects fixed** (none GV-8's doing): a test snippet referencing a `NeverCompletesHandler` class that **does not exist** (real helper is `BlockingHandler`), the plan's only prose-only step made literal, and a Test-Plan numbering collision. **(7) Duplicate-thread-row bug: STILL REPRODUCES** — `BumpThread` (`PhonePage.razor:828-849`) is **absent from GV-8's diff**, still matches on `ThreadId` alone with an unconditional insert-new `else`. Still **derived from source, not observed live** (needs `SendEnabled=true` on both sides). **Net: 8 chunks → 9, 8 tasks → 9.** Test Plan gained the live-verification preconditions this project keeps re-deriving (`192.168.86.50`, `-Runtime linux-x64`, the `psidtsAgeSeconds` blackout clock, and the binary-freshness grep) plus the note that **`auth_unavailable` is the one code observable for free** during a natural blackout.

new()`, `PhoneMessagesPanel` finally forwards the `Loading`/`Error`/`OnRetry` parameters that had existed unused on `PhoneTextsPanel` since GV-3, and the pane branches **skeleton → error → empty → list** so "Start the conversation below." is reachable only for a genuine zero-message result. **[Live UAT](uat/2026-07-31-gv8-error-state/REPORT.md): 10/10, 0 HIGH, 0 MEDIUM**, confirmed under a **real upstream 502** during a natural blackout rather than a synthesised failure — and C7 confirmed the *opposite* lie was not introduced: a genuinely empty group thread still renders **empty**. **One user-visible delta by design (Task 6): a failed open no longer marks the thread read**, and a *successful Retry* now performs the read-marking the failed open skipped. **Three durable operational facts were recorded out of this cycle, because two sessions had to rediscover the first two:** (1) **the box is `192.168.86.50`** — `radio`/`piradio` do not resolve from WSL (→ `CLAUDE.md` § Deployment); (2) **`psidtsAgeSeconds` from `:5004/api/gvbridge/status` is a live blackout clock** (`<660` healthy / `660–1200` blackout) **and the only honest field in that payload** — `available`/`degraded`/`cookiesValid` all read healthy through a confirmed outage (→ `design/INTEGRATIONS.md`, and § Dependency / ordering notes below, replacing the journald probe); (3) **`grep -ac <branch-only-symbol> /opt/radio-console/web/Radio.Web`** is the interim web-freshness gate until OPS-1 ships a real version endpoint (→ the OPS-1 row + `CLAUDE.md`). **Next up: GV-6** (adopts `GvResult<T>`), then **GV-7** (whose header/empty design now sits on a pane that *can* express failure). **Prior entry — 2026-07-31 (Planner):** **GV-8 now has a plan** ([`plans/.../gv-texts-load-error-state.md`](superpowers/plans/2026-07-31-gv-texts-load-error-state.md), 7 tasks, branch `fix/gv-texts-load-error-state`). Status stays **📋** — Builder claims it. Merging with GV-6 was reconsidered while planning and **rejected on the record** — the shared shape is ~60 lines and GV-6's remainder is behaviour (a once-only Warning plus a call-suppressing latch), not plumbing; `GvResult<T>` is introduced in the reusable form GV-6 adopts later. **Prior entry — 2026-07-31 (Planner):** **UAT + root-cause pass on live GV data: F-1 turned out to be THREE defects across two repos. GV-8/GV-9/GV-10 queued; GV-7's "design blind" warning retired.** First: **the upstream parser fix (`627b928`) landed and real GV data IS now flowing on both surfaces** — 20 SMS threads, 20 voicemails with real transcripts — which **supersedes the "read data blocked upstream" status recorded below** and is what made this UAT possible at all. What the pass then found: opening a text thread frequently renders **"Start the conversation below."** with no error, no spinner and no retry ([UAT F-1](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/REPORT.md), HIGH). A [root-cause pass](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/F-1-DIAGNOSIS.md) decomposed that one symptom into three independent defects. **The UAT's own guessed explanation — Google Voice throttling — is FALSIFIED**, three ways: the upstream status is `401 Unauthorized` and **never 429**; failure tracks **wall-clock in a clean 20-minute square wave**, not request volume (our constant-rate 60s poller shows the identical on/off pattern); and recovery lands on fixed boundaries rather than after a variable cooldown. **(1) Defect A — theirs.** GV PSIDTS goes stale ~11 min after each refresh but their CDP refresh fires only every ~20 min with **no reactive refresh on 401** → a deterministic **~9-minute auth blackout every 20 minutes** → HTTP 502 to us; **11 of 11** of our 502s fall inside a dead window. Their `/api/gvbridge/status` also reports `available:true, degraded:false` **during** a blackout, so our "Google Voice is reconnecting" banner never fires in the exact window it exists for. **(2) Defect B — theirs.** Thread ids containing `/` arrive as literal `%2F` (Kestrel deliberately does not decode `%2F` in paths) and fail an exact string compare → **HTTP 200 with `messages: []`**. **Reproduced deterministically in a healthy window.** Framing correction worth carrying: the predicate is **"the thread id contains `/`"**, *not* "the thread is MMS" — `g.Group Message.<base64url>` ids can contain `/`, and group threads simply happen to be the MMS threads. **Not fixable client-side**; both escaping workarounds were tested and both fail. **(3) Defect C — ours → GV-8 📋.** `GvBridgeApiService.GetSmsThreadMessagesAsync` collapses every non-2xx/timeout/deserialization error to `null`, then `PhonePage.razor:632` does `?? new()`, making a 502 **byte-identical** to a genuinely empty conversation. **GV-8 ships independently of A and B and is deliberately NOT blocked on them — ours makes the failure honest, theirs makes it rare; neither subsumes the other.** **(4) GV-7 is updated, not blocked.** Its "⚠ LIVE OBSERVATIONS INCOMING" warning is retired because the observations now exist; the row points at them, most importantly **G-1 — the "2" in `Texts 2` is an UNREAD COUNT, not a thread count (there are 20 threads)** — and **G-3 — zero opaque 36-char sender IDs are reachable from this surface** (no pagination), so that case is **untested against real data, not proven absent**. **G-4 de-risked the layout concern the row was written around:** a 36-char ID measured **safe at 1920×720**, no truncation needed. **(5) GV-9 📋 / GV-10 📋** carry the LOW findings (F-4, F-7; and F-5 as *confirm-or-close*, since its mechanism is **plausible but unproven**). **F-8 deliberately got no row** — the Tester retracted it as not reproducible with trusted input. **⚠ Durable discipline, also recorded in § Dependency / ordering notes: any future UAT of this surface must record wall-clock time against the 20-minute blackout cycle**, or the results look random again — that is precisely how this pass came to hypothesise throttling. Until Defect A is fixed, test within ~10 minutes of a `CDP cookie refresh` log line.

The ADR-028 banner now records the investigation **and** its outcome rather than being deleted — the reasoning is worth more than a clean-looking doc. Depends-on restored to **GV-3** alone. (2) **Reply-ability folded into GV-5** (ADR-028 §8): ~a third of inbound SMS is from **non-dialable** senders (numeric short codes, opaque 36-char sender IDs). Classified **client-side** (`GvCounterparty`, mirroring the existing `GvDirection` precedent — no DTO change, no cross-service dependency) and compose is **gated before the POST**, so an un-repliable thread shows a *disabled composer with a reason* instead of their `400 invalid_number` rendered as a red "That number doesn't look right" — the same lie `send_disabled` (§5.1) already taught us not to tell. (3) **GV-7 📋** owns the **display** side, with an explicit instruction to wait for the Tester's live observations rather than design blind.
>
> **Prior entry — 2026-07-31 (Planner):** **⚠ two corrections that change what this file has been asserting.** (1) **GV-5 is now 🔒 BLOCKED.** A Tester confirmed the deployed RotaryPhone tree is **`D:\prj\rp-deploy` @ `0a86898`**, not `D:\prj\RotaryPhone` — which is the tree **ADR-028 was derived from**. The contract must be re-derived before GV-5 is claimable; GV-5 exists *because* GV-3 was built against an anticipated contract, and claiming it against a contract from the wrong tree repeats that mistake one level up. Early signal: all four source files are **byte-identical** across both trees, so expect confirmation rather than rewrite — but source-tree parity is **not** deployed-binary parity, which is exactly what OPS-1 exists to settle. ADR-028 now carries a PROVISIONAL banner. (2) **The "GV Messages read experience ships fully" claim is RETRACTED** here and in `ROADMAP.md`. RotaryPhone's `PositionalGvThreadParser` expects an object root while `alt=protojson` returns arrays, so every real response yields **0 items behind a clean HTTP 200** — **voicemail and SMS read have never returned data against live Google Voice.** Our client is complete and correct; the data never arrives. Honest status: **read UI complete, read data blocked upstream.** Defect + a wrong configured GV number are recorded in § Cross-repo handoffs; both are `rp-deploy`-side and need **no Radio Console change**.
>
> **Prior entry — 2026-07-31 (Planner):** **three items from a live UAT pass on the Ubuntu box.** (1) **ADR-024 §3.3 amended** — the mark-read response table was missing the dark-state **`409 {"error":"markread_disabled"}`** RotaryPhone returns when their `EnableMarkRead=false`; verified live. Our client degrades acceptably but cannot tell "feature dark" from "GV unreachable," so **GV-6 📋** queues the small diagnostic fix. **Rollout order matters:** flip theirs first, confirm the `409` stops, then flip ours. (2) **OPS-1 📋 — build stamp.** Investigating found this is **~80% already built**: the SHA is stamped by `Directory.Build.props`, `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1` already passes `-p:SourceRevisionId` to both publishes and already **`exit 1`s on an API mismatch**, and `Radio.API` already serves `/api/health/version`. The real gap is narrow and is precisely the 2026-07-30 incident: **`Radio.Web` has no version endpoint** (its assembly already carries the SHA — it is just unreadable), and **deploy verifies `radio-api` only**, checking `systemctl is-active` for `radio-web` and nothing more. (3) Two **cross-repo** items routed to RotaryPhone via `docs/prompts/` — see § Cross-repo handoffs. Note the CDP spam has a **concrete, testable root cause**: their `ChromeCdpPort` defaults to **9224**, but nothing on that box listens there.
>
> **Prior entry — 2026-07-30 (Planner):** **GV-5 queued 📋: reconcile the GV SMS send contract** ([ADR-028](../design/decisions/2026-07-30-gv-sms-send-contract.md), [plan](superpowers/plans/2026-07-30-gv-messages-pr5-send-contract.md)). Reading RotaryPhone's as-built endpoint against ours found the divergence is **worse than the logged response-shape mismatch**: our `SendSmsRequest` omits their required **`ToNumber`**, which binds `null` server-side and returns `400 invalid_number` on **every** send — send is **non-functional today**, not merely mis-handled. Two more defects the fast-follow note missed: we have **never subscribed to `SmsSent`**, the only channel outbound messages arrive on (so GV-3's optimistic de-dupe is unreachable dead code), and the taxonomy is **nine** codes, not the eight logged (`invalid_text` was missing). **No external blocker** — their endpoint is shipped, and their `EnableSmsSend=false` returns `409 send_disabled`, which GV-5 handles as a first-class availability state. GV-5 merges as a **user-visible no-op** (`SendEnabled` stays `false`).
>
> **Prior entry — 2026-07-30 (Builder):** **GV Messages READ experience complete: GV-4 ✅ PR #441 merged.** Durable read-state (GV write-through, ADR-024) now ships behind `RotaryPhone:Gv:MarkReadEnabled` (**default off — zero user-visible change until flipped**). PR #441 had been parked since 2026-06-21 and was merged forward onto current `main` (44 commits: PR #456 feed-row polish + contact-name resolution, the phone dark-theme/scrollbar pass, PR #460 bell-failure surfacing). Reconciliation was clean — the row-polish rewrite re-projects its feed inline per render and reads `IsRead`/`HasUnread` live off the DTOs the reconciler mutates, so the unread annotation lands in the current markup unchanged. `Radio.Web.Tests` **846 passed / 0 failed**.
>
> **RotaryPhone is no longer owner-HELD.** They have since shipped `GvVoicemailController.MarkRead` (`POST /api/gvbridge/voicemail/{id}/read`), `GvSmsController.MarkThreadRead` (`POST /api/gvbridge/sms/threads/{threadId}/read`), `ReadStateChangedDto` + the unconditional `NotifyReadStateChanged` broadcast, **and** `POST /api/gvbridge/sms/send`. Routes verified to match our call sites exactly. **Both sides are now dark by config only** — theirs `GVBridge:EnableMarkRead=false` (+ `EnableSmsSend=false`, `AllowMarkUnread=false`), ours `RotaryPhone:Gv:MarkReadEnabled=false` (+ `SendEnabled=false`). Lighting up mark-read is now a **two-side config flip, no build wait**. SMS **send is unblocked on the endpoint** (was the last external blocker) — but send wiring remains out of scope here; see fast-follows for the response-shape mismatch to resolve first. NU1903 SQLite blocker cleared (ADR-023, PR #438).
